# RiftCombo — project rules

## Orientation
- **Everything in English** (user order, 2026-09-06): replies, issues, commit messages, walk documents, session prompts and reports. Earlier prompts said "contestale en español"; that is reversed.
- Read `docs/status.md` first — it is the measured state of the project, with the command behind every number and an index of the hand walks. `docs/plan.md` and `docs/phase0-findings.md` are the 2026-09-02 plan and spike, kept as history: read them for the reasoning, not for the current state, and mind their inline `[2026-09-06: …]` notes.
- `docs/design/research-2026-09-02.md` holds the design research (LOOPLINE's CSS, Riftbound's palette, graph-UI conventions). Read it before touching the visual layer.
- Repo: `github.com/GermanAbuArab/RiftCombo`, **public** since the de-clone (issues #1–#3) was done — `gh repo view --json visibility` says `PUBLIC`, so the `docs/phase0/...` blob links `sourceHref` builds do resolve for a reader. Account `GermanAbuArab` — run `gh auth switch --user GermanAbuArab` before any push or PR.

## Workflow — GitHub issues are the backlog
- Every task the user names becomes a GitHub issue. Create it immediately with `gh issue create`, don't batch them up.
- Labels: `de-clone`, `ui`, `bug`, `data`, `legal`, `infra`. Reuse them; only add a new label when none fits.
- An issue body states what is wrong, the measured evidence, the files involved, and its dependencies on other issues. No vague one-liners.
- Close issues from commit messages (`Closes #4`) so the backlog does not drift from the code. Qualifying it does NOT scope it: `Closes #23 for web/main.ts` still closed all of #23 on push, and its second half had to be reopened by hand. When a commit does part of an issue, write `Refs #23`.
- `tasks/todo.md` is scratch for the current session only. GitHub issues are the durable backlog.
- **Several sessions share ONE working tree AND ONE INDEX, so commits are made with `git commit --only <paths>`, never from the staging area.** On 2026-09-06 a walk session ran `git add data/combos.json docs/phase0/walks/<file>` — path-scoped, as it should — and its `git commit` still swept four files another session had STAGED but not committed (src/combos.ts, src/matcher.ts and their tests), because `git commit` takes everything in the shared index. `git add <paths>` does not prevent that; `git commit --only <paths> -F -` (or `git commit -o <paths>`) does, because it commits exactly those paths whatever the index holds. Each session owns named paths (a walk: `data/combos.json` + `docs/phase0/walks/`; UI: `web/`, `src/`, `test/`; synergies: `data/synergies.json` + its test) and commits only those with `--only`; a NEW file needs `git add -N <path>` (intent-to-add) before `git commit --only <path>` accepts it, because `--only` cannot take an untracked path (rc-qa5, 2026-09-06); never stash or reset someone else's changes; before pushing, `git fetch` and push only when `origin/master` is an ancestor of `HEAD`.

## Data
- Card text comes ONLY from Riot's gallery content API via `scripts/build-cards.mjs`; never from RiftScribe or other mirrors (they drop Equipment `effect` text and have no ban data).
- Errata is a dated overlay in `data/errata.json`; the build must fail if a find-string stops matching exactly once per printing. The one tolerated exception is a reprint that already carries the new text (Sona VEN-SP2, Void Burrower SFD-243), and it is named per printing in the errata entry's own `alreadyReprinted` field — never inferred from content, since a coincidental match of the replace-string must not be able to disguise a mistyped find-string (#136). The build still requires the replace-string to be present on a listed printing, failing by name otherwise, and fails any unlisted printing whose find-string is already gone. Riot's gallery API lags its errata pages by months, so after each Riot errata post, transcribe it into the overlay (audit of all four pages done 2026-09-03, #17).
- Legality is hand-transcribed from Riot's Rules Hub into `data/legality.src.json` (names) and resolved to codes at build time; it is format-scoped and entity-typed, never a boolean on the card.
- Match cards on base codes (`OGN-212`), never on names. Names are only for parsing pasted decklists.
- Card `type` is exactly one of `unit`, `spell`, `rune`, `gear`, `legend`, `battlefield`. **Equipment is not a type** — it is `gear` carrying the `Equipment` tag, so any filter on `type == "Equipment"` returns zero rows silently. Tags ride on every card in `cards.json` (`tags: string[]`) as well as in `data/corpus_flat.txt`, and a synergy predicate can stand on them alone — `Mega-Mech` is a Mech with no rules text. Two helpers (`UNL-T04 Buff`, `UNL-T08 XP Tracker`) carry an empty `type`.
- All 94 legends are named as bare epithets in the gallery (`Deceiver`), while Riot's own errata pages and players write `LeBlanc, Deceiver`. `resolveName` therefore retries on the text after the first comma, and `normalizeName` accepts both `- Starter` and `, Starter`. A third retry (2026-09-06, #86) handles Riot's own inconsistency: the same champion is printed with two heads — `OGS-004 Yi, Meditative` / `OGS-009 Yi, Honed` in the starter, `UNL-059 Master Yi, Unstoppable` / `UNL-113 Master Yi, Tempered` in Unleashed — and the "Top Decks" articles write the long head for all four, so `resolveName` retries with the last word of the head + the epithet, ONLY when the head is a tag the pool prints (eleven tags are multi-word; `Master Yi` is the only one whose last word heads a name). Verified 2026-09-04: no card name collides with another card's `X, Y` suffix, so the fallback can only rescue, never mislead.
- Base codes are NOT all `SET-NNN`: 22 are `SET-T0n` tokens, `SET-R0n` runes and `VEN-SP1`–`SP6`, and those last six are real playable cards (Kai'Sa, Sona, Ahri, Sett, Ezreal, Lux). A regex like `^[A-Z]{3}-\d{3}$` drops them silently; the shape that works is `[A-Z]*\d{1,3}[a-z*]?` and BOTH suffixes are load-bearing — 45 printings are alt-arts whose code ends in `*` and 102 end in a lower-case `a`; the two families coexist. Any normalisation must be CASE-INSENSITIVE end to end: until 2026-09-06 (#87) `resolveCode` upper-cased the input and then `baseOf` trimmed with a case-sensitive `/[a-z*]$/`, so `UNL-113a`, `UNL-113A` and `ogn-007a` all failed — 306 of the 3,567 spellings of the pool's own codes were being dropped, including Riot's gallery spelling; a test now sweeps all 1,189 codes in three spellings. riftbound.gg writes the alt-art suffix in upper case (`UNL-113A`). Its full dialect (#90, 2026-09-06, measured on 452 lists / 12,150 lines): `-STAR` is exactly the 45 alt-arts the gallery writes with `*` (one to one, normalised to `*`); `-P`, `-P2`, `-P-Champion` are PROMOS (promo=1 in dotgg's index: Nexus Night, Judge, Release Event, Summoner Skirmish) that Riot's gallery does not carry at all, so they resolve to the base code; `OGN-263-a` is the one id that hyphenates the variant letter. And **runes are printed only under Origins and reprinted in Vendetta — there is no `SFD-R0x` or `UNL-R0x` in the gallery**, so a Spiritforged or Unleashed rune line is an alias in `RUNE_ALIAS` (`src/cards.ts`), verified by name (R01 Fury, R02 Calm, R03 Mind, R04 Body, R05 Chaos, R06 Order in all three sets), not a suffix to normalise; without the Unleashed row a list lost its whole rune deck (696 of 775 rune lines). Before: 1,154 lines lost; after: 0. Audited 2026-09-04 (#14): every code regex in the repo was run against all 1189 codes, and the one failure was the TTS line guard in `src/deck.ts`, which used `[a-z]?` and so lost EVERY code on any dump line containing an alt-art.
- Anomalies in Riot's own text (bracket-less keywords, a dangling `[Effect] 1`, wrong tags, double periods) are REGISTERED in `docs/data-anomalies.md`, never normalised. They must never go into `data/errata.json`: that overlay's find-string failure is the only mechanism keeping our text honest, and spending it on a cosmetic typo would hide the next real errata behind it. What IS ours to fix is the HTML decoding in `stripHtml` — Riot ships `p`/`br`/`ul`/`li` and `&gt;`/`&quot;`, and two decoding gaps (undecoded `&quot;` on 12 cards, modal `<li>` modes glued into one clause on 15) were fixed on 2026-09-04.
- **Deck construction (#43, 2026-09-06) — three data facts a builder cannot derive from the code.** Riot's gallery carries NO Signature marker (0 hits in `cards_full.json`, no tag) — `checkBuild` therefore reports `pass | fail | unknown` per rule, never a boolean, for rules the card data cannot see. 103.2.d (max 3 Signature cards) and the Signature half of 103.2.a.2 stopped being two of those on 2026-09-06 (#103): two independent mirrors (Piltover Archive's `card.super`, dotgg's `supertype`) agree on the same 51 names, transcribed by name into `data/signature.src.json` and resolved to exactly one base code each in `scripts/build-cards.mjs`, which fails the build on a name resolving to zero or 2+ bases; `Card.signature: boolean` rides in `cards.json`. `checkBuild` now scores 103.2.d.1 (≤3 total, regardless of name — checked even with no legend, since it does not need one), 103.2.d.2 (each carries the legend's champion tag) and 103.2.d.3 on the Chosen Champion (a Signature card, even one whose tag matches, can never be Chosen Champion — Tibbers is the rule's own worked example). The champion tag IS derivable: a tag T is a champion tag when a card named `T, <epithet>` exists — all 127 legend printings yield exactly one, including the four with 2–3 tags (VEN-155 Yordle + Kennen → Kennen); pinned by a test over the whole pool, which also pins the 51 Signature base cards and their one champion tag each. And 103.2.b (3 copies) is a MAIN DECK rule: 103.3.b keeps the Rune Deck apart and 103.4.c covers battlefields, so counting runes against it declares EVERY legal deck illegal (six Mind Runes). The nine rules are quoted verbatim in `docs/phase0/walks/2026-09-06-deck-construction-rules.md`.
- Tokens, runes and the two typeless helpers stay in `data/corpus_flat.txt` on purpose: 79 corpus lines tell you to play a token without printing what it does, and rule 187 plus the token's own row is the lookup a rules walk needs (the R25 ruling turned on `UNL-T05 Gold`'s text). An empty `type` renders as `-`, not as a blank column.

## Combos
- `data/combos.json` is AUTHORED. `status: verified` requires a hand-walked loop against card text + Core Rules with sources; agent output enters as `candidate` until a human reviews it.
- Every combo carries a class: INFINITE (walked, unbounded), BURST (**one** scoring event reaches 8), CHAIN (reaches 8 across **several** scoring events, without the opponent taking a turn in between), ALT_WIN (a card that literally says you win), or ENGINE. Never label INFINITE without a "repeat" step. CHAIN was added 2026-09-04 (issue #20) for the three Time-Warp-chained and double-Conquer finishers that reach 8+ but not in one event: labelling them ENGINE would have been false, since they win. The class is a plain string in the UI (`c.class.replace("_", " ")`), but it is typed in `src/types.ts` and ranked in `src/combos.ts` CLASS_RANK plus the sort orders in `web/graph.ts` and `web/main.ts` — all five must be updated together.
- Do not accept structural verdicts from agents ("no X exists in the pool") without walking the validation set first; the first hunt's "no infinite combos" was wrong.
- Authoring traps the 2026-09-03 REFUTE passes caught repeatedly, check every new entry against them: entering an EMPTY enemy battlefield is not an attack (Assault and "When I attack" need the Attacker designation, which exists only inside a Combat with a garrison: 807.1.d, 383.4.e, 461); Energy added in Awaken or the Beginning Phase is lost when the Rune Pool empties at the Main Phase start (167) — but NOT an [Add] scheduled "at the start of your next Main Phase", because 316.2 orders the Main Phase tasks and 316.3's sweep runs BEFORE 316.4's start-of-Main-Phase effects (refined by #161, 2026-09-06; Blue Sentinel's rainbow is live Power); and **the pool has exactly THREE trigger multipliers** — a sweep of all 935 base codes for "trigger an additional time" returns `OGN-236 Karthus, Eternal` (Deathknell), `UNL-029 Red Brambleback` (conquer here) and `UNL-087 Blue Sentinel` (hold here), all three catalogued — so no BURST in that family can be scaled by a different doubler, only by copies (103.2.b caps at 3) or a denser payoff, and `VEN-138 Shen, Leader of the Kinkou Order` ("exactly one other unit you control here") is the one hold payoff the Sentinel cannot be stacked on, since the second Sentinel is a second other unit and 383.2.a.1 keeps his trigger off the Chain (#161); a Repeat gives no window to re-exhaust a resource permanent between executions (429.3, R21); Temporary tokens die before Scoring, so they never Hold and never see a Main Phase start (816.1.b) — but that trap does NOT stop a Temporary body being COUNTED by an ability that fires at the same instant, because 816.1 makes Temporary a Triggered Ability and 383.3.d lets the player controlling both abilities order them (settled 2026-09-04 for `gutter-palace`, which had it filed as an open reading); Gold tokens are printed "exhausted", but Renata Industrialist overrides that and they enter ready (R25 = A); recycling a rune for Power sends it to the Rune Deck (161.2.b), so Ekko readies only what is still on the board; a recall is not a move (456) and does not change state (458). A `would die` shield of the heal / exhaust / recall family ERASES the Deathknell of what it saves: 808.1.d.1 removes the triggered ability from the chain when the permanent never reaches the Trash (found 2026-09-06 on `soraka-fiora-loop-shield`, whose replacement has no `may`, so it also switches off the Deathknell of any friendly body under 4 Might that dies at the base). **A token never reaches the trash**: 186 / 186.1 — tokens exist only on the board or the chain and cease to exist the moment they enter any other Non-Board Zone — so a `Recycle 1 from your trash` (Vi, Destructive) can never pick a dead Recruit, Gold or Bird, and a loop that counts on recycling its tokens is wrong on its face; 416.6 makes the recycling player choose WHICH cards a `Recycle X from [Zone]` takes, and it does not target them (found 2026-09-06 walking #62's contradictions). And the shorter form of the same trap, rule 185 — **"Tokens are not cards."** — with 416.1 (Recycling is defined on CARDS only): a line that recycles a token, or feeds a `Recycle N` cost with tokens, is refuted on its face; two sources in #62 believed Mech tokens were recycle fuel. **A Recall comes from ANYWHERE** (455: "relocated from anywhere to its Base without it being a Move"), so a save-by-recall like Guardian Angel also switches off any `While I'm at a battlefield` text on what it saves.
- A BURST entry must reach 8 **with the quantities it declares**, and the class must match `terminatesIn`. Check the entry's own formula against its own `uses` before authoring or promoting: the 2026-09-04 audit of all eleven BURSTs found four that failed this. `ahri-blue-sentinel-hold`, `blue-sentinel-trinity-force-hold` and `ivern-sentinel-hold` each declared a board their own printed formula resolves to 7; `tryndamere-brambleback-conquer` declared one worth 4, which its own REFUTE note had already flagged in 2026-09-03 without the fix ever being applied; and `renata-time-warp-ekko-refresh` claimed BURST while its own `terminatesIn` described points spread over two turns (reclassed ENGINE). A REFUTE verdict in `notes` is not a fix — grep for corrections that were diagnosed and never applied.
- Recycle budget is the real currency of an INFINITE loop, not the 3-copy deck limit. Recycle (416.1) puts cards on the BOTTOM OF THE DECK, so a spell consumed every pass is fine as long as the entry can name the recycle slot AND the matching draw that bring it back. The verified `lux-infinite-energy` engine saturates its 4 draws per pass and leaves EXACTLY ONE spare Forge recycle slot (Forge recycles 3 of its 4; Ekko's Deathknell recycles itself). REFINED 2026-09-04 by the joint ledger on issue #21 (`docs/phase0/walks/2026-09-04-loop-budget-ledger.md`): the scarce resource per pass is **draws, not slots**. Both pass types leave spare slots and ZERO spare draws — the Energy pass leaves 1 slot, and `lux-infinite-power` is a second, different pass that leaves 2. And the draw is purchasable: Renata Mastermind's `1 Energy + 1 Mind: Draw 1` carries NO exhaust in its cost (unlike her Score ability beside it), so extra draws are unlimited and an extra cycled card costs 1 Energy + 1 Mind Power ≈ 10 Energy at the 9-Energy-per-Power rate. No payoff in the catalogue consumes a card per PASS; they consume one per point, and a point costs ~50 passes, so ~50 spare slots accumulate per point. The three Renata routes are alternatives because they all do the same job (ready Renata to score again), not because the engine cannot feed two. Do not re-derive any of this as a copy-limit bound. **Equipment that copies text COMPOSES, it does not add** (settled 2026-09-06 on #45): 434.1.c gives the Top-Most Card ALL the Effect Text of ALL its attached cards; 477.2.c appends that text in Layer 2, 479.1 finds no dependency between two copies that each alter the other, 480.3 falls back to timestamp order (attach order), and 476.1 applies each effect only once across all sequences — so v Svellsongur on one unit yield 2^v instances, not 1+v, and 476.1 is why R6 = A opens no recursion. `svellsongur-copy-hold` went from 11 to 17 points on the same five cards and its ceiling from 26 to 41. Companion fact from the same walk: an ability worded `When I conquer or hold` is BOTH a Conquer Effect and a Hold Effect (823.1.b, 823.1.c.1 spell it out for Hunt), so Reckoner's Arena doubles it twice — `ivern-arena-sentinel-hold` reaches 9 for 20 Energy + 2 Power. Always count the [Equip] costs and the Power: two published "18 Energy" figures had counted neither. **A legend is not at a location** (107.4.b: the Legend Zone "is not a location"), so Reckoner's Arena's "here" and Svellsongur's attach never reach a legend's own `When you conquer` text — the whole "legend + Arena" family is dead on arrival (#47, 2026-09-06). And 206 / 206.1 make cost-triggered abilities (Lady of Luminosity, Curator of the Sands — both are the worked examples) fire on the PRINTED or BASE cost, so discounts and free plays do not switch them off. REFINED by #47's walk (2026-09-06): that holds for CARDS (206 has no exception), but 206.1 for ABILITIES says "unless otherwise specified", and Empower specifies — 827.1.c.3: cost-altering text "is taken into account when determining a card's Empower cost for any reason" — so an Empower discounted to 0 does NOT fire a cost-threshold trigger; the "Empower discounted to 0 that still triggers on its base cost" family is dead. Two more facts from the same walk: Heimerdinger copies ACTIVATED abilities with exhaust in the cost, never a "you may exhaust me to…" (that is a Triggered Ability, 383.1, and triggers are not copied); a token with no printed destination goes to the base OR a battlefield you control (355.2.a) — no walk needed; and "Legend" in card text means the legend in the Legend Zone (355.9.a.4), which is how Heimerdinger reaches a legend's exhaust ability. **Tokens are born at the base, and points are scored at battlefields** (#48, 2026-09-06): of the 49 cards that create unit tokens, 18 say "at your base" outright, 13 say "here" (a battlefield), and 18 name no destination (355.2.a: base, or a battlefield you control). Exactly ONE card in the pool relocates tokens in bulk — `SFD-177 Azir, Sovereign`, "When I attack, you may move any number of your token units to this battlefield" — so the bottleneck of every token line is the walk to the battlefield, not the token count. And there is NO window to add a body at the instant of the Hold: 315.2.b.2 puts the Hold in the Scoring Step of the Beginning Phase, 312.2.a gives priority only in the Main Phase, and 383.2.a.1 measures the condition when the trigger is placed — the seven for the Plaza must survive the opponent's whole turn. Four more from the token walk (#48, 2026-09-06): **"here" is read at execution** (359.3.f.2), so an effect that plays tokens "here" twice on two landings leaves each batch where it fell — two Signpost/Accelerate landings do NOT stack on the Plaza, only the last one counts; **the Plaza count includes the body that conquered it** — 355.2.a needs you to control the Plaza before the tokens are born and 323.6 needs it still occupied, so Corina's line is 8 there, not 7, and `flurry-of-feathers-grand-plaza-win` already counted that way; **Gold born from a Hold is next turn's Power** — Awaken (315.1) precedes the Scoring Step, the Gold token's ability costs an exhaust (187.5), so a Hold-made Gold enters exhausted unless Renata Glasc, Industrialist is out (R25 = A); and **check 103.1.b.1 before folding a card into an existing entry** — #48 proposed Chemtech Cask (Mind) inside an Order/Chaos entry, impossible by Domain Identity. **Stun lens (#61, 2026-09-06):** ZERO cards exhaust an enemy unit (full inventory of the verb, 32 uses; Conscription steals first), and exhausting is not stunning — "exhaust" appears in no combat rule 461–466, 464.2.c.3 and 465.2.b do not filter by state, so an exhausted defender fights with its full Might; what makes a stunned unit PAY is not 423.1.b (which only removes its contribution to combat damage) but 423.1.c read with 710: it still needs damage equal to its full CURRENT Might to die, which is why Leona Zealot's −8 matters (corrected by #89's walk). 423.1.a.1 "A Stunned Unit can not be Stunned again" plus 423.1.a.2 (cleared only at cleanup 3d) means no stun engine can be INFINITE; no card pays points for a stun, so the lens yields no BURST/CHAIN/ALT_WIN; stun is Calm + Order (21 of 29 lines, none in Mind or Fury). A hidden 0-Energy stun caps at one per CONTROLLED battlefield per turn (811.1.b) and must target there (811.1.d.2). **The mechanic lenses are NOT all swept** — the 2026-09-06 tournament pass (#89) showed at least four more with real play behind them: DISCARD and recursion from the trash (35 `discard` + 47 `from your trash` lines; two of #89's biggest candidates live there, and 422.1.b makes "When I am discarded" execute AFTER the discard, i.e. from the trash), BUFF (49 lines; the 258-list Boss + Showstopper + Sett group comes from it and sits on 808.1.d.1), Might REDUCTION (Leona Zealot's −8 to stunned units is in no entry and corrects #61's coverage; Fox-Fire and Ahri's −Might), and "cards played this turn". **Ready lens (#56, 2026-09-06):** "enters ready" is not a ready — 143.4 makes units enter exhausted and 415.1 defines Readying as marking a Game Object already on the board, so the 48 "enters ready" cards can never give an exhaust back; only 16 cards in the pool ready something other than themselves. A legend IS on the board (107.4.c, "The Champion Legend here is a Game Object") even though it is not at a location (107.4.b), so readies reach it: five cards ready your legend (SFD-039, SFD-210, OGN-162 Miss Fortune, VEN-068 Jayce, Brilliant Inventor's "ready something besides me that's exhausted", and OGN-111 via Heimerdinger's copy) — #56's walk added Jayce after #56's hunt had counted four. Five cards ready gear (VEN-149, VEN-150, SFD-221, OGN-162, VEN-068) — **and NONE of the five can ready an EQUIPMENT, which this line did not say (corrected 2026-09-12, #187 batch 17).** Measured over `cards.json` folded by name+type: of 107 gear names, the **40 Equipment carry ZERO exhaust symbols anywhere in their text** while 40 of the other 67 do — the two halves of the gear pool are disjoint on the exhaust. With 359.2.d entering a non-unit gear READY at base, 143.4 exhausting units only, and zero cards in the pool exhausting a gear you control, **no Equipment in this pool is ever exhausted**, and 415.1 defines Readying on something that is. Three of the five name a GEAR (VEN-149, VEN-150, SFD-221) and two name *"something exhausted"* (OGN-162, VEN-068), and the split is proved from the card side by `SFD-221 Veiled Temple` itself, whose two clauses are *"you may ready a friendly gear. **If it`s an Equipment, you may detach it**"* — the ready half can only ever matter for the 40 that tap, and the detach half is the only half addressed to Equipment. Same measurement kills three other things: 718.2 and 721.2 are cited 110 and 66 times here and have exactly ONE Equipment target in the pool (`SFD-090 The Zero Drive`, the only one of the 40 with printed Rules Text beyond its own Equip clause, which is why it alone prints *"use only if unattached"*); 434.5`s worked example about an exhausted Equipment being attached is dead letter; and the standing rule that a repeatable activated gear ability must live on a gear that is never attached **is enforced by the POOL rather than by the designer — there was never a shuttle to build** — a `grep` that swallowed an adjective once published "the ONLY card that readies gear" inside a verified entry; when a note claims uniqueness, grep the whole pool with `-i` and the bare word before shipping it. **Movement lens (#58, 2026-09-06):** `[Ganking]` is NOT an extra move — 810.1.c.3: "It does not give additional abilities or activations of Movement, only new options for the Standard Move" (37 corpus lines carry it). 144.4 restricts destinations and 420.3.a puts the exhaust cost ONLY on the Standard Move, so a move BY EFFECT goes battlefield to battlefield without Ganking and moves EXHAUSTED units — that is the mechanical name of the token bottleneck from #48 (`UNL-045 Forgotten Signpost` does it for 0). And no entry of the catalogue moves an ENEMY unit: emptying the garrison wins the combat with no damage step (465.1 needs both Attacking and Defending units for the damage tasks; 466.5.d makes establishing Control a Conquer), while 323.9 marks no Combat when no opposing units are present, so the "empty battlefield is not an attack" trap is respected — it is a Conquer without an Attacker. REFINED by the #58 walk (2026-09-06), which found the rule the hunt had missed: **323.10** ("If Units of two opposing players are no longer present at a Battlefield that has a Combat Staged **before it has opened**, the Combat will cease being Staged") opens a THIRD route — evacuate a garrison after the Combat is staged and it un-stages itself, leaving only the Showdown, so 348.2.a / 348.2.a.1 give Control and a Conquer with no damage and no Attacker designation. **Only a Triggered Ability reaches that window**: while its trigger sits on the chain the state is Neutral Closed (310.2) and 323.13 (which needs a *Neutral Open* State) cannot open the Combat, whereas after a plain move the very next Cleanup stages the Combat (323.9) and opens it (323.13) with no gap — and 155 bars a spell without [Action]/[Reaction] from a Showdown. So an evacuation SPELL must be cast **before** the entering move, never after; `charm-evacuate-conquer` and `faefolk-challenger-forced-attacker` are two different lines for that reason, not two versions of one. Companion facts from the same walk: 323.6 strips a player's Control the moment their last body leaves (a *staged* Combat is not an *ongoing* one), so pulling the garrison off makes the battlefield uncontrolled — i.e. **open** — in the same Cleanup; 190.3.a / 190.3.a.1 / 450 key Contested on the moved **unit's controller**, not on who caused the move, so dragging an enemy body onto your battlefield makes THEM the Attacker on YOUR turn (464.2.c.1); and 464.2.e.1 puts the Defender's triggers on the Combat Chain last, which by 340.1 makes them resolve FIRST. Two paraphrases to distrust: `447.2` is only the definition of Destination (the invalid-destination list is 447.2.a / 447.2.b), and `190.4.c` ends "unless there is a Combat or Showdown ongoing there", a clause the #58 issue body dropped. `SFD-079 Bard, Mercurial` is the pool's only bulk mover of units of ANY kind (Azir Sovereign moves only tokens, and needs an enemy garrison to attack) and it needs the opposite board: an UNCONTROLLED battlefield, which 190.1 makes both of them at the start of a Duel. REFINED AGAIN 2026-09-06 by the #44 walk: **every deck has a free floor of 2 Power per turn**, so measure any new Power source against it, not against zero — 164.2.b (`Recycle this: [Reaction] — Add [C]`) carries no exhaust, so a rune already exhausted for Energy still recycles for Power; 161.2.b sends it to the Rune Deck, not the Main Deck; and 315.3.b / 430.4.a channel 2 back every Channel Phase before the Main Phase. The "12 recycles per game" ceiling #44 assumed does not exist. Seals produce per TURN and loops spend per PASS, so the ~9 Energy per Power rate for a loop stands. The extra cycled card costs 4 Energy instead of ~10 only when a Forge recycle slot is spare (Forge is `Recycle up to 4`), which only the Power pass guarantees: the Energy pass has one spare slot and the card itself eats it. **Exception, found 2026-09-06 on the Dragonstorm ledger (#46):** `SFD-117 Ancient Henge` — `exhaust: [Reaction] — Pay any amount of Energy to [Add] that much rainbow` — converts Energy to Power 1:1 with NO cap per activation, so a loop that re-plays a Henge every pass (Detonate kills it, Vi recycles it, Show of Strength draws it back; gear enters ready since 143.4 exhausts only units) makes Power stop being the scarce currency: its ledger is Energy-only, and the ~9 Energy per Power rate does not apply to it. The Henge is E2 P1, so the FIRST pass must be primed with the Power the conversion will later supply. **Burn Out, the three rules every draw line must respect (#59, 2026-09-06):** drawing past the deck burns out (431.1.a) and so does milling — `[Burn N]` or any "put cards from your Main Deck" past the deck (431.1.b) — but LOOKING or REVEALING past the deck never does (431.1.c: "does not Burn Out"), so reveal-and-draw is the only free family inside a loop that runs with the Main Deck empty. The "0 spare draws" of the Lux ledger is a WALL, not a slack to widen: with the deck empty, the 4 draws and the 4 cards that come back are balanced on purpose and a fifth draw is 431.1.a — **and it is NOT one point you may choose to pay, which is how this file read it until 2026-09-12 (#187/300-499 lane)**: inside a loop the trash is empty BY CONSTRUCTION, because 416.1 recycles to the bottom of the DECK and never to the trash, so 431.2.b has nothing to refill with and 431.3.a follows verbatim — *"Unless some effect intervenes, this will result in them burning out repeatedly, giving 1 point to an opponent each time, until an opponent passes the Victory Score and wins the game"* — with 431.3.b making every point after the first unpreventable (*"Points gained after the first Burn Out being processed in sequence cannot be replaced or prevented by any means"*). The fifth draw is not a price, it is the game — every extra draw source needs a recycled card riding with it. A draw with exhaust in its cost is useless inside a loop (every pass is the same turn) and no repeatable gear-reader exists, which kills 13 of the 102 uncatalogued draw cards including Garbage Grabber. **Discard/trash lens (#95, 2026-09-06):** a card's own trigger works from the trash — 385.1/385.2 (Triggered Abilities outside the Board self-describe their context; 385.2's worked example is the "return this from your trash" wording of Super Mega Death Rocket!) — and neither 385 nor 422 was cited by any entry before. A Burn Out does more than cost a point: 431.2.b RECYCLES YOUR WHOLE TRASH into the Main Deck, which erases the currency of every trash line (Rhasa, Dr. Mundo, Last Rites, Shadow Assassin) and kills mill-kill outright — 431.3.a says repeated burning wins, but the pool has two cards that mill the opponent (VEN-002 once a turn, VEN-114 once) and the first Burn Out refills their deck. Looking and revealing never burn, not even the zone change that follows (431.1.c.1), so "look at 3, keep 1, trash 2" (VEN-156) is the only Burn-Out-safe trash filler; the eight `[Burn N]` fall under 431.1.b and do burn. A unit played from the trash enters your base OR a battlefield you control (355.2.a), so reanimation lacks #48's walk-to-the-battlefield bottleneck. No INFINITE exists in the lens: the 31 discard outlets fall into seven exhaustive shapes and none is free and repeatable within a turn (827.1.c.1 and 820.1.c.3 close Empower and Repeat). VEN-022 Endless Riches is ANTI-synergic with its own lens: it banishes what would reach your trash, so it erases every Deathknell (808.1.d.1 condition), kills [Flow] and leaves 422.1 incomplete. The lens is Fury/Chaos (43 Chaos + 26 Fury of 85 slots), and VEN-143 Master of Shadows, a Fury/Chaos legend in zero entries, is itself a discard outlet. **Might lens (#97, 2026-09-06):** 22 cards in the pool are MIGHT GATES (kill / banish / bounce / steal / empty the garrison priced by a Might threshold) and only 5 are catalogued — that lens is the key to the other 17. Five measured facts: negative Might READS as 0 for spells, abilities and combat damage but IS NOT 0 (143.2.b / 143.2.b.1), so reverting it costs the real value; a `to a minimum of 1` floor is snapshotted when it applies and floors do not stack (477.3.b) — the nine Origins reductions carry a floor, the fourteen from SFD/UNL/VEN do not; increases apply first and decreases LAST (477.3.e.2.a), so a −Might always wins the race; doubling a negative Might adds +0 (477.3.c, worked example Last Stand + Eclipse); and `[Repeat]` gives EXACTLY one extra execution (820.1.b, 820.1.c.3), which may pick a different target (820.2.a) — Frigid Touch, Feral Strength, Bonds of Strength and Danger Zone are not uncapped engines. Nothing converts Might size into points except OGN-034's excess threshold of 5, so the lens yields ENGINE, never BURST. And buffing is CHOOSING (702.2.a) for THIRTY-SEVEN of the pool`s 39 buffers, which fire Blade Dancer and Irelia Fervent — **but NOT for the two anyone would call a MASS buffer, which is what this sentence used to say and it was FALSE (corrected 2026-09-12, #187 batch 29).** `OGN-223 Peak Guardian` (*"buff ALL OTHER FRIENDLY UNITS there"*) and `OGN-153 Overt Operation` (*"Then buff ALL FRIENDLY UNITS"*) select PROGRAMMATICALLY, and 355.10.d makes such an object not a target — its own worked example being the identical grammar, *"Kill all units at a battlefield targets a battlefield, but does not target any units"*. **383.4.b.3, cited by nothing, is the paragraph that closes it: *"Although these abilities say `choose` in their Condition, they trigger specifically when an appropriate Game Object is Targeted"*** — so 702.2.a`s *"a player chooses a Unit and then places a buff on it"* is the buff ACTION`s internal description and not a statement that the card targeted anybody, and 383.4.b.3 exists precisely to stop that word being read across. **This is the same axis this file already reasons with one lens over** — 355.10.d is why Stare Down moves a garrison of [Deflect] bodies for free while Public Execution pays — and the buff family had it backwards because the removal case reasons from 355.10.d and the buff case reasoned from 702.2.a`s verb. Two riders: **a mass buffer pays no Deflect either** (809.1.c charges *"for each time they choose"*), so Peak Guardian buffs a garrison of Bird tokens for nothing where a split spell pays a rainbow each; and **383.4.b.2 gives a timing nobody had** — a choose-trigger goes on the Chain after the targeting spell is FINALIZED, so with 340.1 resolving newest-first **the trigger resolves BEFORE the spell that targeted**, and Blade Dancer readies the unit before the spell choosing it does anything to it. **And the exception has a boundary this file leans on without having read**: 355.10.d is cited 151 times, 355.10.d.1 once and **355.10.d.2 never** — *"This exception does not apply to objects that are the only valid choice at the moment a spell or ability is placed on the chain, but which would require a choice under other circumstances"*, with Riot`s example that *"Kill a unit at a battlefield"* always targets even when only one unit is there. **A lone enemy unit is still a target and still pays Deflect**, so any future claim that something chooses nothing *because there was only one option* must clear 355.10.d.2 first. **"This turn" lens (#98, 2026-09-06):** the lens splits in two on one word — `costs N or more` reads the PRINTED cost (206; the worked example is Lux, Illuminated) while `if you spent N or more` reads what left the Rune Pool (166.2, 203.1, 356.4.b), so any discount switches the second family off and never touches the first; a card played from [Hidden] costs 0 (811.1.b, "ignoring its base cost"), so the Hidden package pays ZERO to Virtuoso, Revna, Jhin Meticulous, Sivir, Prepared Neophyte and Forgotten Library (both Virtuoso entries were audited and stand). And a token is PLAYED but is NOT a card (350.1, 350.2, 185.2.a): it fires `When you play a unit` and never `[Legion]` or any `played a card` — the catalogue uses the positive half correctly; the negative half is an `excludes` reason for every rule on "cards played". **Denial lens (#100, 2026-09-06):** the opponent NEVER holds priority in a Neutral Open State on your turn — 312.2.a gives it only in Closed States and Showdowns — so every denial card lives in those two windows; `cards` lets tokens through (052 / 185 / 350.2) while `units` catches them (185.2.a), which is exactly the gap between Brynhir and Mageseeker Warden; and no lock protects YOUR Hold, because every lock says `this turn` and is Main-Phase only, which comes AFTER the Beginning Phase (315.1 → 315.2 → 316) — the only thing that reaches a Hold is a [Reaction] in the Closed State its trigger opens (312.2.c/d + 813.1.c.1), and on a winning turn that mana is free since 319.5 + 194.2 win before 316.3 empties the pool. `OGN-070 Mageseeker Warden` is the biggest denial card in the pool and sits in ZERO entries: 054.2 + 355.2.a/b + 811.1.d.1 kill the 15 [Hidden] units outright, switch off the seven cards that grant 355.2.b, and forbid reinforcing an own battlefield — it touches four catalogued ALT_WINs — but it does NOT protect a Plaza: the prohibition is the Warden's own text ("opponents can only play units to their base") biting through 054.2, and 355.2.a forbids nothing — it makes a battlefield you CONTROL a default play location ("the controller's Base or a battlefield the controller controls"), which is why Peak Guardian's "buff all other friendly units here" is live whenever he is played to a battlefield you control ([Ambush], 822.1.b, grants a DIFFERENT location: one where you control units but not the battlefield). Corrected 2026-09-06 by the #111 walk after the hunt had called Peak Guardian dead letter. The lens is Calm (9 of 31) and yields ENGINE only: nothing in it scores. **Four rules the #89 walk added (2026-09-06):** 702.3 — "There can only be one Buff on a Unit at a time" (702.3.a: a second buff is not placed), which settles the whole buff family — Cithria stops at 2 Might, it does not grow — **with exactly ONE printed exception in the pool (swept 2026-09-07, #161): `OGN-078 Lee Sin, Ascetic` prints "I can have any number of buffs", and **426.1.b.2 is the RULES` OWN SLOT for him and is a cleaner source than the rule-002 argument this file has been using (corrected 2026-09-12): *"Some effects may grant a Game Object permission to be Buffed multiple times. Such an effect ignores this restriction."*** So the exception is licensed by the buff rules themselves rather than by card text beating rules text; `lee-sin-buff-bank` already cites it. rule 002 makes card text beat rules text, so he ignores the cap; the corroborating row is the reminder token itself, `UNL-T04 Buff` ("A unit may have no more than one buff at a time"). Same shape as 103.2.b's Spiderling exception: state the rule with the exception attached, or the next session refuses a Lee Sin line on a cap he does not obey;** 818.1 — Equip is an Activated Ability, so attaching is NEVER playing and no [Weaponmaster] re-equip fires a `When you play this`; 429.2 / 429.2.a — an ability that Adds resolves as soon as it is finalized, before any other outstanding chain item, which decides every Add timing inside a resolution; and 811.1.d.2's exception is PER TARGET, settled by name — its worked examples cite Tideturner ("its target may be chosen freely") and Smoke and Mirrors (811.1.d.2.a: the first unit CAN be chosen at the battlefield so it MUST be; the second from anywhere), so no R33 was needed for it. Piltover Archive's `api/external/v1/cards` paginates by `page`, not `offset` (offset always returns page one: 86 of 941 cards); riftbound.gg's deck slug is `standings[].slug`.
- **Tokens are not cards, and that refutes lines (#62, 2026-09-06).** **185** is one sentence — *"Tokens are not cards."* — and **416.1** defines Recycling over *"one or more **cards**"*, with **416.3** requiring a cost Recycle to be completable. So `recycle another friendly unit` can NEVER take a token, and neither can `Recycle N from your trash`: **186.1** makes a token cease to exist the instant it enters any Non-Board Zone, so none ever REMAINS in the trash to be chosen. Two independent community lines in #62 died on this (a Rumble deck recycling its own Mech tokens for the discount, and two players guessing at the rule without knowing it). **416.6** is the companion: *"take X cards of the instructed player's choice"* — the player chooses, so a trash does not have to be emptied first. Check every `recycle` line against 185 before believing it.
- **The combat cleanup is what makes the recall family repeat, and the defender bar is the SUM (#62, 2026-09-06).** No card bounces the attacker home: **466.1.a.1** (*"Insert '3c. Heal all Units.'"*) erases accumulated damage every combat, and **466.1.a.2** (*"Recall Attackers present at the Battlefield if Defenders are still present"*) sends them back; **466.3.d** is No Result and **466.3.d.1** does not re-stage, since only the defender has units left. **458** brings them home still exhausted. The bar the stunned defender has to clear is set by **465.2.c** — *"each player assigns an amount of damage equal to their **summed Might**"* — plus **465.2.c.3**, which forces lethal onto one unit first: one 3-Might Reveler needs a 4+ defender, a Reveler plus a Recruit needs 5+, a Reveler plus Jhin needs **8+**. A source that says "at least five might" while adding a second attacker is wrong by arithmetic, not by rules.
- **A Recall goes to the BASE, so a heal/exhaust/recall shield switches off any "While I'm at a battlefield" text.** **455**: *"A Recall is when a Permanent is relocated **from anywhere to its Base** without it being a Move."* Guardian Angel keeps Tianna Crownguard alive and turns her scoring lock off for a full turn (she also comes back exhausted, so 315.1.b readies her only next Awaken). This is a second, separate trap from 808.1.d.1 (the shield erasing a Deathknell) and it is easy to check the first and miss this one — #62 did.
- **There IS one window in the Beginning Phase before scoring, and it is a `[Temporary]` trigger (#62, 2026-09-06).** **335** is why there normally is not: outside the Main Phase with an empty chain you *"proceed to the next substep"* and nobody gets priority. But **816.1.b** fires *"At the start of this permanent's controller's Beginning Phase, **before scoring**"*, which puts a Chain Item up; **312.2.c** then hands out priority in the Closed State and **813.1.c.1** says a `[Reaction]` card *"can be played during Closed States on any player's turn"*. So bodies CAN be added before the Plaza's trigger is placed — which does not contradict 383.2.a.1, because the window sits earlier than the Scoring Step. And **816.1.c** makes the Trigger Condition *the start* of the Beginning Phase, so a `[Temporary]` permanent that enters inside that window never dies that turn. This is the one Plaza line that uses `[Temporary]` as an enabler instead of paying LeBlanc to switch it off.
- **Read a clause to its end: two of them carry an escape.** **811.1.d.2** confines a hidden card's targets to the battlefield it was hidden at *"unless the ability explicitly restricts targeting in a way that makes this impossible"* — so Tideturner's *"a unit you control at **another** location"* legally reaches the base. And **190.6.d**'s second sentence is what blanks an uncontrolled battlefield's *"you"*, which is why a battlefield you bring starts switched off. Two findings in this project have come from the tail of a sentence somebody quoted only the head of.
- **A lens verdict is scoped to its lens.** #59 declared `OGN-099 Garbage Grabber` dead because its cost carries an exhaust and every pass of a loop is the same turn. Used BETWEEN turns it is fine — 315.1.b readies it every Awaken — and it is now the recursion behind `aurora-elder-dragon-garbage-grabber`. Before treating a hunt's "this card is dead" as general, check whether the reason was the lens's own frame.
- **709 is an event, not a state**, and its worked example says so: *"A Unit with Might 5 that gets +1 does not become Mighty, because it was already Mighty."* So a `+Might` gear only triggers a "becomes Mighty" payoff while it is going onto a body **below** 5.
- **Three facts from the #85 walk (2026-09-06).** 356.4.e — *"If a discount applies a minimum cost, that minimum applies only to that discount"* — so copies of the SAME floored discount never chain below the floor, but the un-floored component does: three Applied Researchers (VEN-055) take Time Warp to 7 Energy + 1 Mind, while the Energy halves stop at 1 each. That discount never touches the loop ledger of #21 (Renata Mastermind's draw is an Activated Ability, 377.1, not a spell); it lands on every spell with a Power cost. No card in the pool prints a rainbow Power COST (`:rb_rune_rainbow:` appears only as a Deflect payment and an [Add] output), so a "one [A] less" discount removes a domain Power or is dead letter (rule 002, the R28 argument). A Deathknell on GEAR that fires because a kill was paid as a cost is cited with 808.1.a + 808.1.d.2 ("present on Permanents" / "the card with an ability that triggers on its own death"), not 428.1.a.1.b, which says "a unit". And 185.1.a — *"Token Game Objects cannot lose their token nature by any means"* — kills the "a Reflection stops being a token once it copies" line; 055 ignores only the copy instruction, and the token still receives its [Temporary]. VEN-149 Defender of Tomorrow is the ONLY Mind/Body legend of the 49, so any Mind/Body line is a forced shell — **and it is not alone in that: the legend census (#160 batch 9, 2026-09-07; 94 base codes, 49 names, 15 domain pairs, every name printed under exactly TWO base codes except the four Starters) has three single-name pairs — Body/Mind = Defender of Tomorrow (VEN-149 / VEN-194), Calm/Fury = Rogue Assassin (VEN-139 / VEN-189), Chaos/Order = Heart of the Tempest (VEN-155 / VEN-197). A `prerequisites.easy` line is a list of NAMES, never of base codes, and "the ONLY X/Y legend" is a name claim that a base-code count gets wrong twice over.**
- **Two facts from the #95 walk (2026-09-06).** 203.3 — if the action a cost asks for is impossible, the cost cannot be paid — is the general form of 414.4 / 416.3 / 422.3, and with 442.1.a it is the real gate on a banish-as-cost line (the issue had cited 827.1.c.1, which is the [Empower] keyword's shorthand and applies only to cards that carry the keyword). And 206 cuts both ways for damage: Get Excited! reads the PRINTED cost of the discarded card, so its in-domain ceiling is 10 (Rhasa, Baron Nashor at E10), not the 3 the issue measured. Three of #95's seven candidates were rewritten for issue errors a walk must always re-check: two cards that cannot share a deck (103.1.b.1 / 103.1.b.2), a mover whose Might cap excludes its own engine, and a Hold-window count that forgot 167 empties the Rune Pool before the Main Phase.
- **Three rules the #97 walk cited for the first time (2026-09-06).** 355.10.d — a Game Object *"programmatically selected based on its characteristics rather than chosen"* is NOT a target, so Stare Down moves a whole garrison of [Deflect] bodies for free (809.1.c / 809.1.d charge only for CHOOSING) while Public Execution pays; that contrast is what separates the two Renekton entries. 441.2 — "Empowered" is a STATE of a Game Object on the board, permanent once reached: the 6 Energy that take Renekton from 4 to 10 buy [Deflect] and [Ganking] for good, not for the turn. And 143.2.b makes a floorless Might reduction cost 0 in a sum such as Fox-Fire's while a floored one costs 1 (477.3.b caps the REDUCTION, not the result: "−3, minimum 1" sends M to max(1, M−3), so four M10 bodies sum 28, not 4). The tail of 465.2.c.4 ("unless…") is a PERMISSION the attacker may decline; R28 = A stands. Four of #97's five candidates were rewritten for issue errors: a "the ONLY removal that reaches the base" claim that a `grep` refutes (OGN-229, OGN-234, SFD-158 also lack "at a battlefield"), a payoff left out of its own combat (464.2.c.3 + 465.2.c sum its Might), and two buff counts off by one card.
- **206 carries BOTH halves of the "this turn" lens, and a [Repeat] cost is spent but never read (#98 walk, 2026-09-06).** 206's second worked example (Defy vs Rocket Barrage) says a `costs N` check IGNORES a paid [Repeat] cost, while 820.1.c.1 makes that same cost *"an Additional Cost to be paid during the steps of playing the spell"* — i.e. SPENT — so a [Repeat] is the cheapest way to reach a `spent N` threshold and is invisible to a `costs N` one (UNL-009 Upstage Comedy, E2 + [Repeat] E2 = 4 spent, is the cheapest ready in the pool). 811.1.c.1: *"Hide is not a subset of Play."* 383.1.b: a `the [Nth] time` trigger fires ONCE when its condition is met several times simultaneously — the controller picks one instance. And a "legal in domain X" count is not well formed: 103.1.b is a subset test against the legend's TWO domains, so the number is per legend pair (the Power ≥ 2 pool is 83 cards, 22–29 per pair), never per single domain. Brynhir's lockout window is its own entry, not a `uses[]` of the loop it protects: adding it would force the matcher to require it.
- **Four rules from the #100 walk (2026-09-06).** 144.4 — the Standard Move is ONLY base ↔ battlefield (battlefield → battlefield needs [Ganking]), so "must play to the base" is not a delay any card imposes: the base is where everything starts anyway, and Mageseeker Warden's real delta is three routes closed (the seven 355.2.b cards, the 15 [Hidden] units, reinforcing an own battlefield) with one named leak — the 26 [Accelerate] units (805.1.a "I enter ready" + 143.4.a). 423.1.a.2 + 423.1.c — a stun applied on the opponent's turn is cleared at THEIR end-of-turn cleanup (step 3d), so it is gone before your attack, and the stunned body still soaks its full Might; only "can't move it this turn" closes the Accelerate leak, which is why the Vex entry was renamed from a stun to a move lock. 469.2 — Hold is defined on a battlefield the player CONTROLS, so an opponent with no battlefield never Holds and every "until they hold" clause (Ashe's banish) is permanent against a walled board. 811.1.c.1 — the Warden and the Saboteur stop PLAYING what was hidden, never HIDING it. And 206's worked example IS Defy: a spell tax never widens your own counters' `costs no more than N` window.
- **Evacuating a garrison is not yet a Conquer (#102 walk, 2026-09-06).** 323.6 only STRIPS the opponent's Control when their last body leaves; nobody gains it. The point needs a body of yours walking in during the Main Phase (144.4.a) → Contested (450) → Showdown (323.8 / 323.12) → 348.2.a → 348.2.a.1, and it does score that same turn; 466.5 is a Combat Resolution step and is never the route when no Combat opens. `UNL-207 Amateur Recital` moves ONE unit, so it only closes against a garrison of one, and denial alone costs the opponent nothing — they Conquer on re-entry. Evidence that a bare "a unit" reaches enemies: `OGN-168 Fight or Flight` prints the same sentence and is banned in both formats. Two vocabulary facts from the same walk: a "buff" is the 702 counter and 702.3 caps it at one per unit, while "+N Might this turn" is a plain modifier that 702.3 never touches; and a rune channelled by a Hold enters EXHAUSTED after Awaken (315.1) has already run, so Startipped Peak gives a per-rune Empower zero turns (the discount and the rune are both worth 1 Energy) and the twelfth rune one turn earlier, not two — the Peak is not held before turn 3 (190.1 + 143.4).
- **A repeatable buffer on a loop yields min(triggers, new unbuffed bodies) (#106 walk, 2026-09-06).** 702.3 / 702.3.a read against a token engine: the bottleneck of `buff for each X` is the NEW BODIES, not the triggers — `lux-infinite-energy` makes exactly one Recruit per pass, so Karma, Channeler places one buff per pass whatever fires (two recycles reach the Main Deck per pass: Forge and Ekko's Deathknell; the rune of step 2 goes to the Rune Deck, 416.1.b + 161.2.b), and a second Karma adds nothing. The honest product is an unbounded army of 2-Might Recruits, never cumulative Might. 436.4.a — predicting with too few cards in the deck does NOT Burn Out, so [Vision] is safe inside the empty-deck shell. 817.1.c — "the trigger is the permanent entering the Board", so a unit whose own play trigger predicts is on the board before that predict resolves and fires its own "when you predict" text. And `[Hunt]` is printed as `[Hunt]`, `[Hunt 2]`, `[Hunt 3]`: a grep for `\[Hunt\]` finds 7 of the 13 cards — open the lens with `\[Hunt`.
- **Two blocks the #107 walk cited for the first time (2026-09-06).** 383.3.e.1 — a `the first time … each turn` trigger *"will only be performed the specified number of times each turn"*, so N simultaneous sources of the same condition are worth ONE execution: three Sprite Fountains played the same turn give Shard of Undoing 2 forced kills, not 6; the 6 exists only as a schedule (T, T+2, T+4), because each Fountain feeds exactly two turns. 355.10.e / 355.10.f — an object *"chosen in whole or in part by other players"* or named in an instruction another player *"must"* complete is NOT a target (the worked examples are "Each player kills a unit they control" and "You must recycle one of your runes"), so the opponent picks which unit dies and [Deflect] never charges (809.1.c / 809.1.d charge only for targeting or choosing). Companion: `UNL-199 Deceiver`'s Reflection is [Temporary] and dies EVERY turn, which makes it the unbounded feed for a `when a friendly unit dies` gear — the exact inversion of `leblanc-zilean-reflection-doubling`, which spends UNL-090 to PREVENT that death (R10 = A); the two lines cannot share a battlefield. And the gear-Deathknell citation (808.1.a + 808.1.d.2, never 428.1.a.1.b) was finally applied to `sprite-fountain-malzahar-jayce`, three walks after it was diagnosed — grep for diagnosed-but-unapplied corrections before closing a walk.
- **An army walks in one action, and the arrivals cap is unsatisfiable in a Duel (#111 walk, 2026-09-06).** 144.3 — *"Players may perform multiple Units standard move simultaneously. This is treated as one game action performed on multiple Units"* (144.3.a: shared destination). 144.4.a.1 counts OTHER PLAYERS, not units, and its cross-reference 447.2.a opens with "In Modes of Play with more than two players", so with 485.1's two players there is no cap on how many of your own units enter a battlefield in one Main Phase. A token minted exhausted (143.4) pays 144.2's exhaust the same turn under SFD-171 Renata Glasc, Industrialist (mono-Order, legal in a Mind/Order shell by 103.1.b.3) — **and this sentence used to say "only under", which is wrong: 143.4.a says outright "This can be altered by Accelerate or similar game effects", and `OGN-011 Magma Wurm` (Fury, E8 P1 M8, "Other friendly units enter ready") is a second and WIDER source, since it reaches every friendly unit played from hand, not only tokens (#153 walk, 2026-09-06)**; otherwise the walk is next turn and the opponent's sweepers (SFD-158 at the base, OGN-229, UNL-180) get their window. Lee Sin, Centered's "+2 Might" is a CONTINUOUS MODIFIER, not a 702 buff counter: it never feeds a "buffed friends" count and never collides with 702.3. A lead that would add a sixth card to a five-card line goes in `notable`, never in `uses` — `uses` is what the matcher and planDeck price.
- **Champion progression and deck manipulation lenses (#116, #117, 2026-09-06 hunts; verify on walk).** The Champion Zone is a NON-BOARD zone (108.3, under 108 Non-Board Zones), unlike the Legend Zone (107.4.c makes the legend a Game Object on the board): a Chosen Champion sitting there is off the board entirely, and 108.3.c ("cannot be returned to this zone by normal means") makes Hallowed Tomb's refill a single event. XP is NOT a ratchet — the #116 walk corrected the hunt: 824.1.d makes a Level ability "Inactive as soon as the controlling player has less than [N] XP" and 730.2 ("To Spend XP, reduce the value of XP marked on the Player") is YOUR OWN spend, so paying XP switches your Level abilities off (registered on `wuju-master-blood-rose-level` since 2026-09-04); the lens still yields ENGINE only because nothing in the pool converts XP to points. 108.3.d: the Chosen Champion is playable from the Champion Zone, never drawn for. 315.2.b.2 Holds EVERY battlefield you control, so a Duel (485.4) pays a per-Hold legend twice a turn. [Flow] banishes the spell after the replay (829.1.b: "play this from your trash for its flow cost. Then banish it") and nothing in the pool returns a card from Banishment (108.6.c), so Flow is one replay per copy. Predict (436.1) and Vision (817.1.b) are defined with "look at", never "reveal" (424.1 is public presentation), so neither fires an "as I'm revealed" payoff; 817.1.c's trigger is "the permanent entering the Board", wider than "when played" — a token or a copy that enters fires it. Recycling always goes to the BOTTOM (416.1.a / 416.5), and no SINGLE deck-manipulation card in the pool turns a draw probability into a certainty - **but a PAIR does, and that is a refinement, not a contradiction (2026-09-07, #188)**: 436.1.a lets a Predict of N recycle any number and put the rest back *"on top of their Main Deck in any order"*, so a Predict N followed by a look at M cards with M <= N is a certainty, and 436.4.a says predicting with too few cards never Burns Out. `OGN-291 The Candlelit Sanctum` is the single-card near-miss already recorded above.
- **2v2 is section 489, its Victory Score is 11, and an [Action] reaches an OPENED combat (#118 hunt, 2026-09-06).** 486 is the 1v1 Match; 2v2 (Magma Chamber) is 489, with 489.3 "Victory Score: 11" against 194.3's default 8 — so every BURST and CHAIN in the catalogue is authored for Constructed and is three points short in 2v2 (issue filed). Teammates run two independent decks (489.8) and Domain Identity is per deck (103.1.b), while `matchDeck()` reads ONE list, so a team combo cannot be represented today. Only 4 cards in the pool name an ally ("teammate"/"partner" appear on none); the "or an ally holds" half of SFD-201 and UNL-193 is uncatalogued. 464.2.f.1 / 464.2.g put an [Action] window inside a combat that has already OPENED, wider than #58's pre-open window: a Defender's [Action] removal that empties the Attacker's side wins the combat with no damage and Conquers (465.1, 466.3.a).
- **Small keywords are swept and empty (#119, 2026-09-06).** Bonus Damage (712–715) and combat excess damage are DISJOINT systems: 713 scopes Bonus Damage to Deal actions and 417.6.c makes combat damage come from the units themselves, and all six Bonus Damage cards say "your spells and abilities deal N", so none feeds the OGN-034 / SFD-120 excess family. Prevent (437.4) stops damage from being marked, so no kill event is ever generated — a different mechanism from the heal/exhaust/recall shield of 808.1.d.1, and no repeatable Prevent card exists. Swap (433) has one real user, Switcheroo, already in eight entries; Green Father's "swapped back" is 438 Replace. Quick-Draw's mechanism (819.1.d) is already in `aphelios-jax-quickdraw-attach`. Fiora, Peerless's Double needs one-on-one combat (740.2.b), which forbids Sivir beside her. Amendment lead: UNL-141 Evelynn, Entrancing repeats `faefolk-challenger-forced-attacker`'s trick under Chaos via a [Hidden] play trigger.
- **Theft is one-way, Flow is six plays a game, and effect text after the colon is not a cost (#115 walk, 2026-09-06).** A stolen card resolves into its OWNER's trash (056 / 056.2: "If a card would enter such a zone, it goes to its owner's corresponding zone instead") and the unchosen cards go back to THEIR deck (416.1.c); 191.1 / 191.2 make the thief the controller of what they play; 431.1.c means a reveal never Burns the victim out. [Flow] banishes the spell after every Flow play (829.1.b / 829.1.b.1 is a delayed replacement), so three copies (103.2.b) are SIX plays a game, never an open engine. In "`<cost>`: Kill a friendly unit …", the kill is EFFECT text after the colon, so 416.3 / 203.3 / 185 never bite and a token can be killed (428.1 + 185.2.d); the cheapest fuel for such a hook is a body already condemned by 816.1.b — a [Temporary] Sprite (3 Might, 187.2) costs zero to kill early. And R2 = A cuts both ways: card-text "score 1 point" is a 194.1.c GAIN, not a 469 Score, so a discount worded "for each point you scored from holding" counts ONE of `ahri-trinity-svellsongur-hold`'s nine points; Needlessly Large Yordle is never free (E8 + 2 Calm Power against a Duel ceiling of two Holds, 485.4).
- **"Alone" is an own-side measurement, and Yasuo is Riot's worked example for attack triggers (#116 walk, 2026-09-06).** The pool's own reminder text settles "alone": SFD-036 Lonely Poro "(I am alone if there are no other friendly units here)" and UNL-210 Forbidding Waste say the same, so "if an enemy unit is alone here" means the OPPONENT has exactly one unit there and your garrison is unrestricted (touches UNL-143, UNL-117, UNL-124, OGN-055, OGN-060, OGS-019). "When I attack, deal damage equal to my Might to an enemy unit here" is the worked example at 359.3.f (Yasuo, Remorseful): Might is read AT EXECUTION (359.3.f.2), moving the unit home in response makes the trigger mistarget, and a control steal does not blank it (359.3.f.4) — cite the example instead of arguing. 383.4.e.2.a/b (and the .f mirror): an attack trigger's extra condition is checked only at the instant of the Attacker designation (464.2.c.3) and once per combat. Which combat resolves first is the Turn Player's call (461.1), not a chain-order question (429.2).
- **A cost after a leading "you may" is the trigger's BASE COST, paid at finalization; a granted keyword reaches a token as it enters (#117 walk, 2026-09-06).** 383.3.b: a cost written at the start of a Triggered Ability's effect or right after its opening "you may" / "they may" is the ability's base cost, and 383.3.b.1 pays it to FINALIZE the trigger onto the Chain — so a "when X, you may pay N to …" trigger that fires inside a Cleanup (no priority there: 312.2 / 453 / 323.12) or on the opponent's turn must have the Energy banked beforehand (164.2.a + 813.1.c.2, and 167 empties the pool at the Main Phase); a whole family of such triggers is priced wrong if read as pay-on-resolution. 383.2.c / 383.2.c.1: a trigger's condition is evaluated AFTER the inciting event, and an ability active in a zone triggers for an object entering that zone at the same time — so a static "your Mechs have [Vision]" is already on a Mech token (187.4 gives it the tag by rule) when 817.1.c's "entering the Board" is checked. 817.2 stacks a second Vision instance, 817.2.b is its limit. 323.14: an escalating Showdown does not count twice. Lead not yet hunted: OGN-121 Teemo, Strategist reveals the top FIVE cards on defend — a wider door to Undertitan than Diana, and a removal scaled by the [Hidden] package (15 units + 2 gear).
- **A Defender never Conquers by emptying the attacker's side, and an ally's Hold never doubles a legend (#118 walk, 2026-09-06).** 466.5 establishes Control only for a player "if they didn't already control this Battlefield", and 464.2.c.2 + 323.11.a make the Defender the player who already controls it — so `OGN-203 Possession` played by the NON-turn player inside the Combat Showdown the opponent's attack opened wins the combat with no damage, keeps the garrison and the Control, and the Hold survives (315.2.b.2 / 469.2), but it is a DEFENSIVE payoff, not a point; the attacker-side version of the same chain is `sinister-poro-attack-evacuate`. 465.3 is the second task inside 465.1's gated list, not an else-branch. Runes pay on the opponent's turn: 167 empties the pool at YOUR Main Phase, but 164.2.a / 164.2.b are [Reaction] and 164.2.b has no exhaust, so eight ready runes held through your own turn pay E8 + 3 Power there. 2v2 (489): "or an ally holds" on a legend whose cost is an exhaust yields ONE activation per team round-trip, since 315.1.b readies it only in YOUR Awakening — it buys a choice of window, never a second trigger; 489.8.c "Control is not shared"; 489.8.a: playing on a teammate's turn is an INVITATION using their priority, not an [Action] window. The 18-card "[Action] spell that removes a unit at a battlefield" family (806.1.b, 464.2.f.1 / 464.2.g, 465.1, 466.3.a, 466.5.d; OGN-168 and SFD-122 banned) is a synergy shape, recorded in the #118 walk §1.8.
- **A Signature card names the legend, the shuffle is 39 cards, and Spiderling beats the 3-copy cap (#141 walk, 2026-09-06).** 103.2.d.2 makes every Signature card carry the legend's champion tag, so ONE Signature card in a list forces the legend: SFD-191 Rabadon's Deathcrown (tagged Ornn) is legal only under SFD-189 Fire Below the Mountain, the pool's only Ornn legend; the same rule drops OGN-256 Fox-Fire from a Calm/Mind [Hidden] count and SFD-202 Hostile Takeover from Mind/Order under three of its four legends — a hunt that names a Signature card without naming the legend has an illegal shell. Deck arithmetic: 103.2 counts the Chosen Champion inside the 40 and 103.2.a.1 puts it in the Champion Zone before play, so a reveal or draw samples a 39-card deck; use the uniform-5-subset framing, which needs no hand-size assumption. VEN-097 Spiderling prints "Your deck can have any number of cards named Spiderling", and rule 002 makes card text beat 103.2.b — the 3-copy cap is not universal (check `checkBuild` honours it). A Defend trigger fires on YOUR turn when you drag an enemy body onto your own battlefield (190.3.a.1 + 450 key Contested on the MOVED unit's controller): UNL-141 Evelynn, Entrancing ([Hidden], mono-Chaos) and OGN-043 Charm (mono-CALM, not Calm/Mind - measured 2026-09-09) do it; none of the 24 enemy-movers is Mind/Order.
- **"Open" was half-defined in two verified entries, and playing TO a battlefield contests it (#146 walk, 2026-09-06).** 190.3.a.1: units moving to OR BEING PLAYED TO a battlefield apply Contested status — 450 speaks only of units that moved, so it is not the citation for the played half. 170.11.c ("unoccupied and uncontrolled") had been applied to `amateur-recital-free-evacuation` but never to `charm-evacuate-conquer` (which said "without a Controller") or `faefolk-challenger-forced-attacker` (which leaned on the Yone errata alone); both verdicts survive, the stated rule did not — the R31 retirement was a diagnosed-but-unapplied correction for three days. The four "play me to an open battlefield" cards (355.2.b; #146 counted five, but SFD-079 Bard MOVES units, 449, and grants no play permission) are walkers only where the battlefield is BOTH unoccupied and uncontrolled after the evacuation — never behind the Faefolk Challenger, who stands on the battlefield he emptied (170.11.a). An evacuator is never a walker: the play location is fixed at 355.2 during the play steps, before the Cleanup of 323.6 opens anything. XP sinks are a bounded step function (11 Spend-N-XP costs, 20 [Level N] clauses topping at [Level 16], none scores), so an XP faucet is never an engine on its own; and the Lux loops' identity is fixed at Mind/Order by 103.1.b.2, which keeps every mono-Chaos card out (103.1.b.3).
- **A "when you conquer here" battlefield fires once a game unless a [Temporary] garrison hands it back, a Recall ignores every movement lock, and Forge of the Fluft is the one free attach that chooses its carrier (#171, 2026-09-07).** Fourteen of the 66 battlefields carry "when you conquer here" (OGN-282, OGN-287, OGN-289, OGN-291, OGN-298, SFD-207, SFD-210, SFD-212, SFD-217, SFD-218, SFD-220, SFD-221, UNL-217, VEN-162 - the #171 walk said twelve and said none was catalogued; four already were, from #153 and the same night, and the count was corrected by the #175 cross-audit on 2026-09-07), and the other ten were not because 315.2.b.2 makes the Hold mandatory, 470 caps Scoring once per battlefield per turn and 348.2.a refuses Control to a player who already has it — the trigger fires once. The fix is a `[Temporary]` garrison: 816.1.b kills it at 315.2.a before scoring, 319.6 then 323.6 strip Control in 310.1's Neutral Open State, and it costs nothing in exposure because 144.1.a/b/c + 312.2.a leave the opponent no state in which they both hold priority and may move; it buys the TRIGGER, never a second point (`sprite-queen-targon-peak-conquer-cycle`, `treasure-hoard-renata-conquer-gold` — the first entry to declare `needs: conquer-engine`, which 32 entries produced and nothing consumed). 456.3 verbatim: *"A Recall cannot be prevented by actions and Game Effects that restrict or block Movement"* — so no movement lock in the pool stops 466.1.a.2's combat recall, a Guardian Angel save or 323.7's gear recall; `OGN-295 Vilemaw's Lair` ("Units can't move from here to base") strands only a garrison that meant to stay. `SFD-208 Forge of the Fluft` gives legends *"exhaust: Attach an Equipment you control to a unit you control"* — "to a unit", against [Weaponmaster]'s "to me" — so it is the ONLY free attach in the pool that can place a carrier-dependent Equipment (Svellsongur, Skyfall) on a chosen carrier, and 434.1.f + 434.4 / 434.4.a make it a free relocation across the board that is not a Move (434.1.g: re-attaching to the current carrier does nothing). 108.3.c makes the Champion Zone a one-way door and `OGN-281 Hallowed Tomb` is the pool's only key (2 corpus rows say "Champion Zone"; the other empties it); `UNL-172 LeBlanc, Fragmented` is the only unit that is both a legal Chosen Champion and carries a Deathknell (Kog'Maw, Karthus and Ekko have Deathknells and no legend prints their tag); 103.2.a.3 makes the Chosen Champion a NAME. And the ban marker for battlefields lives in `corpus_flat.txt`, not `cards.json`: five of the 47 uncatalogued battlefields are banned in both formats (OGN-276, OGN-284, OGN-285, OGN-290, OGN-292) — check `data/legality.json` before walking one.
- **381 splits every "exhaust this to X" card in two, Lunar Boon is the only [Reaction] discard, and a cost-fired trigger resolves before the card it paid for arrives (#169, 2026-09-07).** 381: *"All Activated Abilities can only be activated on the Controlling Player's Turn and during an Open State"* — never cited before — read with 383.3.c: an exhaust inside an ACTIVATED ability (a colon, 377.1) is your-turn, Open-State only unless the card carries [Action] (806.1.b) or [Reaction] (813.1.c.2), while an exhaust inside a TRIGGERED ability ("When you X, you may exhaust this to Y") fires on anybody's turn — `OGN-023 Unlicensed Armory` is the first family, `SFD-144 Spirit Wheel`, `OGN-072 Solari Shrine` and `SFD-195 Blade Dancer` the second. `UNL-125 Lunar Boon` is the ONLY [Reaction] discard in the pool (swept: discard ∧ [Reaction] = 1 row), so it is the only card that makes a discard-watching trigger fire on the opponent's turn. A trigger fired by a cost paid while PLAYING a card resolves BEFORE that card arrives — 340.1 resolves the newest Finalized item while the played card is still Pending — so the card being paid for can never be the target of what its own cost triggered (Brazen Buccaneer discarding Mask Mother: her +2 Might cannot go on him). And 466.5 establishes Control FIRST, then 466.5.d makes it a Conquer (469.1.b), so a battlefield's own "When you conquer here" already has you as Controller and 190.6.d never blanks it — no reading needed.
- **A conquer trigger is PRESENCE, not participation, so a bloodless Conquer is a mass trigger; and a card-text point is not a Score, so the two-Conquers-a-turn ceiling never sees it (#170, 2026-09-07).** 383.4.c.2.a: *"The Conquer Abilities of Units are put on the Chain as Pending Items after the Unit(s) these effects correspond to are present at a Battlefield when a player gains control of it"* — nothing asks which unit walked in or which the spell chose, so with 144.3 (multiple Standard Moves are ONE action to a shared destination) an evacuation spell scales with the number of conquer payoffs standing in the pack, not with copies of the spell; no Stare Down entry had cited it. 194.1 lists three SEPARATE items — 194.1.a Hold, 194.1.b Conquer, 194.1.c *"Spells, Triggered Abilities and Activated Abilities that instruct them to gain one or more points"* — 469 defines Scoring as Conquer or Hold only, 470 caps those two, 471.1.a.1 repeats it for the final point: `OGN-205 Yasuo, Windrider` (*"The third time I move in a turn, you score 1 point"*) scores 3 a turn under three copies and a mass ready, and 470 / 485.4 never see it (R2 = A, from the other side). *"Ready your units"* is exactly two cards in the pool — `SFD-192 Shurelya's Requiem` and `SFD-204 On the Hunt` (Body/Chaos, E1 P2) — names no unit, and therefore multiplies by COPIES, the one thing a per-unit effect mover cannot do.
- **A Signature card in `uses[]` makes `prerequisites.easy` a ONE-LEGEND field, not a domain field, and the test now says so (#167 cross-audit, 2026-09-07).** 103.2.d.2 forces the champion tag onto the legend, so a domain-correct list can be mostly illegal: three Moonfall (Diana) named four Mind/Chaos legends of which one was legal; Daisy! (Ivern) said "a Calm/Order legend", admitting six of eight base codes. The second test in `test/legend-lines.test.ts` intersects, for every Signature card an entry uses, the legends carrying its champion tag with the legends covering the entry's domains — and its FIRST run reached fourteen entries no audit had opened, among them the correction #141 had diagnosed three days earlier and nobody applied (Hostile Takeover under non-Renata legends), Curtain Call under non-Jhin legends, Death from Below, Relentless Pursuit, and `swain-shurelya-double-conquer`, whose legend line #166 had just "fixed" to four Calm/Mind names when Shurelya's Requiem is Ornn's Signature and forces Fire Below the Mountain alone. All fourteen were rebuilt from the census. A uniqueness claim survives only at the precision it was measured at: "the only two `While I'm buffed` statics" is true, "the only cards that key on carrying a buff" is not (Sett Kingpin counts them, Spirit's Refuge grants them [Deflect], Vanguard Helm and The Boss react to one dying).
- **A count of entries is a perishable claim and a rule is not; an answer supplied to an open question must remove the question in the same edit (self-audits of 2026-09-07).** Two walks pointed the audit at their own work and each found two defects. "Nine entries use Blue Sentinel and none spends its [Add]" and "VEN-106 is in zero entries" were both false within the hour — parallel walks moved the counts — so **when a note cites how many entries use a card, write it AS OF the walk date, and keep the durable half, which is always the rule, never the count.** `last-rites-sentinel-trash-holds` was #165's defect inverted: the prose demanded a separate carrier while `uses[]` (correctly) had two cards — 818.1.b attaches to any unit you control and the Sentinel IS one — so an optional deckbuilding choice must not be written as a requirement. And `daisy-green-father-four-tag-attacker` carried a notable saying the Brush question was open beside two notables answering it: **the session that supplies the answer is the one that knows the stale text has to go, and it goes in the same edit.** An audit-time check is worth keeping as a standing one; the defect you find in someone else's work is usually in your own, in mirror image. **And a count of CARDS is checkable in a way a count of entries is not, so name the members**: the #175 cross-audit (2026-09-07, 52 of 54 entries clean) found a walk claiming twelve battlefields carry "when you conquer here" when a one-line grep returns fourteen, and claiming none was catalogued when four were - and the sentence had already propagated into this file within the hour. Write the base codes into the claim; the next reader then refutes it in one grep instead of inheriting it.
- **Legend-list arithmetic in `prerequisites.easy` is the failure class the ledgers are not, and a spell leg with a target is a dependency (#166 cross-audit, 2026-09-07).** Over 30 entries, 1,429 rule references across 182 paragraphs all existed and the twelve load-bearing ones said what was claimed; the four defects were three legend lists (three Mind/Chaos legends named as able to run an Order card; "two legends" where the census has four names; a name-vs-base-code slip) and one silent dependency — `swain-shurelya-double-conquer`'s spell leg was `VEN-061 Decree of Insight`, *"Give an enemy Body unit −5 Might"*, which has no legal target against a board with no Body unit, so the spell cannot be played and all six Swain points evaporate; a leg that only has to BE a spell should be one you can always cast — **filler must be unconditionally castable, and a targeting spell never is**; a card chosen purely to satisfy a "you have played [type] this turn" condition is doing no work of its own, which is exactly why it gets picked on cost and never re-read. The same author's legend lists went wrong the same way: the entries whose domain check was RUN against `cards.json` were right, the ones TYPED from memory were wrong, and the wrong list was copied between two entries — an unchecked claim propagated twice. `test/legend-lines.test.ts` now checks every legend base code named in `prerequisites.easy` against the domains the entry's `uses[]` indicate (103.1.b), so the census does the arithmetic. Two rows on one card set are allowed only as a loop-financed twin — identical cards, `needs` declared on one so the matcher can route a loop into the window — and both must cross-reference each other and share the twin's `produces` (a BURST never produces `win-the-game`). The #166 scope was 30 entries, not the ~45 the request assumed: the 29 written from the orphan synergy rules (#153) were in nobody's audit and got their own.
- **A card a Trigger Condition REQUIRES belongs in `uses[]`, and the headline of an entry should be its own shortfall (#165 cross-audit, 2026-09-07).** `shen-kinkou-svellsongur-hold` said in its notable, its first step and its cost line that Shen needs "exactly one other unit you control here" (383.2.a.1 makes the count part of the Trigger Condition; a lone Shen places nothing on the Chain) — and the companion was not in `uses[]`, so `matchDeck` reported a 9-point BURST complete for a board that scores ZERO and `planDeck` could never suggest the missing card. `uses` is what the matcher and the planner price: a body the condition requires is a use at quantity 1, role `enabler`, not a phrase in a notable; Equipment never fills a "unit" slot.
