The same audit found 51 of 53 entries clean — the walk had counted its [Equip] costs, cited paragraphs that say what it claims, and stated its own caps — and singled out the shape to want more of: `ferrous-forerunner-karthus-mech-plaza` opens its notable with *"SIX IS ONE SHORT OF SEVEN, AND THAT IS THE ENTRY'S REAL CONTENT"*, which is exactly why its arithmetic survived. A `terminatesIn` that prices the trick ("4 Energy") and not the board ("on a Jinx already in play, E5 + 1 Power") is the "18 Energy" shape again.
- **344.2 and 323.9 are two different ways to take a battlefield, attack triggers exist on only one of them, and an unbounded token engine does not beat a sweeper (#159 Azir refusal, 2026-09-07).** 344.2: *"If Control of a Battlefield is Contested, there aren't units controlled by different players there, and the turn is in a Neutral Open State, a Showdown is opened during the next Cleanup"* — no combat, no losses. 323.9: *"Mark a Combat as Staged at each Battlefield that Contested was applied to that have units present controlled by opposing players."* An attack trigger (383.4.e needs the Attacker designation; 807.1.d and 461 need opposing units) lives ONLY on the 323.9 path, so on the unoccupied board `ready-recruits-grand-plaza` is written for, `SFD-177 Azir, Sovereign`'s ability never fires and he is a 4-Energy body. On the garrisoned board, survivors are K − D and unbounded K clears seven trivially — but the Plaza pays at YOUR next Beginning Phase, the opponent takes a whole turn first, and `OGN-133 Flurry of Blades` (Body, E1, [Reaction], *"Deal 1 to all units at battlefields"*) kills every 1-Might survivor SIMULTANEOUSLY for ANY K: **the binding constraint on every 1-Might Plaza line is Might PER BODY, not the number of bodies, so an unbounded token engine does not unlock it and the only fix in the pool is `UNL-077 Soul Shepherd`** (Mind — which narrows the shell to Mind/Order: garen-fiora-malzahar-facebreaker-recruits yes, jhin-fiora-facebreaker-recall no, 103.1.b.4). `SFD-171 Renata Glasc, Industrialist` strictly dominates Azir for the job — a static ("Your tokens enter ready", every token, permanently) against a trigger that fires once per combat, needs an enemy garrison and reaches one battlefield — and 144.3 moves a ready swarm as one action anyway. Azir's genuinely unique edge is narrow: EXHAUSTED tokens moved battlefield to battlefield with no [Ganking] (420.3.a puts the exhaust on the Standard Move only; 810.1.c.3 says Ganking only widens it), which consolidates a split board and no Plaza line needs. `lady-luminosity-loop-comet` stays at zero point routes ON PURPOSE (two Conquers a turn is the ceiling); connecting it would be a regression.
- **Empower is a once-per-object event, so the partner set of every "empower" anchor is the DISEMPOWERERS, and a Hold-triggered buff readies nothing (#159 groups 31–39, 2026-09-07).** 441.1.b *"An Empowered Game Object can not be Empowered"*, 441.1.c *"nothing additional happens"*, 441.2 (a permanent state): a *"When you empower something else"* legend (`VEN-151 Soul's Reflection`) gets NOTHING from a board full of Empowered permanents, and a *"When I become [Empowered]"* unit (`VEN-110 Mel`, `VEN-047`, `VEN-114`, `VEN-104`) fires once per copy per game — so the real partners for all six empower-other anchors are the cards that DISEMPOWER (`VEN-035 Sanction`, `VEN-082 Profiteer`, `VEN-099 Tornado Warrior`, and the permanents whose payoff cost begins "Disempower this/me": `VEN-054 Questionable Tome`, `VEN-087 Hextech Disc`, the two legends themselves) — single digits against the 35–38 a "when you empower" regex reports. Those two anchor sets are TWO synergy rules on one `/[Dd]isempower/` predicate, not one: the legends want a repeatable source of the event, the units want to be disempowered so their own trigger fires twice. ``VEN-110 Mel, Defiant Soul` is ONE OF FIVE [Empower] costs in the pool that pay neither Energy nor Power - *"Discard a spell"*, beside `VEN-054 Questionable Tome` and `VEN-087 Hextech Disc` (an exhaust each, cheaper than a card), `VEN-007 Punching Poro` (discard 1) and `VEN-124 Escaped Grayback` (kill a friendly unit, which 185.2.d makes FREE when it is a token); the earlier text here called her the cheapest outright, corrected 2026-09-07 by reading every [Empower] cost string in the corpus - and her banish is permanent (108.6.c) but lands AT END OF TURN, so a [Reaction] enabler never makes it instant-speed removal. And `SFD-047 Simian Ancestor` ("When you buff me, ready me") under `OGN-283 Navori Fighting Pit` ("When you hold here, buff a unit here") is a NO-OP on the phase boundary — 315.1.b has already readied him when the Hold fires at 315.2.b.2 — true of every Hold-triggered buff in the pool; a ready-on-buff pays only when the buff arrives in the MAIN PHASE after the unit spent its exhaust, and 702.3 then caps it at one ready per buff SPENT (nine buff-spenders in the pool).
- **The Awaken Phase is a free engine, the two "Mighty" legends want opposite decks, and a high uncovered-partner count marks a WELL-MINED card (#161 rows 40–47, 2026-09-07).** 315.1.b *"The Turn Player readies all Game Objects they control that are able to be readied"* with 415.1.b (only the exhausted ones — every unit that acted last turn): `VEN-088 Jayce, Hammer in Hand` ("When I become ready, choose one…"), `OGN-143 Pirate's Haven` ("When you ready a friendly unit, give it +1 Might this turn") and `VEN-071 Fretful Feline` ("When I become ready, give me +2 Might this turn") all fire on your own upkeep every turn with no card spent — the pairing was never "find a readier"; the game readies for you and nobody was collecting (`awaken-ready-body-triple-trigger`, the three in one mono-Body entry, none of them in an entry before). Same keyword, opposite builds: `SFD-205 Grand Duelist` — *"When one of your units **becomes** [Mighty]"* — is governed by 709's crossing EVENT, pays once per sub-5 body pushed over (702.2.a keeps the buff there, 702.3 stops a re-crossing), and its ceiling is the number of DIFFERENT unbuffed Might-4 bodies you can present; `OGN-249 Relentless Storm` — *"When you **play** a [Mighty] unit"* — needs no crossing, pays on any body already at 5+ as it enters, every time, no buff. `UNL-074 Frigid Jewel` reads *"your **second** card each turn"*, so one extra draw arms it and every further draw is wasted — refused on its own wording; its 100 "partners" and `SFD-075 Prize of Progress`'s 62 were a bare-exhaust / any-draw regex artifact, and the Prize earned its entry only because `OGN-120 Seal of Insight` is E0 and already the Mind deck's Power base. And the matrix's own ranking lies at the top end: `SFD-180 Fiora, Worthy` showed 117 uncovered partners, the most of any trigger card, while sitting in THIRTEEN entries and two rules of her own — a pairwise coverage test removes a pair only when those two cards share an entry, so every MECHANISM can be catalogued while the particular partners are not. **Read a high uncovered-partner count as a warning flag, not a prize.**
- **A ready that buys nothing, the [Hidden] cycle's two halves a turn apart, and three clause-level facts from the matrix's rows 6–15 (#160, 2026-09-07).** Refuse-by-rule bucket E: a "ready me" trigger is ONE extra Standard Move and nothing on defence (464.2.c.3 and 465.2.b do not filter by state, #61), and nothing at all if the unit has no exhaust-cost ability — so a ready trigger only pairs with a cause that fires on YOUR turn, and the "+1 Might" rider is usually the real payoff; this generalises "a Hold-triggered ready is a no-op" (315.1 before 315.2) to every window where moves are illegal (144.1.b) or pointless. 811.1.b reads *"hide this facedown at a battlefield you control … beginning on the next turn … play this, ignoring its base cost"*, so the hide and the play are a TURN apart — `UNL-023 Katarina, Reckless` is the only card watching both events and is paid twice by one card for one rainbow Power; 811.1.d.2 decides WHERE (a hidden spell targets at the battlefield it was hidden at, so Hidden Blade kills there, while Katarina's own "deal 2" is her ability and unrestricted). `SFD-139 Edge of Night` is the only [Hidden] card that ATTACHES ITSELF on the face-down play (*"When you play this from face down, attach it to a unit you control"*): 818.1 makes Equip an Activated Ability with a cost, the play trigger is an effect, so the [Equip] is never paid. 437.4 — *"Damage dealt to a Unit that has all of that damage Prevented is not considered to have been dealt to it at all"* — generates NO kill event, a different mechanism from the would-die replacements of #119. `SFD-142 Jae Medarda`'s clause says "when you choose me **with a spell**", so every unit and gear in his partner list is a false positive, unlike Blade Dancer and Spirit Wheel, which fire on any choose including a buff (702.2.a). And `OGN-107 Ava Achiever` plays a [Hidden] card FROM HAND — 811.1.c.1 *"Hide is not a subset of Play"* — so neither Katarina nor the Ember Monk ever sees her (an `excludes` on both anchors).
- **[Tank] makes every other body an INVALID assignment, "recycle" catches every rune unless the predicate says otherwise, and a matrix ranks on the TRIGGER side (#159 rows 19–30, #161 close-out, 2026-09-07).** 815.1.c.2: *"Units without Tank are invalid assignments until all units with Tank have lethal damage assigned to them"* — so four 1-Might [Tank] Birds from `UNL-044 Flurry of Feathers` (the only card that plays four token units at [Reaction] speed) under `UNL-058 Lillia` absorb exactly four damage (465.2.c.4) and nothing else of yours may legally be assigned any; Lillia is therefore ANTI-synergic with the whole Grand Plaza family, whose seven bodies must survive. `OGN-228 Vanguard Helm` + Baited Hook: **this file said the ceiling climbs by TWO and that is FALSE, corrected 2026-09-12 (#191 batch 23).** The reasoning was that 703 makes a buff exactly +1, so a buffed corpse would be read at printed Might plus one. Two paragraphs refute it and both were cited by nothing: **705** — *"If a Unit leaves play, remove all Buffs from it"* — so the corpse carries no buff at all; and **711** — *"Units in Non-Board Zones are evaluated according to their printed Might"* — whose own example ends *"It doesn't matter if there were effects raising or lowering its might while it was on the board."* **The ladder climbs by exactly ONE** (each fetched body's PRINTED Might sets the next ceiling) **and the Helm contributes NOTHING to it**; its real value there is +1 Might on a LIVING body, which is worth having and is much smaller than this file claimed. 711 is the trash half of the same split whose board half this catalogue already owned, the 815.2-against-815.3 shape again — and the entry standing on the false sentence was corrected in the same commit. Two predicate traps for the synergy layer: `OGN-235 Karma, Channeler` prints *"(Runes aren't cards.)"* and 161.2.b sends a recycled rune to the Rune Deck, so any "when you recycle" predicate that does not exclude runes catches every 164.2.b activation in the game; and every [Weaponmaster] body and `SFD-024 Rell, Magnetic` attach Equipment to THEMSELVES, so they never satisfy another unit's "attach an Equipment to me" trigger — the honest partner set is the EQUIPMENT, not the attachers. A ranked matrix or co-occurrence list is NOT legality-checked: run the ban and Signature check on every trigger card before spending a walk on its family (`OGN-177 Stealthy Pursuer` reached row 20; `pursuer-herald-recruits` is a verified INFINITE that legality drops at match time). Matrix close-out: over the 1,522 unrefused candidates the trigger-side distribution is 1–5 partners: 4 groups · 6–10: 3 · 11–20: 4 · 21–40: 29 · 41+: 7 — four tight groups in the whole pool, all walked; everything else is a family, walked as one entry per trigger card with its best partner plus a synergy-rule lead. **Grouping by CAUSE card is a mirage**: 785 of 812 cause groups show 1–5 "partners" only because each event has 2–9 trigger cards in the whole pool; rarity is measured on the trigger side or not at all.
- **190.6.d blanks only the word "you", a "one or more" trigger counts EVENTS, and the small-combo matrix's method (#161 batch 6, 2026-09-07).** 190.6.d: *"'You' in a battlefield's abilities refers to the battlefield's Controller … If the battlefield has no Controller, 'you' refers to no one, and all such instructions are ignored"* — the SCOPE is the word. `UNL-214 Ripper's Bay` says *"that player may pay 1 Energy to channel 1 rune exhausted"*, never "you", and 190.6.b keeps an uncontrolled battlefield's abilities on the Chain, so the Bay works from turn one before anyone walks in and pays the opponent too; every "a battlefield you bring starts switched off" argument must first check that the text says "you". `OGN-202 Jinx, Rebel`'s *"When you discard one or more cards"* fires once per discard EVENT, so three cards that discard one each beat one card that discards three — 383.1.b's collapse of simultaneous instances, reached from the other side. **The matrix** (the user's "small combos" request, walked by #161): 1,028 deckable base codes × 24 events harvested from the corpus's 142 trigger clauses; coverage folded from every synergy `partnersOf` pair and every entry card set; 1,620 same-identity uncovered pairs in 49 groups, then four refused-by-rule buckets counted rather than dropped — A Hold payoff + Main-Phase engine (4), B excess-damage + bloodless removal (0, a real result), C [Hidden] attacker (7), D banned in every format (87 — the first pass read a scalar `base` from `data/legality.json`, whose `entries[].bases` is an ARRAY, and matched nothing; the ban marker is applied to `corpus_flat.txt`, not `cards.json`). Two classifier traps to carry into any rebuild: `/kill (a|an|…)/` matches "kill **al**l gear", and `/\[hidden\]/` matches cards that merely MENTION Hidden. The rarest event in the pool (rarity 5, Battle Mistress + Sigil of the Storm) was a refusal — the Sigil's recycle is mandatory and 164.2.b is the RUNE's own ability, so an instruction to recycle is not it — and the matrix rediscovering a trap CLAUDE.md already names is what a good matrix does. `SFD-138 Windsinger` prints `Hidden` bracket-less (registered anomaly), so the two `\[Hidden\]` predicates were widened to the reminder-anchored form `\[?Hidden\]? ?\(Hide now for` — the bare `\[?Hidden\]?` let in `VEN-160 Mystic Vortex`, which only mentions the keyword in Reaction's reminder text; no other bracketed keyword prints bracket-less anywhere in the pool (Laurent Bladekeeper's `Ganking` is caught because those rules match the bare word). And a DOM test that assumes "this fixture has no near miss" is a test the catalogue will outgrow: search for the empty state, do not assume it.
- **Every in-turn repeatable point ability in the pool is MIND, unbounded removal is worth exactly what bounded removal is worth, and #63's routing table is superseded (#159 INFINITE × payoff pass, 2026-09-07).** `SFD-088 Renata Glasc, Mastermind` is the only card that converts resources to a point inside the Main Phase at all, and she, Time Warp and Bottled Constellation are all Mind; everything else scores on a Hold or a Conquer. 315 puts the Hold BEFORE 316 and 167 empties the pool at end of turn, so a loop's unbounded Energy exists only in the Main Phase — **for a non-Mind loop the CONQUER is the only window its Energy can be spent into**, which is why the two Calm/Fury loops had no point route until `reveler-loop-nasus-brambleback-conquer` (a BURST on the same card set as `nasus-ascended-brambleback-conquer` declaring `needs: infinite-energy`, on the precedent of the two Brambleback + Trinity + Skyfall twins that differ only in `needs`: the twin is what lets the matcher connect a loop to its window). And removal has a ceiling no loop lifts: 469.1 defines Conquer on a battlefield "not yet Scored this turn", 470 caps Scoring once per battlefield per turn and 485.4 puts two on a Duel table, so **two Conquers a turn whether you fire one comet or a thousand** — `lady-luminosity-loop-comet` stays at zero routes on purpose. Do not price from #63 §3.1/3.2 ("2 of 12 engines connected"): run `generateVariants` over the current catalogue and count variants headed by a BURST/CHAIN/ALT_WIN per engine — it read 11 of 14 after this pass, because #64's feature merge connected the four Recruit engines to the Plaza. Companion from #160 batch 9: the *"and vice versa"* half of `SFD-030 Skyfall of Areion` is the better half and no entry had used it — the catalogued direction turns a per-turn Hold payoff into a one-off Conquer, the other turns a CONQUER payoff into a HOLD payoff that fires every Beginning Phase forever (three Culls on one bearer: 3 Gold EVERY Hold instead of once), except where the conquer payoff's condition only a combat can meet (Yeti Brawler, Tryndamere: a Hold assigns no damage, and 383.2.a.1 makes that clause part of the Trigger Condition).
- **Time Warp is the pool's ONLY extra-turn source, so every Additional-Turn CHAIN must admit Mind, and 3N ≥ 8 is the test (#161 batch 5, 2026-09-07).** A sweep of all 935 base codes for "take a turn" / "additional turn" / "extra turn" returns one row, `OGN-122 Time Warp` (Mind, E10 P4, *"Take a turn after this one. Banish this"*); it is three copies (103.2.b) and banishes rather than recycles, so up to FOUR consecutive Beginning Phases exist and an engine scoring N per turn chains at N ≥ 3, not N ≥ 4. An ENGINE whose identity excludes Mind can never become one: `lucian-skyfinity-double-conquer` (Fury/Body) is the best CHAIN candidate in the catalogue by arithmetic — its own `terminatesIn` says "eight across two turns, with the opponent's turn in between, which is why it is not a CHAIN" — and is unrescuable on 103.1.b alone. `shen-sentinel-time-warp-chain`: **Shen's only weakness is free inside a Time Warp** — his "exactly one other unit" garrison of two is safe for exactly as long as the opponent takes no turn, and K is 1 on purpose. `SFD-150 Last Rites` prints *"(You still pay its costs.)"*, so a Sentinel doubles a PERMISSION, not a price — card advantage, not tempo — and its [Equip] cost (a Chaos rune AND "Recycle 2 cards from your trash") is self-limiting and cannot be freed by [Weaponmaster], which discounts a single rune. And the Veteran Poro rewrite of the Weaponmaster BURST caught two errors in the entry it replaced: "eight bodies" were FIVE (Trinity Force is gear), and "22 damage to clear" was the wrong question, because the burst needs T × (1 + K) ≥ 7 and ANY single loss breaks it — the version that survives a loss is K = 3.
- - **805.6 and 805.6.a are the RULES-TEXT HOME of two things this project derived, including the mechanism R25 was ruled on (#191 batch 20, 2026-09-12).** This file sources *"enters ready is not a ready"* to 143.4 read with 415.1; 805.6 says it outright and names the mechanism - *"Accelerate generates a delayed replacement effect that replaces a unit entering the board exhausted with it entering ready. It does not enter exhausted and then become ready."* - and 805.6.a states the consequence alone: *"Accelerate will not interact with, or trigger, abilities that are affected by units becoming ready."* **Widened 2026-09-12 (#187 batch 9): this is not Accelerate`s private problem.** 805.6.a is scoped to the keyword, but `OGN-021 Sun Disc` grants the same enters-ready property with NO keyword, and 415.1 closes it for the same reason — Readying is marking a Game Object ALREADY on the board. So **no enters-ready grant in this pool, keyworded or not, ever fires a become-ready payoff**, and the three payoffs remain exactly `OGN-143 Pirate`s Haven`, `VEN-071 Fretful Feline` and `VEN-088 Jayce, Hammer in Hand`, all mono-Body (re-measured 2026-09-12). A Fury/Body line reaching for the Sun Disc to fire one is refuted on its face. Both were cited by nothing. It is ALSO the general form of the replacement R25 was ruled on through 369.3 / 370.1.c on 2026-09-04, so that ruling now has a paragraph of its own rather than an argument. Two excludes fall out of it, measured and named: the pool has exactly THREE become-ready payoffs, all mono-Body and all three already in `awaken-ready-body-triple-trigger` (`OGN-143 Pirate`s Haven`, `VEN-071 Fretful Feline`, `VEN-088 Jayce, Hammer in Hand`), and **Accelerate feeds none of them**, so any future line reaching for Accelerate to fire one is refuted on its face; and exactly TWO cards name an EXHAUSTED friendly unit as a target (`OGN-124 Arena Bar`, `UNL-201 Voidreaver`), which an Accelerated body never is until it has spent its own Standard Move. Companion from the same batch: **813.3 / 813.3.a and their Action twin 806.3** restate 355.2.a`s confinement from the keyword side (*"Playing Units with Reaction still has the inherent restrictions of playing Units without Reaction"*), which is what makes 822.1.b`s FIRST half do real work - Ambush has to grant the location permission separately, because the Reaction half grants none.
**"Enters ready" is worth nothing on a UNIT token made on the opponent's turn and everything on a GEAR token whose ability costs its own exhaust (#160 batch 8, 2026-09-07).** 144.1.b forbids the Standard Move in a Closed State, combat does not read exhaustion (#61), and 315.1.b readies the unit at your own Awaken before you could use it — so R25 = A adds ZERO to a Recruit or Sprite born on the opponent's turn, which kills `OGN-117 Viktor, Innovator` as a Renata, Industrialist pairing outright. A Gold is the opposite case: 187.5 is *"[Reaction] Kill this, [exhaust]: [Add] [rainbow]"*, so an EXHAUSTED Gold cannot pay its own cost and is dead until your next Awaken, and Renata makes it live Power in the window it was created (`chemtech-cask-renata-off-turn-power`). 415.3.a is the rule under the whole off-turn-mana theme — *"A player Readies all non-spell Game Objects they Control during the Awakening Phase on their turn"* — so a rune exhausted on your turn is dead for the whole of the opponent's; Dark Child hands two back at 164.2.a's 1 Energy each at Reaction speed (exactly Abandon's cost), the Cask + Renata fixes it from the Gold side. And `OGN-113 Malzahar, Fanatic`'s token-for-2-rainbow is SHOWDOWN-SPEED mana (806.1.b, [Action]), a fact none of the seven entries that use him states; a generic row for him was refused as a strict subset of all seven.
- **The BURST class is saturated, and [Weaponmaster] equips "to ME", so it only ever pays for carrier-independent Equipment (#161 batch 4, 2026-09-06).** All fifteen BURSTs were re-derived and every one is already over 8 with its own declared quantities (9 to 17), so more points is worth nothing to the class; its three levers are closed by sweeps — six cards score on a Hold or a Conquer (Ahri, Trinity Force, Ivern Friend to All, Shen, Nasus Ascended, Power Nexus), all catalogued under at least one multiplier; exactly three multipliers exist; and the [Equip] cost is bounded: 29 Equipment carry a one-rune cost (free under [Weaponmaster], *"[Equip] one of your Equipment to me for one rainbow less"*) and 6 carry Energy + rune (only partly discounted). Because the keyword says **to me**, it frees only Equipment whose text does not depend on the carrier: `SFD-115 Trinity Force` ("When I hold, score 1 point") works on any body, so the free attach is pure profit; `SFD-059 Svellsongur` copies the CARRIER and must sit on the payoff unit, so Weaponmaster can never free a Svellsongur attach on a copy line — `svellsongur-copy-hold`, `ivern-svellsongur-four-tags-hold` and `shen-kinkou-svellsongur-hold` keep paying their Calm Power on purpose; `SFD-030 Skyfall` ("MY hold effects") has the same limit, which is why `lucian-skyfinity-double-conquer` can use it (Lucian carries both) and `skyfall-ahri-conquer` cannot. Only four carrier-independent Equipment have a hold or conquer effect at all — Trinity Force (points), World Atlas (Gold), Last Rites (reanimation), Hunter's Machete (XP) — and only Trinity Force scores. `SFD-099 / UNL-223 Veteran Poro` (Body, E2 M2, plain [Weaponmaster]) is the cheapest Weaponmaster body in the pool and was in no entry; `SFD-102 Hexdrinker` (one-rune Equip, [Deflect]) is the carrier-independent answer to the single point of failure every Sentinel BURST names; `VEN-041 Riven, Shattered` is the only Weaponmaster body that pays for what it accumulates. Standing picture: Plaza, the Sentinel BURSTs, the Brambleback conquer BURSTs and the ALT_WIN class are closed by sweeps; **the CHAIN class is the only finisher class with room**, and it is bounded by two Conquers per turn (470 + 485.4) — which points at sub-8 Hold engines × Time Warp.
- **The ALT_WIN class stands on 195, not 194.2; a Hold-triggered ready is a no-op; a [Hidden] removal can never attack; a bloodless conquer has zero excess damage (#159 batch 4, #160 batches 6–7, #161 batch 3; 2026-09-06).** The pool has exactly THREE "win the game" cards (swept: `OGN-293 The Grand Plaza`, `UNL-088 Gutter Palace`, `OGN-276 Aspirant's Climb` — the last banned in both formats, a marker that lives in the card TEXT and that no card-set check surfaces), and 195 (*"a player also wins the game if an effect instructs them to do so"*) is a DIFFERENT clause from 194.2 - which is **not** "points >= Victory Score", the shorthand this file used until 2026-09-07: it reads *"greater than or equal to the Victory Score AND MORE POINTS THAN ANY OTHER PLAYER"*, with 194.2.b adding that tied players keep playing, so a BURST taking you from 0 to exactly 8 against an opponent already on 8 does NOT win, so raising the Victory Score costs every BURST and CHAIN a point and touches an ALT_WIN not at all — no entry had said why the class is robust. `gutter-palace-reaction-dials`: the Palace's own adjust ability has no [Reaction], so the 4-cards / 4-units check at 315.2.a is only defensible with Reaction-speed dials (`UNL-071 Chakram Dancer` via 822.1.b was in zero entries), and **the opponent's removal opens the window the correction needs** — 316.5.b gives you nothing in their Neutral Open State, but their spell on the Chain is a Closed State, 312.2.c hands out priority there and 813.1.c.1 admits a [Reaction]; so the answer is played IN RESPONSE, never after, and the count is done FORWARD (they aim at one of four, you go to five, they resolve, you are at four); your Draw Step 315.4 comes AFTER the check, which is what makes a fixed hand size holdable. **Any payoff that READIES is worthless on a Hold trigger**: 315.1.b has readied everything before 315.2.b's Hold — the third consequence of the Awaken order, and it kills `UNL-043 Enthusiastic Promoter` and `OGN-283 Navori Fighting Pit` as `fiora-buff-ready` partners even though both fire her. `OGN-078 Lee Sin, Ascetic` is printed Might 5, so 709's second example applies verbatim and he can NEVER trigger Fiora; `OGN-143 Pirate's Haven` ("When you ready a friendly unit…") closes twice — the readied unit was already Mighty, and readying a ready unit performs no ready (415.1). `OGN-124 Arena Bar` is the only buff source whose TARGET clause names an exhausted unit (swept `-i "exhausted friendly"`: Voidreaver's exhausted clause is a MOVE). **No [Hidden] removal can play the attacker-side role of the #118 family**: 811.1.b hides *"at a battlefield you control"* and 811.1.d.2 pins the targets there, and the battlefield you attack is by definition not one you control — Fox-Fire, Hidden Blade and Sprite Call are defender-side only, where 466.5 gives no point. **A bloodless conquer generates ZERO excess damage**: 465.1 assigns nothing and R28 = A defines excess as attacking Might never assigned, so the removal-conquer family (Rebuke, Wind and Ghosts, Alpha Strike, Noxian Guillotine) and the six excess-damage cards are mutually exclusive IN PRINCIPLE, not only by domain. Azir, Sovereign + the Plaza is refused with the count: dragged tokens are Attacking Units (464.2.c.3.a), the defender's summed Might D kills D of them at 1 Might each (465.2.c.4), so the Plaza wants K ≥ 6 + D tokens AND an opponent turn survived; his real niche is tokens made THIS turn, since 315.1.b + 144.3 walk yesterday's swarm without him. **A Svellsongur copy carries a card's DRAWBACK as well as its payoff** — `SFD-123 Corrupt Enforcer`'s *"When I win a combat, draw 1"* has no "may", so eight instances are a FORCED eight-card draw into 431.1.a: read every sentence of the copied card, not the one you copy it for. `UNL-105 Imposing Challenger` at eight instances carries #58's 323.10 route by himself (his move trigger holds a Neutral Closed State, 323.13 cannot open the Combat, the garrison empties, only the Showdown remains). 431.1.c.1 extends the no-Burn-Out protection to the zone change that FOLLOWS a reveal, which is what makes `svellsongur-teemo-strategist-defend-burst` safe at forty reveals.
- **438 Replace answers the Green Father question by name, two conquers a turn is the Duel ceiling, and [Temporary] fodder lives inside one Main Phase (#161 batch 3, 2026-09-06).** The deferral over what a Brush battlefield TOKEN is for 190.x was resolved from the rules text, not a reading — R31's retirement again: 438.1 *"Replacing is the act of Creating a token in the place of another card or token without playing it while inheriting all effects or statuses of the game object it replaced"*, and 438.1.a's worked example IS *"A player with Green Father as their legend conquers Navori Fighting Pit"*, ending *"the Brush inherited all statuses and conditions"* — Control is a status, so it carries across and back (438.7.b), 190.6.a gives the Brush's text to its Controller, 190.6.d's blanking never fires, and 438.5.a keeps the replaced battlefield *"Replaced and not Banished"* so 108.6.c does not bite; `UNL-T03 Brush` itself prints *"When you score here"*. It is a TOKEN, so it is never a `uses[]` row (0 of the entries name a token code; the synergy layer filters them) — a notable on the legend's line, and its ceiling is ENGINE because only Fury converts Might to points. Uncatalogued and now noted: Green Father is a DENIAL tool, since "that battlefield" may be the opponent's and only the Brush's controller may swap it back. **Grep `^438\.` before filing a reading over any printed term; the rules used the card as their own example.** Next: 470 refuses a second Score at an already-scored battlefield and 485.4 puts two on the table, so **two Conquers per turn is the hard ceiling in a Duel** and every conquer-based CHAIN must reach 8 from TWO scoring events — which is exactly why `swain-shurelya-double-conquer` needs three payoff bodies (1 + 3, twice); `fiora-vault-breaker-jhin` and `jhin-relentless-pursuit-wallop` cap at exactly 2 a turn and their extra moves buy Energy and Power, not points. And the third phase-order fact from the same source (315.1 Awaken → 315.2 Beginning → 316 Main): 816.1.b kills every `[Temporary]` permanent in 315.2, and a "start of your Main Phase" cost is 316, so a body minted on turn N is dead before turn N+1's Main Phase — **[Temporary] fodder must be created and consumed inside the SAME Main Phase**, which is why `UNL-081 Keeper of Masks` (E0 from [Hidden], three bodies at once) is the only clean feed for `bottled-constellation-time-warp` and a Sprite engine is not. **A multiplier is worth nothing on an effect with no per-execution cap** (#160 batch 4): `SFD-117 Ancient Henge` and `SFD-083 Hextech Anomaly` ("Pay any amount") and `SFD-177 Azir, Sovereign` ("move any number of your token units") each already do the whole job in one instance, so they are dead under every readier, every copier and Heimerdinger — before pricing a doubler, check the effect has a cap at all. And 431.1.c against 431.1.a splits the draw half of `svellsongur-copy`: eight instances of `SFD-041 Apprentice Smith` (reveals, then draws only the gear it finds; non-gear to the bottom by 416.1.a) never Burn Out, eight of `SFD-027 Dunebreaker` are sixteen draws and an instant Burn Out that 431.2.b then pays for by recycling the trash.
- **The Duel's two-battlefield count is a SETUP parameter, not a cap, and `UNL-147 Baron Nashor` puts a THIRD battlefield on the board that nothing in the pool can remove (#187 batch 16, 2026-09-09).** The bullet above derives "two Conquers a turn" from 470 read with 485.4 — but 485.4 is *"Battlefield Count: 2"* sitting above 485.4.a's *"Each player provides three (3) Battlefields, included in their deck during deck building"*, i.e. what the board STARTS with — **and 483.4 says it outright, which nobody had cited (2026-09-12): *"Battlefield Count: Determines how many Battlefields are in play, CONTRIBUTED BY PLAYERS, during play."*** A battlefield token added by a CARD is not contributed by a player, so it sits outside the Count by the field`s own definition rather than by an argument from 485.4 against 485.4.a. This catalogue cites 485.4 **274 times** and had cited 483.1 through 483.8 **zero** times; 483.4.b is the general statement behind that pair (*"This may involve utilizing less Battlefields than there are players"*) and 483.6 — *"Format: Conditions to win or additional rules added over play"* — is the field 489.3`s Victory Score of 11 instantiates. While the two rules that actually pay read the board as it is: 470 (*"A player may only Score, from either method, once per Battlefield per turn"*) and 315.2.b.2 (*"The Turn Player Holds all Battlefields they Control"*). Baron Nashor prints *"As you play me, add the Baron Pit battlefield token to the board if it's not there already. If you do, I enter there"*; 187.9 defines that token in the rules themselves (*"a domainless battlefield token with `Units can move here from anywhere.`"*); and nothing removes it — 170.3 *"Battlefields cannot be Killed during the course of regular play"*, 170.4 *"Battlefields cannot be Moved"*, and 185.2.e's own example *"Token battlefields and legends can't be recycled"*. **So the per-turn Score ceiling on a Baron board is THREE, and the sentence above is true of a two-battlefield board only.** The price is printed in the same block and cuts the other way: 471.1.b.1 gives the Final Point only *"If the player has Scored every Battlefield this turn"* (otherwise *"that player draws a card instead"*), so a Conquer-based finisher on a Baron board must score all THREE, while 471.1.a.1 exempts every point that is not a Conquer — **a Hold deck pays nothing for the extra battlefield and a conquer CHAIN pays a great deal.**
- **A finisher earns its slot on a CONTESTED board, twelve of them never named the card that answers them, and a "class by identity" count answers a question you have to pick (#200 synthesis batch 1, 2026-09-12).** Three findings from the first lane that designed from a TARGET instead of from a card. (1) **The catalogue is not denominated in turns and that hides its own value.** The pool prints exactly TWO deckable units at Energy cost 1 or less out of 513 - `UNL-111 Determined Sentry` (Body, M1) and `VEN-043 Steel Paws` (Calm, M0) - so a mono-Body deck plays both on turn 1, Conquers both battlefields on turn 2 and Holds to eight on **turn 5 with no combo at all**, while every other identity is turn 6 because its cheapest bodies cost 2. Priced on the same rune curve, `tryndamere-brambleback-conquer` lands on **turn 7**, one turn SLOWER than doing nothing in its own identity. That is not an argument against the entry: unopposed, the Hold curve has already won, and a finisher is what turns ONE Conquer into nine points on a board where the Hold curve has been broken. No entry says this because no entry is priced in turns. (2) **718.5.b makes an attached Equipment a legal target every turn it sits there** (*"Attached cards still can be chosen or targeted by game effects while Attached"*) even though 718.2 makes its printed text Inactive and 721.2 its [Equip] unusable - and of the 29 BURST and CHAIN entries, **TWELVE stand on attached Equipment and not one named a gear-removal card**. Swept over text+effect of every deckable base: **16 printings, 15 names** (`OGN-022 Thermo Beam`, `OGN-056 Adaptatron`, `OGN-113 Malzahar Fanatic`, `OGN-179 Acceptable Losses`, `OGN-224 Salvage`, `SFD-005 Detonate`, `SFD-032 Disarming Rake`, `SFD-074 Pickpocket`, `SFD-077 Rocket Barrage`, `SFD-084` / `VEN-175 Jayce Man of Progress`, `SFD-160 Zaun Punk`, `VEN-003 Brittle Steel`, `VEN-067 Bottled Constellation`, `VEN-080 Noxian Demolitionist`, `VEN-131 Decree of Unity`), **every one of the six domains prints at least one**, so no identity hides from it, and **ZERO carry [Reaction]** - so no KILL reaches the Hold window (312.2.a + 813.1.c.1). **That sweep was built from a KILL predicate and is narrower than the question (corrected 2026-09-12):** `SFD-011 Angle Shot` (Fury, E2, **[Reaction]**) reads *"Choose a unit and an Equipment with the same controller. Attach that Equipment to that unit or detach that Equipment from that unit. Draw 1"*, and that shared controller need not be YOU — so at Reaction speed it answers an equipped body WITHOUT killing anything, stopping the Might Bonus (137.3.a) and taking the appended Effect Text off the carrier (718.3, 719.1). A kill-predicate can never see it. It is already catalogued, in `jax-angle-shot-attach-draw`, used on your OWN gear for draw; the enemy-facing use is in no entry. **State the predicate with the number**, and do not restate "no answer reaches the Hold window" until somebody walks whether a detach beats a Hold trigger already placed. Every other one of the sixteen and every one must be cast a full turn early, which is equally the window in which to defend it, since the removal spell on the Chain is a Closed State where 312.2.c hands out priority. Thermo Beam alone kills ALL gear at [Action] speed for E5 + 2 Fury and is already the payoff of `raging-firebrand-thermo-beam-free-gear-wipe`: **the catalogue owned the gun for months and had never pointed it at its own finishers.** (3) **"Entries per identity" is two different numbers and the manager published the wrong one.** Computed from the identity each entry's own `uses` IMPLY, chaos/fury looked like fifty entries with zero finishers; computed as *what a deck in that pair may RUN* (every card's domains a subset of the pair, which is what 103.1.b actually says), chaos/fury has a BURST and the genuinely empty cells were **body/chaos (120 runnable, 0 of the three classes)** and **body/order (151 runnable, 0)**. The lane corrected the brief, aimed at body/chaos and filled it the same day. Ask which of the two questions a cell count answers before calling it empty.
- **THE THREE FINISHER CLASSES DIVIDE BY WHAT THEY ARE FOR, AND 33 OF 45 PAY LATER THAN DOING NOTHING (#200 synthesis batch 3, 2026-09-12; reproduce with `npm run adversarial -- --turns`).** Denominate a line in TURNS and the catalogue splits in a way no entry states: **INFINITE 4 of 14, BURST 19 of 20, CHAIN 15 of 17 — 38 of 51 point-scoring finishers, re-derived 2026-09-12 at catalogue 763** (it read 33 of 45 when first measured; the ratio holds as the catalogue grows, which is the point, and the COUNT is perishable while the clock is not — re-derive it, never quote it) against the do-nothing Hold curve in the line's own identity. An INFINITE is a small cheap loop that arrives on turns 3-5 (`lux-infinite-power` is TURN 3 and six more are turn 4), so it beats the clock and changes what you do with the game; a BURST is a pile of expensive bodies on turns 6-8 and a CHAIN usually carries `OGN-122 Time Warp` at E10 + 4 Power a copy and runs turns 7-14 (`bottled-constellation-time-warp` is 60 Energy and 18 Power of deployment, i.e. **turn 14**). So a BURST's nine or seventeen points are REDUNDANT against an opponent who lets you hold two battlefields - eight were already arriving on turn 5 or 6 for free - and it earns its slot only where the Hold curve has STALLED and one scoring event must make up the whole deficit. That is the missing sentence in nearly every finisher, and it only became askable once something was priced in turns. **The model runs entirely in the finisher's favour** (perfect draws, nothing else ever cast, no interaction, domains ignored when paying Power, and the slower seat by 485.7), so 33 of 45 is a FLOOR, not a ceiling; its engine is that R runes afford R Energy AND R Power in one turn if you exhaust before recycling (164.2.a costs the exhaust, 164.2.b costs the recycle), each Power spent takes that rune off the board (161.2.b) and it returns at 2 a turn (315.3.b) capped at 12 (161.2.a). **The count is perishable and the clock is not**: it moved from 31 of 44 to 32 of 45 while the report was being written because another lane merged an entry, and then to **33 of 45** when the lane found that TEN OF THE FOURTEEN INFINITEs produce only FUEL (infinite-energy, infinite-power, token-body-engine) and no points at all, so timing the engine alone understated them by the entire cost of whatever consumes the fuel; the clock now folds the needs/produces DAG and adds the cheapest legal consumer, with 103.1.b capping the union of the two entries domains. It is ONE HOP and says so in its own comment - `generateVariants` in `src/combos.ts` is the real walker, so never quote a "no consumer" from the script as a structural claim. Re-derive it, never quote it. `scripts/adversarial-check.mjs` also sweeps its OWN answer sets out of `cards.json` rather than carrying a typed list, which is why it cannot go stale against a new set, and it reports **22 unanswered holes across all 71 finishers** - six of them entries standing on a Might-1 body that never name `OGN-133 Flurry of Blades`.
- **AN OPPONENT-GATED PAYOFF IS A CREDENTIAL, NOT A WEAKNESS, AND 29 OF THE 71 FINISHERS DIE WITH THE CURVE THEY EXIST TO RESCUE (#200 batch 5, 2026-09-12; `npm run adversarial -- --stalled`).** Classify every finisher by what the board has to look like for it to pay, from the printed text of its own `uses[]`: **ATTACK 7** (needs the Attacker designation, which 807.1.d with 323.9 makes impossible without an enemy garrison - dead on an empty board, alive on a contested one), **CONQUER 15**, **HOLD 46** (needs battlefields you ALREADY control, so **the stall that makes the line necessary is the stall that switches it off**), **INDEPENDENT 8** — **corrected 2026-09-12 from a first run that read CONQUER 27 / HOLD 29, which flattered the catalogue by seventeen entries.** The bug was PRECEDENCE, not pattern: the classifier tested attack, then conquer, then hold, and the conquer test fell back to the ENTRY`S OWN PROSE — where every Grand Plaza and Reckoner`s Arena line says *"conquer"*, because you conquer the battlefield to get it and the Arena`s text literally reads *"activate the conquer effects of units here"*. Of the 30 finishers whose payoff is a *"when you hold here"* battlefield, **17 were in the wrong bucket**. So **58% of all finishers die with the stall they exist to rescue, not 38%.** **The standing lesson is about classifiers, not about this one: a classifier that assigns ONE bucket to a MIXED object is wrong in whichever direction its precedence leans, so THE BUCKET ORDER IS THE THING TO AUDIT, not the patterns** — and the fix names both signals on a row that genuinely has two legs rather than flattening it, because silently flattening is the same error reversed. It surfaced the way the others did: a row said body/order`s one survivor was an entry whose own `terminatesIn` opens *"burst of ten points in ONE Hold at one battlefield"*, and **the count contradicted one sentence of card text**. Read with the turn clock, this inverts a finding this file already carries: chaos/fury`s point sources being opponent-gated was catalogued as the identity`s WEAKNESS, and it is the same property that makes a finisher earn its slot - `OGN-034 Tryndamere` needs an enemy garrison, which is precisely why he is worth a card, while a Hold line that pays only when unopposed is redundant with the free eight points the Hold curve was already delivering by turn 5 or 6. **Only SEVEN finishers have the shape worth designing for** (dead on an empty board, alive on a contested one), four of them Twilight Reveler loops whose *"When I attack, ready another friendly unit"* cannot fire on an empty battlefield - which this file records as a TRAP (*"entering an empty enemy battlefield is not an attack"*) and which is, read from the other side, a credential. Third confirmation of the Mind law in the same pass: of the 8 board-independent finishers, five are Mind engines on Renata Mastermind or Bottled Constellation and the rest are the two Gutter Palace ALT_WINs plus a fuel engine that scores nothing - **the board-independent finishers in this pool are the Mind ones plus the cards that literally say you win.** Both passes are ONE HOP and name the phrase they matched, so a reader refutes a row in one look.
- **BODY/ORDER CANNOT REACH EIGHT ON A BOARD IT DOES NOT ALREADY CONTROL, AND THAT IS A FACT ABOUT THE IDENTITY RATHER THAN A GAP SOMEBODY FAILED TO FILL (#200 batch 9, 2026-09-12).** Swept over every card whose text gains a point with domains inside {body, order}: Trinity Force, Power Nexus and Shen are Hold-only, `OGN-290 The Arena`s Greatest` is banned in both formats, the Brush is a token — and **`UNL-177 Ivern, Friend to All` is the identity`s ONLY non-Hold point source**, by 823.1.b making *"when I conquer or hold"* both kinds of effect. Ceiling: 3 Ivern (103.2.b caps by NAME and he has one printing) + 2 Conquer Scores (485.4`s Battlefield Count of 2 with 470`s once per battlefield per turn) = **FIVE**. The third battlefield that would lift it is the Baron Pit, and Baron Nashor is mono-Chaos. **The escape was checked rather than waved away**: conquering BOTH battlefields with the same three Iverns is 4 + 4 and is legal — a Conquer point is banked at the Score and 323.6 stripping Control later does not unbank it — but it dies on movement arithmetic, since Ivern has no [Ganking] and 144.4 makes each trip base → A → base → B, nine moves across three bodies at an exhaust each (144.2), i.e. six extra readies. The identity HAS readiers and they were run: `SFD-180 Fiora, Worthy` is its only ready with no exhaust in its cost, and **she can never ready an Ivern** — 709`s own second example is *"A Unit with Might 5 that gets +1 does not become Mighty, because it was already Mighty"*, and Ivern is printed **Might 6**, so he is Mighty on arrival and can never cross; `OGN-152 Mistfall` exhausts, so three copies are three readies against six needed, and 702.3 caps one buff per unit. Neither *"Ready your units"* card is in the identity. **The positive statement matters for how the site reads: body/order`s recovery from a stall is structurally an ENGINE and not a finisher** — conquer both for five this turn, hold both for eight next, with the opponent`s turn in between being exactly what disqualifies it as a CHAIN — so `ivern-arena-trinity-body-order-hold` is the RIGHT entry for that identity. Reopens the moment one new Body or Order card gains a point on a Conquer, an attack or a combat win.
- **BODY PRINTS EXACTLY ONE POINT CARD AND IT IS HOLD-GATED, WHICH BOUNDS EVERY BODY PAIR IN ADVANCE (#200 batch 12, 2026-09-12; re-measured by the manager).** Folded by name over text+effect of every card, **SIXTEEN cards in the pool gain a point or win the game**, and split by domain one column is nearly empty: **body — `SFD-115 Trinity Force` alone** (*"When I hold, score 1 point"*); calm — `OGN-066` (hold) and `VEN-046 Nasus`; chaos — `OGN-205 Yasuo` and `SFD-148 Draven`; fury — `OGN-034 Tryndamere`; order — `UNL-177 Ivern` and `VEN-138 Shen` (hold); mind — five; colourless — `OGN-293 The Grand Plaza` (hold), `SFD-214 Power Nexus` (hold) and `OGN-290`, **which is banned in both formats**. So **every LIVE colourless point card is Hold-gated too, and a Body deck`s non-Hold point budget is entirely its partner domain`s** — no colourless card can rescue it. **That retro-explains three separate results of this project and would have named each answer in advance**: body/calm`s partner budget is Nasus alone, and the Nasus BURST is what got built; body/chaos`s is Yasuo and Draven, and both got built; body/order`s is Ivern alone, and 103.2.b caps him at three copies against 485.4`s two battlefields, so 3 + 2 = 5 and it was refused. **Honest limit, and it is the interesting half: the NUMBER of partner sources does not predict the number of survivors** — body/fury has ONE source and four surviving finishers, because Tryndamere is heavily catalogued. The law bounds **which cards are eligible**, not how many entries exist. **Standing form: when an identity looks thin, sweep what its two halves can PAY WITH before sweeping what they can DO** — the point budget is at most six cards wide per domain and bounds the design space far harder than card count does.
- **THE BODY LAW CLOSES: EXACTLY ONE CARD IN THE POOL CONVERTS A HOLD EFFECT INTO A CONQUER EFFECT, AND IT IS FURY (#200 batch 13, 2026-09-12; re-measured — one hit in the corpus).** `SFD-030 Skyfall of Areion`, *"My hold effects are also conquer effects, and vice versa"*. So: Body prints one point card and it is Hold-gated; one card lifts a Hold effect onto a Conquer and it is Fury; **therefore body/fury is the ONLY Body pair in which Body`s own point card can pay on a board it does not already control**, and in every other Body pair every non-Hold point comes from the partner alone. The table confirms it without being arranged — body/fury has the most spanning stall-surviving finishers of any Body pair, and two of them are Trinity plus Skyfall exactly. This file had already noted Skyfall as unavailable to body/order and filed it as a fact about one entry; it is a fact about the whole Body half of the pool.
- **AVAILABILITY IS THE WRONG AXIS, AND EVERY TABLE IN THIS PROJECT INCLUDING THE MANAGER`S USED IT ANYWAY (#200 batch 13, 2026-09-12).** Count a pair`s finishers three ways — AVAILABLE (every card`s domains inside the pair), **SPANS BOTH** (the entry actually uses both domains), and spans-and-survives-a-stall. **A pair whose every finisher is mono-domain is not an identity with a plan; it is two solo domains sharing a legend.** Measured at 763 over INFINITE + BURST + CHAIN: **chaos/fury and chaos/mind span ZERO**; body/order and body/calm span one; mind/order spans 12 of its 17. **Availability alone calls body/mind the HEALTHIEST Body pair (8 available); on the honest axis it is among the emptiest** — its three spanning entries are ONE MECHANISM WRITTEN THREE TIMES (Trinity Force multiplied by Blue Sentinel, on a Hold) and all five of its stall-surviving finishers are mono-MIND, four of them board-INDEPENDENT. That is also *why*: a board-independent plan needs nothing from the board, so there is no support role for Body to fill, and Body has no non-Hold points to offer instead. The contrast that proves it is body/chaos, where the spanning entry works precisely because its points are COMBAT-gated — a board state Body can help with.
- **A LINE THAT STALLS AT SEVEN IS NOT REFUSED UNTIL YOU HAVE LOOKED FOR A NON-CONQUER EIGHTH POINT, AND TWO HALVES CAN COVER OPPOSITE BOARDS (#200 batch 3c, 2026-09-12).** 471.1.b.1 gives the Final Point only to a player who *"has Scored every Battlefield this turn"* and otherwise hands them a card, which is why this file records that **a CONQUER engine is exactly the kind that can stall at seven**. The converse is 471.1.a.1 — *"points Gained from sources that are not Conquer are not beholden to these restrictions"* — so **an ABILITY GAIN as the eighth point never meets the clause at all.** **SWEPT 2026-09-12 (#200 batch 5), and the manager`s list of three was one too many.** Of the three named — Trinity Force + Skyfall + Reckoner`s Arena REOPENS and is now `trinity-skyfall-arena-second-battlefield-chain`; the Body/Order Shen board STAYS refused for a reason the rule does not reach (the eighth point IS a non-Conquer ability Gain, but adding Ivern puts a third body at the battlefield and 383.2.a.1 makes Shen`s *"exactly one other unit you control here"* part of the Trigger Condition, so it is a BOARD constraint); and **Karthus over Machine Evangel and Viktor, Leader was never a refusal at all — its "seven" is a count of RECRUIT BODIES for a Grand Plaza line, not a point total.** 471.1.a.1 and 471.1.b.1 are about POINTS and a body count is untouched by either. **STATE THE UNIT WITH THE NUMBER**: inside one sweep, "seven" meant points twice and bodies once. **And the reopening`s general form is worth more than the line: a Hold-based seven at ONE battlefield in a Duel is an EIGHT**, because Holding one battlefield and Conquering the other IS Scoring every battlefield for 471.1.b.1 — `swain-double-conquer` has said so in its own words since 2026-09-04 and nobody applied it here. The refusal shape to distrust is a walk that hunts its eighth point INSIDE THE SAME SCORING EVENT it built: *"one short"* is a claim about that event, not about the turn. **Check that before refusing a seven, and prefer a line that never has to pass 471.1.b.1 over one that would pass it — the second is one clause more fragile.** The structural half is the design idea that produced `ivern-arena-draven-chaos-order-chain` and it answers the stalled-board problem directly: **a Hold-gated half and a combat-gated half want OPPOSITE boards**, since 315.2.b.2 pays only on battlefields you already control while 323.9 stages a combat only where opposing units meet, so neither half covers both board states and TOGETHER THEY DO. That line uses both PHASES of one turn — seven in the Beginning Phase, an eighth from a combat win in the Main Phase, a ninth from the Conquer — rather than more bodies, which is what every earlier attempt in that identity tried; Chaos/Order owns exactly TWO scoring cards (Ivern, Order; Draven, Chaos) and every combination of bodies lands on seven.
- **ANY "YOU MAY PAY X TO Y" TRIGGER IS PAYABLE OUT OF RUNES AT FINALIZATION, IN EVERY IDENTITY, WITH NO ADD-ON-TRIGGER CARD — AND A RECORDED REFUSAL SAID OTHERWISE (#200 batch 4, 2026-09-12; the lane refuted its OWN batch-1 refusal).** The refusal ran: 383.3.b makes the cost a BASE COST paid at finalization, 312.2.c withholds priority until every pending chain item finishes finalizing, 167 empties the Rune Pool at end of turn — therefore the Power must be banked, the only funder that Adds on a Hold is Blue Sentinel, and the Sentinel is Mind, so `SFD-214 Power Nexus` is dead outside Mind. **Every step is true and the inference is false, because priority is not payment.** 444.2.c: *"Players may activate abilities that Add resources with the Reaction keyword at any time that they are instructed to Pay resources. Those abilities finalize and resolve immediately, ignoring normal restrictions."* The instruction to pay IS the window. Three paragraphs finish it, all verified at source: **164.2** gives every Basic Rune TWO abilities and BOTH carry [Reaction] (`[E]: [Reaction] — Add [1]` and `Recycle this: [Reaction] — Add [C]`), so 444.2.c reaches them; **164.2.b`s cost is the RECYCLE, not an exhaust**, so a rune already tapped for Energy still pays; **164.2.b.1** Adds Power of that rune`s DOMAIN, which **135.2.e.5.a** makes legal against a rainbow cost — *"When required as a cost, [A] can be paid by Power of any Domain."* Price: four runes a point, 161.2.b returns them to the Rune Deck, 161.2.a caps at twelve and 315.3.b channels two a turn, so **one extra point every two turns at zero card cost, in any identity.** All five pre-existing Power Nexus entries fund it with a Mind card and none cited 444.2.c; they are optimisations of a line that runs without them, and all five were upgraded in the same commit. **The class matters more than the card: before refusing a line because its Power cannot be banked across 167, check whether the runes are simply still on the board.** Nobody has swept for how many recorded refusals that reopens — it is the same shape as the seven-stall class, and this catalogue`s refusals appear to fail in CLASSES rather than one at a time.
- **167 EMPTIES THE POOL, NOT THE BOARD — and this file carried both halves of that in different bullets and never joined them (#200 batch 5, 2026-09-12).** One bullet says *"Energy added in Awaken or the Beginning Phase is lost when the Rune Pool empties at the Main Phase start (167)"*; another says 167 reads *"the POOL, not the runes"*. Put together: **the error shape is assuming a resource must be ADDED when it becomes available rather than when it is SPENT.** Two refusals fail on it, and neither needs the exotic 444.2.c route — `OGN-288 Startipped Peak` and `SFD-219 The Papertree` were both refused because the rune they channel arrives EXHAUSTED (so 164.2.a is dead) and any Power it could add in the Beginning Phase is emptied by 167. Both true, and irrelevant: **164.2.b`s cost is the RECYCLE, not an exhaust**, so the exhausted rune recycles for 1 Power — and you do it in the MAIN PHASE, at the moment you spend, because the rune is sitting on the BOARD, which 167 never touches. The Peak`s rune is **1 Power this turn OR 1 Energy next turn, your choice** — not *"one extra rune, as Energy, from the following turn"*. Both had already been reopened once by #170 on a rune-COUNT reading; this is a second, independent reading of the mana half that the count reading explicitly left standing, so a refusal can be wrong twice over in two different ways. **A refusal in this catalogue fails in CLASSES rather than one at a time**: 48 candidates swept from 19,362 records, four reopenings, and not one of them was findable by any validator — the only reader who can catch a wrong refusal is somebody re-reading the reason, because a wrong ENTRY has arithmetic and a card set and three cross-audits, while a wrong refusal leaves nothing behind at all.
- **PLAYING A CARD, STANDARD-MOVING AND HIDING ARE ONE CLASS WITH ONE TIMING RULE, AND THIS CATALOGUE CITES 312.2.a EIGHTY-ONE TIMES WITHOUT EVER CITING THE PARAGRAPH THAT SAYS WHAT PRIORITY IS FOR (#187 batch 5, 2026-09-12).** Measured: 312.1, 312.1.a, 312.1.b, 312.1.b.1, 419.1, 419.1.a, 419.2, 419.2.a, 410.1, 410.1.a, 410.1.b, 410.1.b.2, 410.1.b.3, 410.2.b and 316.5.a are cited ZERO times, and 420.3 three times against 420.3.a`s 200. The chain every per-card derivation here is shorthand for: **419.2** says of playing a card *"This is a Discretionary Action"*, **420.3** says the Standard Move is one and **421.2** says Hiding is one; **410.1.a** — *"A Discretionary Action is a Game Action that may be performed at any time during a player`s turn during a Neutral Open State"*; **312.1** — *"Priority is the singular exclusive right to take Discretionary Actions."* So [Action], [Reaction] and [Ambush] are exceptions written against **410.1.a**, not against 312.2.a. **And the escape clause is the general licence this project has been deriving card by card: 312.1.b.1 — *"Players can always take and make choices for Limited Actions when instructed, regardless of Priority"*** — which with 410.2.a/410.2.b is why every *"play it, ignoring its cost"* effect in the pool works. Same shape as 444.2.c one bullet up, and the same sentence covers both: **priority governs what you may do AT WILL and never what you are INSTRUCTED to do.**
- **A UNIT CANNOT BE COUNTERED, BY CONSTRUCTION, AND THE POOL`S COUNTER AXIS IS ONE CARD WIDE (#187 batch 15, 2026-09-12).** 359.3.c scopes the pre-resolution window to SPELLS by its own words, and 359.2 — *"A Permanent leaves the Chain and becomes a Game Object"* — is why a permanent never has one. **NARROWED 2026-09-12 by the 401-406 block, and the narrowing is the better statement: ABILITIES get the window too.** 406.2 makes an Ability a Finalized Chain Item exactly as 359.3.a does a spell, so **the object with no pre-resolution window is specifically **a UNIT, A GEAR, OR AN ABILITY THAT ADDS RESOURCES** — **corrected again 2026-09-12 by 337.2, which is ONE RULE saying all three and is cited by NOTHING**: *"If, after finalizing the Chain Item, that item is a Unit, Gear, or an ability that Adds resources, it resolves immediately—Move to Step 4: Resolve."* This project derived two of those three SEPARATELY — the Add third from 429.2 / 429.2.a (cited 77 and 28 times, and the basis of the 444.2.c reopening) and the unit third from 359.2 (41 times) — for 118 citations across two unrelated arguments where one sentence covers all of it. **What 337.2 adds beyond tidiness is the REASON: those three SKIP Step 2 Execute and Step 3 Pass and go straight to Step 4 Resolve, and Step 2 is where 338.1 hands the player with Priority the right to respond** — nobody is ever offered a window, because the process never reaches the step at which windows exist. The corollary is usable and cuts both ways: **anything true of one third is true of the other two — an Add cannot be countered for exactly the reason a unit cannot** — and the Seal cycle`s printed reminder *"Abilities that add resources can`t be reacted to"* is restating 337.2, not 429.2** — which is precisely why the eleven counters split nine *"a spell"* to two *"spell or ability"* and none names a unit. 401.1`s tail is the companion: an ability`s chain item has no card representing it and still creates a Closed State, **which is why an ETB trigger hands the opponent the window its own body never did**; with 329.2, 312.2.c and 330, **playing a unit with no triggered ability gives the opponent no priority at all**, the Chain empties and 310.1 hands priority back to the turn player. That is the denial lens from the other side: #100 says the opponent`s only windows are Closed States and Showdowns, and 359.2 is the paragraph saying a unit play creates neither. Swept over `cards.json` folded by name: **ELEVEN counter printings, all carrying [Reaction]** — Defy, Wind Wall, Not So Fast, Hard Bargain, Riposte, Flurry of Feathers, Repulse, Abandon, Lilting Lullaby, Crumbling Sands, Rebuttal. **NINE say *"a spell"*; exactly TWO say *"spell or ability"* (`SFD-045 Not So Fast`, `UNL-106 Repulse`) and those two are the only cards in the pool that can answer an ETB trigger; ZERO name a unit or a permanent.** So an **ETB-removal BODY is strictly harder to answer than a removal SPELL**, and the gap is nine cards to two — #200 priced the gear-removal answers to the twelve Equipment finishers and nobody has priced this axis. Predicate warning attached, because it bit on the way in: a `spell or ability` sweep catches `UNL-044 Flurry of Feathers` on its [Reaction] REMINDER text (*"Play any time, even before spells and abilities resolve"*) while the card`s actual mode reads *"Counter a spell"*.
- **A TRIGGER HAS THREE GATES WITH THREE DIFFERENT RULES, AND THE WHOLE ABILITY-PLAY PROCESS WAS UNCITED (#187 batch 21, 2026-09-12).** Rules 401-406 mirror the card process 349-359 step for step, and 402.3, 402.4, 402.4.a, 402.4.b, 403.1.b, 403.1.b.1, 404.1, 404.2, 404.2.a, 406.3, 406.4 and 406.5 are cited **zero times each** — every ability finding in this project has been derived from the CARD process or from 383. The asymmetry it buys: **you may refuse to PAY for a trigger and you may not refuse to CHOOSE for it.** 402.4.b — *"If there are legal options to choose, the ability`s controller must choose them. They may not decline this stage of playing a Trigger"*; 404.2 — *"players may decline to pay for Triggered Abilities that have incurred a cost… It never becomes a Finalized Chain Item."* With 383.3.e.2.a that is three gates: a leading *"you may"* is declinable and decided BLIND at finalization; the CHOICE stage is NOT declinable; the COST stage is. **So a mandatory downside trigger cannot be dodged by choosing nothing, and any trigger with a cost can be walked away from.** Both removals are marked in identical words as **not a counter** (402.4.a, 404.2.a), so a declined trigger is a THIRD category that nothing watching Countering can see — which matters because 425.1.b makes a countered card not-played and 425.1.c refunds nothing. Two companions, also uncited: **403.1.b.1** is the rules` THIRD statement of the *"pay X to Y is the base cost"* fact this file cites through 383.3.b and 204.3.a; and **402.3** is the activated mirror of the #166 filler lesson — an ACTIVATED ability with no legal option is not activatable at all, where a TRIGGERED one goes on the chain and is then removed by 402.4.
- **FINALIZE OLDEST-FIRST, RESOLVE NEWEST-FIRST — AND THE FIRST HALF IS WHY A RESOURCE FROM ONE TRIGGER CAN NEVER FUND A BASE COST IN ANOTHER (#187 batch 23, 2026-09-12).** The frame is HOT FEPR (Handle Outstanding Tasks, Finalize, Execute, Pass, Resolve) and this catalogue has reasoned inside it for months without naming it — 334, 334.1, 334.2, 334.2.a, 335.1, 337.1.a, 337.2, 337.3, 340.2 and 340.3 are all uncited. Two lines carry the weight: **337.1.b**, *"Chain Items are Finalized in the order they were appended to the Chain"* (cited twice), against **340.1**, the NEWEST finalized item resolves (cited 94 times). **This file records three times, always from 383.3.b, that a resource produced by one trigger of an event cannot pay a BASE COST in another and CAN satisfy a 205 game action in another — 337.1.b is why**: base costs are paid at FINALIZATION, which runs oldest-first, while resolution runs newest-first, so the paying happens in placement order and the spending in reverse. **337.1.a is the one-sentence source for the 312.2.c this project quotes constantly** — finalizing an item to the chain does not pass Priority: 312.2.c says when priority arrives, 337.1.a says why it does not arrive earlier. And **335.1 is the Showdown half of the priority rule this catalogue holds only in its Main-Phase form** (335 is cited 14 times, 335.1 zero): *"If there are no Outstanding Tasks, no pending Chain Items, and there is an ongoing Showdown, the player with Focus receives priority"* — which with 345 and 464.2.c.1 completes the chain saying the ATTACKER acts first inside a combat showdown, a conclusion this file states from 345 alone.
- **THE NON-TURN PLAYER`S SIMULTANEOUS TRIGGERS RESOLVE FIRST, AND THIS FILE HAS ONLY THE COMBAT-SCOPED SPECIAL CASE (#187 batch 31, 2026-09-12).** 383.3.d is cited 75 times — it orders YOUR OWN simultaneous triggers — and **383.3.d.1 is cited ZERO**: *"If multiple players separately control Triggered Abilities that are Triggered simultaneously, then starting with the Turn Player and proceeding in Turn Order, each player orders their Triggered Abilities on the Chain."* With 340.1 resolving newest-first, **the non-turn player`s go first**. This file records exactly one instance of that, scoped to combat through 464.2.e.1; 383.3.d.1 is the general rule it is a special case of, and it reaches **every shared event** — a board wipe, a symmetric spell like `OGN-209 Cull the Weak` or `OGN-179 Acceptable Losses`, any Cleanup that kills across the table. Read with 303.2.a, **Turn Order starting with the Turn Player is the universal tiebreak** — for triggers by placement and for actions by execution — **and 340.1 inverts it on resolution.**
- **383.4.c.2.b IS THE POSITIVE HALF OF A STORY THIS FILE ONLY HAS THE NEGATIVE OF (#187 batch 33, 2026-09-12).** Cited ZERO, while its Hold twin 383.4.d.2.b has ten and its Unit sibling 383.4.c.2.a forty-two. 383.4.c.2 names two halves — a Conquer Effect triggers from units that were PRESENT during the Conquer, **or from abilities that reference the PLAYER who performed it** — and .2.b is the second. The first needs a body at the battlefield; **the second needs only the player, which is the whole reason a LEGEND can carry *"when you conquer"* despite 107.4.b putting the Legend Zone outside every location.** Five legends print a conquer clause (`OGS-023`, `SFD-187`, `UNL-187`, `UNL-195`, `UNL-199`). This file records the NEGATIVE half from #47 — a legend is not at a location, so Reckoner`s Arena`s *"here"* and Svellsongur`s attach never reach a legend`s own conquer text, and the legend-plus-Arena family is dead on arrival. **Stated together they are ONE fact rather than two: the legend`s trigger fires because it references the PLAYER, and it cannot be doubled by anything keyed to a LOCATION because it was never at one.** Companion: 383.4.c.3 and 383.4.d.3, both uncited, are the sentences that NAME *"Conquer Effects"* and *"Hold Effects"* — terms this project uses in hundreds of places and which `SFD-030 Skyfall of Areion` prints on the card, so that card is reading a DEFINED TERM.
- **"IS NOT A SUBSET OF" APPEARS EXACTLY FIVE TIMES IN THE CORE RULES; THIS PROJECT CITES ONE OF THEM 37 TIMES AND THE OTHER FOUR NEVER (#187 batch 35, 2026-09-12).** The cited one is 811.1.c.1, *"Hide is not a subset of Play"* — a workhorse here: it settles R33, it is why `OGN-070 Mageseeker Warden` and the Noxus Saboteur stop the PLAY and not the HIDE, and it is why `OGN-107 Ava Achiever` escapes 811.1.d.2. The four uncited siblings each answer a question this file currently answers by argument. **427.2.a, *"Banish is not a subset of Kill"*, is the one with a grid behind it**: this file handles banish-instead-of-death through 808.1.d.1, and 427.2.a covers the commoner case — a plain banish of a LIVING unit, where no death was ever in prospect. Swept, five cards banish a friendly unit (`OGN-102 Portal Rescue`, `SFD-200 Arcane Shift`, `UNL-184 Thrill of the Hunt`, `VEN-066 Temporal Breach`, `VEN-143 Master of Shadows`) and **four of the five banish and then REPLAY, so at the table they read exactly like a sacrifice outlet — and they feed NONE of the pool`s four death-watchers** (`OGN-228 Vanguard Helm`, `SFD-169 Altar of Memories`, `UNL-068 Spectral Centaur`, `UNL-129 Vicious Snapjaws`). That is a four-by-five EXCLUDES grid for the synergy layer, and it is the natural first pairing anyone would try in Mind or Fury/Chaos. **427.2.b, *"Banish is not a subset of Discard"*, takes a card out of the discard lens** — `UNL-169 Ashe, Focused` removes a card from a hand and is NOT one of the 31 outlets, and the two verbs sit next to each other on ONE card, since `VEN-110 Mel`s Empower cost is a real DISCARD while her payoff BANISHES. **428.2.a is the general form of two things derived separately here** (*"it is only considered Killed if its origin was any zone ON THE BOARD"*) — it covers a token that has already left the board AND a body saved by a recall, and it is also why an ATTACHED Equipment killed by `OGN-224 Salvage` IS killed and does fire its Deathknell, since 718.5.b keeps it a legal target on the board; 428.2.b then closes the move question. **438.4, *"Replacing is not a subset of Banishing"*, is the categorical statement behind the 438.5.a this file already uses**, and is what stops 108.6.c`s no-return rule riding along; 438.6 is 186.1 arriving from the Replace side. **The reusable move is the sweep itself: when a phrase turns out to be load-bearing, grep the rules for the PHRASE and see how many members its family has.**
- **A MOVE-BASED SAVE AND A BOUNCE ARE NOT INTERCHANGEABLE, BECAUSE VALIDITY IS RE-CHECKED AT RESOLUTION (#187 batch 31, 2026-09-12).** 359.3.e.3, uncited under a block cited 155 times: *"If a target ceases to meet the targeting requirements while the spell is on the chain, then meets them again, it`s a legal target"* — Riot`s example being a unit moved to base and back. So a **MOVE** save (`OGS-011 Flash` at Reaction speed, `SFD-043 Emperor`s Divide`) only works **if the body stays away until resolution**, while a **BOUNCE** is permanent by the very next clause, 359.3.e.4, since a target that leaves for a Non-Board Zone and returns is not the same object. **That is the same identity rule this file already uses from the other side**, where a bounce ENDS a delayed would-die shield (390.5.a): the property that costs you a shield is the property that makes a bounce the better dodge. Companion from the same batch: 456 is cited 81 times and **456.2 — the only affirmative sentence in a block of three negatives — is cited zero**: *"A Recall causes a Permanent to change locations."* This file sources its recall-shield finding to 455, which says WHERE a Recall goes; 456.2 says **the location CHANGES**, which is the half a *"while I`m at a battlefield"* clause actually reads.
- **THE CLEANUP BLOCK IS THE LOPSIDED CITATION IN THIS PROJECT: 323 IS CITED 857 TIMES AND THE PARAGRAPHS SAYING WHEN A CLEANUP HAPPENS, WHAT YOU MAY DO IN ONE AND HOW IT ENDS ARE ZERO (#187 batch 24, 2026-09-12).** 318, 319.2, 319.4, 319.7, 320.1, 322 and 322.1 are all uncited. Three consequences. **(1) 320.1 is the one sentence for "no priority inside a Cleanup"** — *"New Pending Items can be added, but Finalized Items cannot be executed and Priority and Focus are not passed or awarded"* — where this file assembles that conclusion from 312.2, 453 and 323.12, none of which is about Cleanups. It also supplies the half those three do not: **new Pending Items CAN be added**, so a Cleanup is not a frozen window — triggers pile onto the Chain throughout it — it is a window in which nobody is ever OFFERED the chance to respond. Same shape as 337.2: the items exist and the step that hands out priority is never reached. The escape is the one already recorded: **444.2.c is not priority**, so a rune still pays into a finalization inside a Cleanup. **(2) 319 is the complete list of the eight times a Cleanup becomes an Outstanding Task, and 319.7 — *"After the status of any number of Game Objects changes for any reason"* — is the WIDEST and is cited ZERO** against 319.6`s 79 and 319.8`s 23. Exhausting, readying, stunning, Empowering and applying Contested under 190.3.a.1 all change something with no zone change and no move — **and calling them all "status changes" is WIDER THAN THE TEXT LICENSES, corrected 2026-09-12: the rules do not use "status" as a closed term of art.** 414.2 and 415.2 call Exhausted and Ready **STATES**; 441.2 calls Empowered a state and 442.1 calls it a status two paragraphs later; 719.4 sets the two words against each other in one sentence; and 719.4.a counts LOCATION, ATTACHED and TOP-MOST among the statuses, which is very wide. All five of those paragraphs are cited zero. The CONCLUSION survives — 319.7 is still the widest of the eight, and a Cleanup arrives for an exhaust anyway through 319.1, 319.3, 319.4 and 319.6 — but so 319.7 is the only entry in the list that covers them** — which matters wherever an argument here turns on a Cleanup arriving, the evacuation family included. **(3) 322 with 322.1 is the CLEANUP LOOP** — a qualifying event during a Cleanup causes another immediately after it completes, repeating until one occurs with no new change in state — and **the 324.2 this file already carries is the SPECIAL case of it**. Its companion in the other direction is 321 / 321.1: a Cleanup cannot interrupt a resolving Chain Item and queues instead. The two fence each other, and together they are why 370.1.a.2`s simultaneity of EVENTS and 303.2`s one-action-at-a-time can both be true.
- **323.1 IS TASK 1 OF EVERY CLEANUP AND IS CITED ZERO TIMES, WHICH MEANS EVERY FINISHER IN THIS CATALOGUE RELIES ON IT WITHOUT SAYING SO (#187 batch 30, 2026-09-12).** Rule 323 is cited 857 times and 323.6 alone 277, and the FIRST task in its list had never been opened: *"1. If a player has points greater than or equal to the Victory Score, and more points than any opponent, that player wins."* **The win check runs BEFORE everything else in the Cleanup** — 323.2 designations, 323.3 board state, 323.4 Deathknells, 323.5 bodies to the trash, 323.6 losing control of a battlefield your last body left, 323.9 marking a Combat Staged, 323.11 through 323.13 are ALL numbered after it. So **once the points are on the board, nothing later in that Cleanup can take the win away**: a BURST that empties its own board wins before 323.4 and 323.5 process a single death (`bottled-constellation-time-warp` kills three friendly bodies for its eighth point and wins before those corpses are handled); an evacuation line wins before 323.6 could strip the battlefield; a line that leaves a body in front of an enemy garrison wins before 323.9 could stage the Combat. **None of the 26 ALT_WINs or 20 BURSTs says this and all of them rely on it.** 323.1 also states 194.2`s TWO-PART test verbatim in the place it is actually applied — the test whose shorthand this file already had to correct once — with 194.2 cited 26 times, 194.2.b once, and 323.1 never.
- **AND A SPECIAL CLEANUP HAS THE SAME TASK 1, WHICH THE WIN-TIMING CLAIM ASSERTED AND DID NOT CITE (#187 batch 34, 2026-09-12, self-audit).** A reader immediately asks whether the Combat Cleanup or the End of Turn Cleanup share 323.1`s first step. **324 and 324.1, both cited zero, are the answer**: *"Special Cleanups are Cleanup steps invoked at specific times that have ADDITIONAL STEPS not present in a normal Cleanup"*, and *"the unique steps added will be inserted and defined by the sub-section that invokes it"* — which is why 466.1.a.1 and 317.2.b both read *"Insert 3c. Heal all Units"*. **They add to a list that already has 323.1 at the top**, so the win check is Task 1 of every Cleanup including the special ones. **A NEW STANDING CHECK, beside "does this rule describe an instant or a loop": DID I USE A WORD THE RULES DEFINE DIFFERENTLY?** The same self-audit caught its own *"status change"* gloss being wider than the text licenses, and neither question is visible to a reference checker, a quote checker, or a reviewer reading for truth — only to the author asking them.
- **THE EVACUATION ARGUMENT IS A THREE-SENTENCE ARGUMENT AND THIS FILE HAS THE MIDDLE ONE (#187 batch 30, 2026-09-12).** The #58 third route stands on 323.10 alone (cited 21 times), and its two halves are uncited: **323.9.a** — *"The Combat remains Staged at that Battlefield as long as there are Units present from two opposing players there"* — is the PRECONDITION, i.e. staging persists exactly while both sides are present, so removing one side is what un-stages it; and **461.2** — *"If Staged Combats stop being Staged before the Steps of Combat are initiated, they are not resolved or executed"* — is the CONSEQUENCE. **461.1** (cited 14) is the fourth: with several staged Combats the Turn Player picks the order, which is what lets an evacuation be timed against one of them. **Method note on the probe that found all of this**: the orphan-neighbour sweep (an uncited heading whose parent or best sibling is cited 15+) reports 158 rows in 300-499 and its yield is HIGH while its precision is LOW — the top of the list is procedural scaffolding correctly left uncited (*"the following Task becomes Outstanding"* lines, format parameters beside 485.4). **Read the text, never the rank.**
- **A BLOODLESS CONQUER CAN BE TURNED INTO A FIGHT, AND 46 OF THE 47 ENTRIES THAT RELY ON ONE DID NOT SAY SO (#187 batch 25, 2026-09-12; all 47 now carry the caveat).** 344.2 is cited by 47 entries and **316.8.b.1.a by exactly one** — the entry built to EXPLOIT the conversion rather than to defend against it. That paragraph says units controlled by a different player *"may become present at the Battlefield where the Showdown is"* during a Non-Combat Showdown, converting it to a Combat Showdown in the following cleanup. **The answer set is THREE cards**, swept on the ACTOR rather than on the destination: `OGN-173 Ride the Wind` (Chaos), `OGN-262 Zenith Blade` (Calm/Order), `SFD-184 Relentless Pursuit` (Fury/Body) — twelve printings move a friendly unit and nine send it to base or need a body already there.
