They span three identities, so **no identity is safe**, and Zenith Blade is the worst to walk into because it STUNS the body on the way in (423.1.b removes its damage contribution, 423.1.c keeps its full Might as the price to kill it). **[Ambush] is not a fourth answer** — 822.1.b needs a battlefield where the player already has units, which is exactly what that board lacks. ****The remedy is a COUNTERSPELL, and a first version of this bullet said something false that is withdrawn here (corrected within the hour, 2026-09-12).** It said that because all three answers are [Action] and 358.4 admits an Action card only as the card that CLOSES the state, the attacker — holding Focus by 345 and priority by 335.1 — could shut them out by playing any cheap Action card first. Every step is true and the conclusion is false, because **a Showdown is a LOOP, not a single window**: 346 (*"When the last item on the chain resolves and the turn returns to an Open State during a Showdown, Focus passes, and the next Player gains both Focus and Priority"*), with 347.1.b and 347.2.a. Closing the state buys a lock-out lasting exactly that CHAIN; when it resolves the Showdown reopens and Focus passes TO THE DEFENDER. **The real sequence is: walk in, take Focus, PASS; they play the answer, which closes the state; you COUNTER it, since 358.4`s other half then admits only [Reaction] and all eleven counters in the pool are Reaction, with 425.1.c refunding them nothing; everyone passes in sequence, 347.2.a ends the Showdown, and 348.2.a gives you Control and the Conquer.** The cheap counter is `OGN-045 Defy` (Calm, E1 + 1 Power) which covers all three answers, or `OGN-064 Wind Wall` unconditionally — **so the protection narrows to pairs containing CALM, not to any deck for one Energy.** **The failure shape is the MIRROR of the true-but-incomplete class**: here every citation was complete and correct and was reasoned ONE STEP PAST WHAT IT LICENSES — 358.4 describes an INSTANT and was used to answer a question about the rest of the Showdown, which is 346 and 347`s subject. The check is one question: **does this rule describe an INSTANT or a LOOP?** — and 347 is literally headed *"During a Showdown, the player with Focus may do one of the following"*. Measured beside it: 340.2.a and 347.1.a are cited ZERO and 347.2.a twice, against 348`s 381 and 345`s 73 — **this catalogue holds the paragraph that OPENS a Showdown and the one that CLOSES it and almost nothing about the loop in between**, which is presumably why this was available to get wrong. **Method note, and it is a new member of the predicate family: SWEEP ON WHO PERFORMS THE ACTION, NOT ON THE SHAPE OF THE INSTRUCTION.** The first sweep required *"move … to <destination>"* and returned 45 rows that did NOT include Ride the Wind — the card the one existing entry is built on — because its text is *"Move a friendly unit and ready it"* with no destination clause at all. **A move by effect names no destination precisely when it is LEAST restricted, so a destination-shaped regex is blindest exactly where the answer lives.**
- **A SHOWDOWN RUNS TWO NESTED LOOPS, AND COLLAPSING THEM IS HOW THE WITHDRAWN REMEDY ABOVE WENT WRONG (#187 batch 27, 2026-09-12).** The **inner** loop is PRIORITY — 338 Execute, 339 Pass, 340 Resolve — and **passing priority inside it KEEPS your Focus** (313.3). The **outer** loop is FOCUS, and it turns over only when a Chain closes (346, 347.1.b) or when the holder declines to act (347.2.b). Three of the four paragraphs stating this are uncited: **313.2** (a player who gains Focus also gains Priority), **313.4** — *"A player may not make discretionary actions with Focus unless they also possess Priority"*, which is the teeth — and **313.5** (in a Neutral State nobody has Focus), which is why 312.2.a`s Main-Phase priority and 335.1`s Showdown priority are **two separate grants** rather than one. **And the four words that would have prevented the error sit TWO PAGES EARLIER than the paragraph it was reasoned from**: 342, cited twice — *"A Showdown is a Window of Opportunity in which Players have an Open State in which they may play Spells IN AN ALTERNATING FASHION"* — with 342.1 (*"Each spell played this way creates a Chain as normal"*), uncited. **Finish a block before skipping to a new one; the sentence that refutes you is as likely to be behind you as ahead.** Two more from the same close-out: **343.2** — *"The turn is said to be in a Neutral State if no Showdown or Combat is in progress"*, uncited — is the definition that makes 344.2`s precondition readable, on a phrase whose parent is cited 177 times and whose second half nobody had looked up; and **340.2.a with 346.1 are the same exception written twice** — Focus does NOT pass when the chain was opened by a triggered ability or an Add — which is 337.2 appearing a THIRD time, now from the Focus side, with Riot`s own example noting the Combat Chain opens from triggered abilities. **Registered, not erratized:** 342 says players may play SPELLS while both operative paragraphs are wider — 316.8.c admits cards AND activated abilities (cited 52 times) and 347.1 says *"Play a Card or Activated Ability that is legally timed"* (22) — so 342 is summary prose and the permissions govern; same treatment as the 383.1.b / OGN-118 mismatch and the 763.1 comma.
- **A SPLIT IS N CHOICES AND PAYS THE [DEFLECT] TAX ONCE PER BODY, AND BOTH SPLITS IN THE POOL WERE PRICED WITHOUT IT (#187 batch 28, 2026-09-12).** 355.14.a — *"If a card specifies that an amount of damage may be split among some number of Units, then each Unit chosen is Targeted"* — with 355.14.d, *"Each Target is valid, and contributes to Targeting Effects individually"*, cited once and zero against 355.14.h`s sixteen. 809.1.c charges Power *"for each time they choose"*, **47 printings carry Deflect, and 187.7 gives it to a Bird token BY RULE** — so `UNL-044 Flurry of Feathers`, four Birds at [Reaction] speed, taxes an incoming split **four rainbow**. The pool prints exactly two splits (`UNL-192 Alpha Strike`, Riot`s own worked example, and `OGN-041 Volibear, Furious`, who carries Deflect 2 himself) and 355.14.c lets you take FEWER targets, so the mitigation is to spread less. **It reaches the trigger, not only the spell**, and batch 21`s asymmetry then bites a real card: the tax is paid at step 404, where 404.2 permits DECLINING — and declining removes the whole trigger, so against a Bird garrison Volibear`s attack trigger is pay-per-body or nothing. **Companion, uncited and the mirror case: 323.2.b** — a unit whose CONTROL changes mid-combat loses its designation and takes its new controller`s at the next Cleanup — is cited by nothing while 323.2 and 323.2.a carry 66 each, and of the four cards that steal an enemy unit **three RECALL it**, so `SFD-202 Hostile Takeover` is the only one that leaves the body where the rule can reach it: stolen mid-combat, 465.2.c then sums its Might on YOUR side instead of theirs, **a swing of twice its Might for zero Energy** off a [Hidden] play.
- **ACTIONS ARE NEVER SIMULTANEOUS; ONLY EVENTS ARE (#187 batch 21, 2026-09-12).** 303.1 — *"Game Actions of any nature are performed one at a time and are executed completely"* — and 303.2 — *"Game Actions cannot be performed simultaneously for any reason"* — are the missing half of this project`s simultaneity model, whose other half is 370.1.a.2 and which is why a 1-damage sweep kills every 1-Might body at once. 303.2.a gives the tiebreak (Turn Order starting with the Turn Player), the same ordering 373.1 gives replacement effects. Companion pair, also uncited: **331.1.a and 331.1.b are the CLOSED-STATE default bar and the exact twin of 343.1.a / 343.1.b`s Showdown bar** — TWO separate defaults, which is the clean two-paragraph basis for the Action-versus-Reaction split this file derived from one worked example: [Reaction] answers both by 813.1.c.1, [Action] answers only the Showdown one by 806.1.c.1.
- **THE ELEVEN BIGGEST SACRIFICE COSTS IN THE POOL ARE ON UNITS, WHICH CAN NEVER LOSE THEM TO A COUNTER (#187 batch 22, 2026-09-12).** 356.2.b licenses the Optional Additional Cost that 425.1.c never refunds, and the two blocks meet at exactly six cards. Swept over `cards.json` folded by name+type for an as-an-additional-cost clause whose payment is NOT Energy or Power: **seventeen cards — ELEVEN units and SIX spells.** Since a permanent never becomes a Finalized Chain Item, **the eleven can never lose their cost to a counter at all**, including `OGN-231 Commander Ledros` (*"you may kill any number of friendly units"*), the largest irreversible cost in the pool. That reads as deliberate: the biggest sacrifice costs sit on the card type with no counter window. **The exposed set is six**, with costs and timing: `OGN-146 Wallop` (Body E2, spend a buff, optional, Action), `OGN-207 Call to Glory` (Order E3, spend a buff, optional, Reaction), `UNL-140 Conscription` (Chaos E5+2, spend 5 XP, optional), `UNL-142 Heedless Resurrection` (Chaos E2+1, kill a friendly unit, **MANDATORY**, Reaction), `UNL-173 Sacrifice` (Order E1, kill a friendly Mighty unit, **MANDATORY**, Reaction), `VEN-008 Ruthless Strike` (Fury E3, discard 1, optional, Action). **`UNL-140 Conscription` is the worst blowout and the only one whose resource has a THRESHOLD behind it**: 730.2 reduces the XP and 824.1.d makes a [Level N] ability Inactive the moment you hold less than N, so a countered Conscription costs the card, the 5 XP AND every rung it drops you below — a buff, a card and a body are flat losses, XP takes other cards down with it. Two of the six cannot opt out and sit in the worst window: `UNL-142` and `UNL-173` are Reaction spells with a MANDATORY kill, cast into the Closed State where all eleven counters live. The other four can DECLINE the optional cost at step 2 (356.2.b.1) and lose only mana. **And the replacement question is DISJOINT from the counter question in this pool**: `OGN-208 Cruel Patron` and all eleven sacrifice-cost cards are UNITS, so a cost that can be REPLACED (357.2.a) is on a card that can never be COUNTERED.
- **426.1.c SPLITS EVERY BUFF PAYOFF IN TWO, AND THE SPLIT ONLY PAYS ON A SATURATED BOARD (#187 batch 16, 2026-09-12).** *"Units with Buff Counters can still be chosen for actions that Buff units, but will not be Buffed as part of the execution."* Swept over `cards.json` folded by name+type: **"when you BUFF" is TWO cards** (`OGN-152 Mistfall`, `SFD-047 Simian Ancestor`) and 426.1.c kills both on an already-buffed body, while **"when you CHOOSE" is FOUR** (`SFD-057 Irelia Fervent`, `SFD-142 Jae Medarda` spells only, `SFD-144 Spirit Wheel`, `SFD-195 Blade Dancer`) and 426.1.c keeps every one alive, because the buffed unit can still be CHOSEN. So on a board where every body already carries a buff, **a mass buffer stops being a buffer and becomes a pure choose-N-friendly-units engine**: every buff does nothing, Mistfall and Simian Ancestor go dark, Spirit Wheel and Blade Dancer fire exactly as before. This file already carries the positive half (buffing is choosing under 702.2.a); 426.1.c is what makes it survive SATURATION, which is the only board where the distinction pays. Two riders: 809.1.c charges [Deflect] *"for each time they choose"*, so you pay the tax for choosing an already-buffed body and get nothing back; and the scale is worth stating — **this catalogue cites 702.3 four hundred and eighty-eight times and 426.1.c once.**
- **434.3 IS THE GENERAL HOME OF THE EQUIP-VERSUS-WEAPONMASTER SPLIT THIS PROJECT DERIVES CARD BY CARD, AND IT WAS CITED BY NOTHING (#187 batch 17, 2026-09-12).** *"Attaching cards does not inherently choose or specify a target, however Game Effects that Attach cards may do so."* That is the sentence explaining why 818.1.b.1 and 821.1.c.6 can differ at all: **the attach ACTION never chooses — only the effect performing it can.** The precision worth carrying, and it is the same shape as the buff split one bullet up: **a [Weaponmaster] attach chooses the GEAR and NOT the UNIT**, because 821.1.c is *"you may choose a Card you control with the Equipment tag"* and 821.1.c.6 removes only the unit — so a PAID Equip fires a *"when you choose a friendly unit"* payoff (`SFD-144 Spirit Wheel`, `SFD-195 Blade Dancer`) and a Weaponmaster attach fires **neither**, because the pool prints no *"when you choose a gear"* payoff at all. The action is the same and the choosing is not. **Sourcing correction from the same batch:** the one-way-door refinement this file derives from 718.1 read with 719.5 and 457.1 has a one-sentence source in the Detach block where a reader would look — **435.1.c**, *"the card being Detached has its Effect Text become Inactive and its Rules Text cease being Inactive"* — with 435.1.e (the carrier losing the Might Bonus) and 435.1.a.1 beside it, all three uncited.
- **NOTHING IN THIS POOL IS *CREATED*, SO EVERY TOKEN IS PLAYED — AND THAT TURNS A COINCIDENCE INTO AN EXCLUDES FACT (#187 batch 16, 2026-09-12).** 439.2.c draws the line with two worked examples: *"Play a 1 [M] Recruit token"* specifies the zone implicitly, so the token is PLAYED and created on the chain; *"Create a 1 [M] Recruit token"* specifies none, so 439.2.b.1 puts it anywhere on the Board it could be played to and it fires NOTHING. Measured over `corpus_flat.txt`: **the word "create" appears ZERO times in the pool**, against 60 lines printing *"play a … token"*. The one card that puts a Game Object on the board without playing it is `UNL-147 Baron Nashor`, and that is a BATTLEFIELD (439.2.b.5, not .b.1). So **every unit and gear token in this pool is PLAYED, universally** — this catalogue uses the positive half constantly (350.2 with 185.2.b for a token firing a play trigger) and never said it was universal, which is what makes it an EXCLUDES fact rather than a coincidence: **no token can dodge a prohibition or a watcher that bites on PLAYING.** `OGN-070 Mageseeker Warden`, the pool`s biggest denial card, has no token-shaped leak, and the 26 [Accelerate] units remain its only named one.
- **THE TRASH CAN CARRY A TRIGGER, THE DECK CAN ONLY CARRY A PASSIVE, AND THE HAND CARRIES NEITHER (#187 batch 18, 2026-09-12).** This catalogue cites 385.1 and 385.2 — the exception — 32 and 50 times, and had never cited **384, 384.1 or 384.2**, the rule they are an exception to. Put the two defaults side by side and **one word differs and is load-bearing**: 365.1 says Passive Abilities of Permanents are *"TYPICALLY only active while on the Board"*, while **384.2 says Triggered Abilities of Permanents are *"ONLY able to have their Conditions evaluated while on the Board"***. The passive default is soft, the triggered default is hard — and the trigger`s escape hatch carries a clause the passive`s does not: 385.1 makes it *"rely on the Information Level of the zone they are in"*, and the reference table, **355.10.a.1** (also uncited), lists the public zones as *"Battlefield Zones, Bases, Trashes, Legend Zones, Champion Zones, and Facedown Zones"* — **the Main Deck and the Hand are not on it.** So `SFD-175 Undertitan`s clause **could not have been written as a trigger**: 384.2 bars the Condition off the board, 385.2`s escape needs self-description, 385.1 makes that escape depend on an Information Level the Main Deck does not have, while the passive escape at 366.1 asks only for self-description with no such clause. Swept, twenty printings act on themselves in a non-board zone and split exactly along that line: TRIGGERED ones live in the TRASH (`OGN-037 Immortal Phoenix`, `OGN-252 Super Mega Death Rocket!`, which is 385.2`s own worked example); PASSIVE ones in the trash are `UNL-025 Undying Legion` (366.1`s own example) plus the fourteen [Flow] spells, where 366.2 / 366.2.a make the Flow cost payable from a zone the card is not normally played from; **PASSIVE REPLACEMENT in the MAIN DECK is exactly two — `SFD-175 Undertitan` and `OGN-194 Nocturne`** — and from the HAND there is nothing at all. That is an excludes fact for any future line, and it is also *why* the two deck-dwellers are immune to every trigger multiplier in the pool, which their entries state from the 370.1.b.1 side without knowing the card had to be built that way.
- **A FAILED LEGALITY CHECK COSTS NOTHING AND A COUNTERED PLAY COSTS EVERYTHING, AND THE TWO PARAGRAPHS HAD NEVER BEEN PUT SIDE BY SIDE (#187 batch 20, 2026-09-12).** **358.5**: *"If any of the above checks fail, the actions taken in this process are undone and the action is cancelled"* — targets released, costs unpaid, the attempt rewound. **425.1.c**: *"Countering does not refund any costs paid to play a card, activate an ability, or trigger an ability"* — the Energy, the Power, the discard and the friendly unit an Optional Additional Cost ate at 357.2 are all gone. **REFINED 2026-09-12: it bites on ANY cost you would not otherwise have paid, which is wider than "non-mana" — a [DEFLECT] TAX IS EXACTLY THAT.** 809.1.d makes it a MANDATORY additional cost under 356.2.a.2 and it SCALES with the number of bodies you chose, so `UNL-192 Alpha Strike` aimed across four Bird tokens is E3 + 1 Power **plus four rainbow**, and a counter refunds none of it — a larger loss than four of the six non-mana cards. You pay a Deflect tax because of what the OPPONENT`S board looks like, not because of what you were going to do with your turn, which is the property that makes a cost hurt when it is countered. So **the entire risk of a costed play sits in the counter window, and only SPELLS have one** — **and that pair is a pair FOR CARDS, corrected 2026-09-12 by the second self-audit of the lane that wrote it.** The ABILITY process has a third shape: **405.2 prints no undo clause at all.** 358 has five checks and 358.5`s undo; **405 has TWO checks and, for the illegal-state case, a CROSS-REFERENCE back to rule 349 rather than an answer in place** (*"resolve in the same way you would resolve a Card that creates an illegal state"*), and it says nothing about targets that have gone illegal, because 402.4 already handled the no-legal-options case at the CHOICE step. For abilities the block is one clause shorter than it looks (359.3.c scopes the pre-resolution window to spells; 359.2 is why a permanent never gets one). 425.1.c is cited 18 times here and 358.5 once. Third member: **425.1.b** — a countered card *"is not considered to have been played for abilities that TRIGGER on cards being played"* — which is the Counter-side statement of the split this file already carries from 419.4.b against 419.4.a.1, where a non-triggered check counts a fizzled card and a trigger does not.
- **THE FAILURE SHAPE OF A SELF-AUDIT: CITE THE GENERAL PARAGRAPH, MISS THE SPECIFIC ONE (#187 batch 20, 2026-09-12).** A lane audited its own five previous batches and found two real gaps in its own work, both the same shape. It had cited **366.1** (passives outside the board self-describe their context) and missed **370.3** (*"If a Game Object has a Replacement Effect that is ACTIVE IN A SPECIFIC ZONE, it is evaluated and subsequently applied if it enters that zone before an event occurs that it could replace"*), which is the narrowest paragraph for the Undertitan family and was cited by **exactly one entry in 762**. It had quoted **411.6**, whose first condition turns on the word ATTRIBUTED, and missed **411.5.a** (*"Spells and abilities have attribution for game actions, while players have responsibility"*), which is what that word means and why 411.6 has three conditions rather than one. **In both cases the general paragraph was enough to reach the right answer, which is exactly why nothing flagged it**: every reference resolved, every quote was verbatim, the conclusion was right. **No validator can see a citation that is TRUE and INCOMPLETE, and the only reader who can is the author re-reading their own work looking for the paragraph they did NOT cite** — which is why that pass is budgeted every third or fourth batch rather than at the end of a lane.
- **355.13 NAMES THE EXCEPTION CLASS TO "FILLER MUST BE UNCONDITIONALLY CASTABLE", AND THE CLASS HAS THIRTEEN MEMBERS (#187 batch 18, 2026-09-12).** This file carries the negative half as one of its hardest-won lessons, from the #166 cross-audit — a targeting spell is never safe filler, the case being `VEN-061 Decree of Insight`, unplayable against a board with no Body unit, which evaporated six Swain points. **355.13 is the paragraph that names the exception**: *"If a card specifies that a player chooses `any number` or `up to` some number of Game Objects to be affected, they may choose any number of available targets, including zero. If they choose zero, the spell or ability can be played without any targets."* Swept: SEVENTEEN spells print *"up to"* or *"any number of"*, of which **thirteen are unconditionally castable** (`OGN-105`, `OGN-224`, `OGN-256`, `OGN-264`, `OGS-011`, `SFD-043`, `SFD-080`, `SFD-198`, `UNL-054`, `UNL-103`, `VEN-103`, `VEN-107`, `VEN-150`) and **FOUR still brick, which is the real trap** — the tell is whether ANY choice in the sentence lacks the qualifier: `SFD-023 Piercing Light` deals 2 to *a unit* first, `SFD-184 Relentless Pursuit` moves *a friendly unit* first, `UNL-198 Moonfall` chooses *a battlefield where you have units*, `VEN-140 Shuriken Flip` then moves *a friendly unit*. **The cheapest member is `OGN-224 Salvage`** (Order, E2 + 1 Power, *"you may kill up to one gear. Draw 1"*), which is castable into an empty board by 355.13, counts as a card played for every non-triggered check even when every instruction fizzles (419.4.b), replaces itself — **and is simultaneously one of the sixteen gear-removal printings that answer the twelve Equipment finishers.**
- **THE WHOLE TIMING MODEL IS THREE ACTIONS, AND THE CENSUS IS NOW CLOSED (#187 batch 15, 2026-09-12).** Rules 413-446 label every Game Action with one of two sentences: swept, **34 classification lines, and exactly THREE are Discretionary** — 419.2 playing a card, 420.3 the Standard Move inherent to Units, 421.2 Hiding. The other 31 are Limited, from Draw and Deal Damage to Counter, Banish, Prevent, Skip and Pay. Read with 312.1 (*"Priority is the singular exclusive right to take Discretionary Actions"*) and 312.1.b.1 (*"players can always take and make choices for Limited Actions when instructed, regardless of Priority"*), the model collapses to one sentence: **there are three things a player may ever do AT WILL, every timing keyword is written against one of them, and everything else happens whenever an effect instructs it, on anybody`s turn, with no priority and no keyword.** That is the general form of at least six things this file derived card by card, including 444.2.c`s rune payment inside a finalization, Hiding being confined to your own Main Phase by 421.2 and nothing in 811, and a move by effect at 420.2 escaping every restriction the Standard Move at 420.3 carries. **The negative half is printed identically five times** — 416.2.a, 422.2.a, 427.4.a, 437.6.a, 443.3.a all say *"only when directed to by Game Effects"* — so no player may ever recycle, discard, banish, prevent damage or skip anything as a voluntary act.
- **RULE 410`s LIMITED HALF, measured beside the Discretionary half above (#187 batch 7, 2026-09-12).** Citation counts at catalogue 755 — Discretionary: 419.2 playing a card **0**, 420.3 the Standard Move **3** (against 200 for 420.3.a), 421.2 Hiding **2**. Limited: 410.2.b`s Draw example 0, 416.2 / 416.2.a Recycling 0 and 0, 422.2 / 422.2.a Discarding 0 and 0, 443.3 / 443.3.a Skipping 0 and 0. Two consequences this project has been re-deriving card by card. **HIDING IS CONFINED TO YOUR OWN MAIN PHASE BY 421.2 READ WITH 410.1.a** — *"Hiding is a Discretionary Action"* — and by nothing in 811: this file records that the hide and the play are a turn apart and has never said why the HIDE itself cannot happen at Reaction speed. And **an INSTRUCTED recycle or discard needs no priority and happens on anybody`s turn**, by 312.1.b.1 with 416.2.a and 422.2.a — the same sentence that licenses an instructed PLAY. One distinction is doing all of this work. **Companion, a scope widening rather than an overturn:** the recorded 443 Skip refusal (*"exactly one card prints Skip, zero cards trigger on the Draw Phase"*) is true and is scoped to the draw PHASE, while DRAWING is a different question — `UNL-074 Frigid Jewel` (*"When you draw your second card each turn"*) is the pool`s only card that triggers on drawing, against `VEN-022 Endless Riches` as its only Skip. 315.4.b is the draw the phase performs, so 443.2 removes the first of the Jewel`s two draws, and 443.4 makes Skip a Replacement Effect that 370.2 caps at one application per event. One card against one card, which is why it stays a note.
- **EVERY ADD IN THIS POOL IS AN ABILITY, SO 429.2`s INSTANT RESOLUTION IS UNIVERSAL HERE AND NO ADD CAN BE COUNTERED (#187 batch 8, 2026-09-12).** 429.2 / 429.2.a — an ability that Adds resources resolves as soon as it is finalized, before any other outstanding chain item — is cited 49 times here, and its EXCEPTION was cited by nothing: **429.2.b, *"Spells that Add resources will linger on the chain as normal when they are finalized"***, which would make such a spell counterable and unable to pay for something already being finalized. Measured over `corpus_flat.txt` on 2026-09-12: **of the pool`s 233 spells, ZERO Add resources** — the single regex hit is `UNL-073 Deadly Flourish`, whose Add belongs to the reminder text of the Gold gear TOKEN it plays. Scope is today`s pool; the exception goes live the moment a set prints an Add spell. Two more from the same batch, both uncited. **A LEADING "you may" IS A BLIND COMMITMENT**: 383.3.e.2.a puts the CHOICE *"during finalization of the Triggered Ability"* and 383.3.e.2.b removes a declined trigger from the chain, while 312.2.c withholds priority until every pending item finishes finalizing — so every *"you may pay X to Y"* trigger in the pool is committed to **before you see a single response**. This file already records that such a cost is PAID at finalization (204.3.a, 383.3.b.1); the DECISION being made there too is new. And **A DELAYED ABILITY FOLLOWS WHAT IT AFFECTED INTO BANISHMENT**: 392 — *"they are executed when their condition and/or specified time occurs regardless of whether the source of the Delayed Ability is still on the board or not"* — is the paragraph behind `UNL-169 Ashe, Focused`s printed *"(even if I`m no longer on the board)"*, with 390.5.c.1 supplying the zone. The whole of 390.5, 391 and 392 was uncited, and 391`s two worked examples are `Ravenborn Tome` and `Noxian Guillotine`, both printed here.
- **THE CORE RULES JUMP FROM 489 TO 649 — THERE IS NOTHING BETWEEN 490 AND 648 (measured 2026-09-12), AND THE 500s AND 600s THIS PROJECT CITES ARE TOURNAMENT RULES.** A lane`s range was labelled "500-829" for days and was really 649-829, which is why its slice measured 31 Example-carrying rows against 73 and 163 in the other two: **a third of its numbers do not exist.** The sideboard paragraphs `checkBuild` scores (601.1.c.1 to .c.3) live in `data/Riftbound-Tournament-Rules-2026-07-16.txt`, a separate numbering space of 940 headings whose content is procedure — Deck Registration, Sideboard, Match, Concessions, Judge Calls — and which is **an unsurveyed vein in its own right**. Nobody should look for combat or combo material in Core Rules 490-648 again. Companion from the same batch, and a second free integrity check nobody had run: **763.1 prints Riot`s canonical tag list and it matches `cards.json` 127 to 127**, with a single discrepancy that is a SPURIOUS COMMA IN RIOT`S OWN TEXT — it writes *"Mel, Miss, Fortune, Morgana"*, while eight printings carry `Miss Fortune` as one tag and zero carry `Miss` or `Fortune` alone. The card that reads the list refutes the typo itself: `UNL-138 The List` prints *"For example, Miss Fortune, Demacia, and Poro are tags"*, so rule 002 settles it from the card side and no reading is needed. **Registered, never put into `data/errata.json`** — same treatment as the 383.1.b / OGN-118 discrepancy, and for the same reason: that overlay`s find-string failure is the only mechanism keeping our card text honest.
- **THE 2v2 MODEL IN THIS FILE IS WRONG IN THREE PLACES, AND RULE 489 WAS READ END TO END TO FIND IT (#187 batch 13, 2026-09-12).** 489.3 (Victory Score 11) is cited 42 times here; 489.4, 489.5.x, 489.6, 489.7, 489.8.c.1/.c.2/.d/.e/.f/.g/.h/.i are cited **zero**. (1) **489.4 reads *"Battlefield Count: 3"***, so with 470 capping Scoring once per battlefield per turn and 315.2.b.2 Holding every battlefield you control, **a 2v2 board pays THREE points a turn where a Duel pays two.** This file says every BURST and CHAIN is *"three points short in 2v2"* — the target is 37% higher and **the RATE is 50% higher**, so an unopposed Hold curve is slightly FASTER there, and what is genuinely short is a BURST, which fires once and cannot borrow the extra battlefield. Same shape as the Baron Pit finding, printed in the format`s own setup. (2) **489.5.b removes the FIRST player`s battlefields entirely** (*"They will not be used"*) and 489.5.a gives each of the other three one of theirs at random — so a battlefield entry`s 2v2 caveat is stronger than the Duel caveat it currently borrows: **dead if you go first, one in three otherwise**; 489.8.h and 489.8.i add that teammates may share neither a battlefield nor a Champion Legend. (3) **489.8.e: *"Friendly describes controlled Game Objects by a player or their Teammate"***, with Riot`s example *"When you play me, ready a friendly unit"* reaching the teammate`s units — so **every *"friendly unit"* in the pool reaches the ally`s board in 2v2**, which roughly doubles the legal object set of a large fraction of this catalogue. The four ally-NAMING cards this file counts are not the reach; they are the exceptions that reach what *"friendly"* cannot, a teammate`s HOLD, which is an event and not a Game Object. Printed limits beside it: 489.8.c *"Control is not shared"*, 489.8.c.1 no hiding at a teammate`s battlefield, 489.8.c.2 no Standard Movement of their units, 489.8.d.3 makes any check of a player`s points read the TEAM`s, and 489.8.g.1 makes a Conquer Final Point require EACH battlefield except one your ally occupies. **No entry can come from this block until a representation decision is made** — `matchDeck` reads ONE list and 489.2 puts two independent decks on a team with 103.1.b applying per deck.
- **TOURNAMENT RULES 505 GOVERNS ALL 14 INFINITE ENTRIES AND WAS CITED BY NOTHING (#191 batch 27, 2026-09-12; Tournament Rules, labelled).** 505.2 — *"Players performing a loop must have each iteration of the loop be identical with no conditional actions"* — and 505.9 — *"If a sequence of actions is non-deterministic, it may not be shortcut and iterations must be performed manually."* Read against Core Rules 416.5 (two or more cards recycled to the Main Deck simultaneously go to the bottom in RANDOM order), a loop recycling 2+ per pass is non-deterministic **unless it draws all of them back in the same pass**, which an empty Main Deck makes automatic. All 14 were checked BY HAND and all 14 pass. **So the *"zero spare draws"* invariant from #21 is load-bearing a SECOND time against a rule it was not written for**: the catalogue maintains it because a fifth draw is Burn Out on one side and a stranded random card on the other, and it turns out to be exactly the condition that makes a loop shortcuttable rather than hand-iterated at an event. Companion: **Tournament Rules 506.5** — a trigger you FORGET still burns a non-optional *"first time each turn"* slot, which meets the 383.3.e.1 / 383.1.b family from the other side. And a measurement worth keeping: of 1,082 distinct rule references in the catalogue, exactly ONE resolves only in the Tournament Rules (703.3.a.3, labelled at both sites) while **52 exist in BOTH books**, so `test/rule-refs.test.ts` accepting either cannot disambiguate — **write every Tournament Rules citation labelled.**
- **`grep` LIES ABOUT THE RULES FILE A SECOND WAY, AND THE TWO COMPOSE (#200 batch 6, 2026-09-12).** This file already records that `^NNN\.` cannot see the 89 headings after a form feed. The second way: **the file writes headings with a TRAILING period**, so an anchor ending `NNN\. ` (dot then SPACE) returns zero for every paragraph — eight rules came back empty in one batch and read exactly like a discovery. The safe form is `grep -E "^[[:space:]]*NNN\."` with no trailing space, which is what the committed probes use. Third variant from the same batch, and the fourth appearance of the singular-verb trap: a point-gain sweep written on `gain` misses `OGN-290`, which prints *"gains"*.
- **AN "AS YOU LOOK AT OR REVEAL ME" CLAUSE IS A REPLACEMENT EFFECT, NOT A TRIGGER, AND A SHIPPED ENTRY CALLED IT A TRIGGER TWICE (2026-09-12).** 370.1.b.1 — *"In the case of Replacement Effects that describe a game action to occur `as` an event occurs, the described event is replaced by that same event plus the game action being performed"* — and its own worked example is `SFD-175 Undertitan`, which is why the five Undertitan entries owed it as a citation (flagged first by one lane, applied here after a second found it independently). Three things a replacement buys that a trigger would not: **no Chain Item**, so no window exists between the look and the body arriving; **immunity to every trigger multiplier in the pool** — Karthus doubles Deathknells, Blue Sentinel and Red Brambleback double Hold and Conquer triggers, and none of them can see a replacement; and 371.2 is what makes the *"you may"* legal. Companion from the same batch, on the adjacent block: **355.5.b** exempts a permanent`s own ETB from the Make Relevant Choices step (*"The target will be chosen when the ability triggers"*), so **a body is already on the board when its own ETB chooses and is a legal choice for its own trigger**, and an opponent reacting to the card BEING PLAYED is acting before the choice exists — their real window is after finalization, when 337.1 has already locked it. Its second worked example is Targon`s Peak by name.
- **TWO DIFFERENT PARAGRAPHS MAKE DAMAGE SIMULTANEOUS, AND THE ONE THIS FILE LEANS ON WAS NEVER CITED (#187/300-499 lane, 2026-09-12).** Every Flurry of Blades refusal here turns on `OGN-133` killing all the 1-Might bodies AT ONCE, and the sentence above carried no paragraph at all; the manager then pointed a lane at 465.2.c.1.a, which is the WRONG one. **370.1.a.2 is the spell case** — *"An event can occur simultaneously with other events only when those events are all the result of the same Game Action or change in state occurring"* — and its worked example is a spell killing two units at once *"because their deaths result from the same game action"*, with a negative example one line down: *"Kill a friendly unit. If you do, kill an enemy unit"* is TWO actions and is not simultaneous. **465.2.c.1.a is the Combat Damage Step and nothing else** (*"When all Damage is assigned, it will be Dealt simultaneously"*). Reaching the right conclusion through the wrong paragraph is the same failure class as quoting reminder text as rules text. **The companion half has teeth for the whole defensive suite:** 465.2.c.1 (*"Assigning Damage is not Dealing Damage"*) with 417.3.a (*"Assigning Damage causes Damage to be dealt outside of being directed to Deal Damage"*) and the already-cited 417.1.a mean there is **NO WINDOW between assignment and dealing** — a [Reaction] played *"in response to the damage"* does not exist, and anything that moves a Might must land before 465.2.c assigns. All three of 370.1.a.2, 417.3.a and 465.2.c.1 were cited by nothing. Measured beside them, **the same death event pays three different ways**: of the five cards watching a friendly unit die, `UNL-068 Spectral Centaur` and `UNL-129 Vicious Snapjaws` have no gate and pay PER BODY, `Altar of Memories` is rationed by its own exhaust and pays once however many die, and `OGN-118 Wraith of Echoes` with `UNL-174 Shard of Undoing` say *"the first time each turn"*, which 383.1.b collapses to a single execution — so simultaneity is worth everything to the first pair and nothing to the other three. And Mind prints NO 1-Might fodder (every Mind token is a 3-Might Sprite or Mech), which is why the obvious Flurry-plus-Centaur pairing is dead on arithmetic and `UNL-081 Keeper of Masks` is the exception: Might 1 himself plus two Reflections that R27 leaves at 0 Might, all three killed by one damage under 143.2.a, and all three condemned by 816.1.b anyway, so the fodder is free.
- **AN "ATTACK OR DEFEND" TRIGGER IS BOTH KINDS AT ONCE AND FIRES TWICE PER TURN CYCLE, AND SIX ENTRIES PRICED IT AT HALF RATE (2026-09-12).** 383.4.e defines an Attack Trigger as firing when a Unit **or PLAYER** gains the Attacker designation for the first time in a combat; 383.4.f is the identical sentence for the Defender. A card worded *"when I attack or defend"* satisfies both, so it is an Attack Trigger AND a Defend Trigger - the same shape the rules state outright for the other pair at 823.1.b, *"Hunt is both a Conquer and a Hold effect"* - and the honest rate is once on your turn attacking, once on theirs defending. All six entries were upgraded in the same commit. Two limits keep it bounded: **383.4.e.2.a checks the condition ONCE PER COMBAT** despite a unit gaining and losing the designation several times within it (383.4.f.2.a mirrors it), so blinking a carrier out and back re-fires nothing; and **the attack side is UNIT-keyed while the defend side is PLAYER-keyed** - measured three ways on 2026-09-12, the pool prints **ZERO** cards worded *"when you attack"* against **FOUR** worded for defending, three of them battlefields (`OGN-279 Fortified Position`, `OGN-285 Reaver`s Row` which is banned in both formats, `SFD-215 Ravenbloom Conservatory`, plus `SFD-126 Loyal Pup`). That asymmetry explains two things this file records separately: every attack trigger needs its unit present when 464.2.c.3 designates, which is the general form of the attack-reinforcement problem that only Rengar and Ava Achiever lift; and a BATTLEFIELD can carry a defend trigger at all only through 383.4.f`s player half read with 190.6.d, because a battlefield is never a unit and never gains the designation. **Refused with it:** `OGN-279` plus `SFD-215` as a pairing, because 485.4.a has each player provide three battlefields and 485.5 selects one at RANDOM, so two of your own battlefields never share a board.
- **NEVER ROUND-TRIP `data/combos.json` THROUGH A JSON DUMP TO EDIT IT, AND THE TELL IS A CONTRADICTION BETWEEN TWO MEASUREMENTS (2026-09-12).** A lane changed two sentences by parsing the file, mutating and dumping it back at `indent=1`. The file is indent 2, so the round trip rewrote EVERY LINE: `git diff --stat` read **55,416 insertions and 55,416 deletions for a two-sentence change**, in a tree four lanes were writing to, where that collides with everything and buries any concurrent edit. It was caught because the STRUCTURAL diff said *"two notes fields"* while the line count said 110,832 — **a big number alone is rationalisable; a big number that contradicts a small structural diff is not.** The fix is to splice on a unique substring and then verify structurally that exactly the intended fields changed and the entry count is unchanged. The manager`s merge scripts do rewrite the file wholesale, so both now ABORT unless `JSON.stringify(db, null, 2) + "\n"` reproduces the file on disk byte for byte, proven by deliberately reformatting the file and watching them refuse (exit 3). Same family as the `noclobber` and `argv` traps, each of which has now bitten twice. **And `cp` is aliased to `cp -i` in this shell**, so a scripted backup prompts and silently does nothing — use `/bin/cp -f`, which is how that guard was tested at all.
- **A needs/produces JOIN MEANS NOTHING WITHOUT THE DOMAIN CHECK, and the manager published one without it (2026-09-12).** Two joins over the same two fields answer different questions: *needs INTERSECTS produces, no domain check* counts pairings that **cannot legally be built**, while *needs SUBSET of produces, with 103.1.b capping the union of the two entries` domains at a legend`s two* counts the ones a deck can actually run. I used the first to refute a lane`s structural claim, and two of my three counter-examples were illegal shells: `bottled-constellation-token-points` is mono-Mind, `pursuer-herald-recruits` is Chaos/Order and `twilight-reveler-eye-facebreaker-recruits` is Fury/Order, so both unions are THREE domains. The lane`s claim was still false under its own join and nothing was recorded either way - but **the intersect-without-domains table must never reach this file as a structure.** Same shape as #200`s own "class by identity" lesson, one level down: ask which of the two questions a join answers before you quote it at anyone.
- **A quote you TYPED is wrong precisely where the rules text is ungrammatical, and four copies of one such quote were already in the catalogue (#191 batch 19, 2026-09-12).** 437.4 is printed *"Damage dealt to a Unit that has **that all of that** damage Prevented is not considered to have been dealt to it at all"* — Riot's own doubled wording — and a walker who types it from memory silently produces the grammatical version. That is the whole failure mode: **not laziness, but that memory repairs a broken sentence on the way past.** The lane's own quote checker caught it in a draft; a sweep of the merged catalogue then found FOUR entries already carrying the repaired form (`unyielding-spirit-challenge-unit-damage`, `counter-strike-tianna-crownguard-in-place-save`, `esteemed-hierophant-tomb-raider-seven-runes`, `papertree-esteemed-hierophant-rune-count`), all four now pasted. A `quote` that reads smoothly is the one to re-check. **And the companion method, which is cheap and now proven twice: the ORPHAN-NEIGHBOUR probe** — for every heading cited zero times, take the citation count of its parent and siblings and keep it where the best is 15 or more. 815.3 (the Tank READER, zero citations beside 815.2's 25) and 813.4 (the Ambush undo, zero beside 822.1.b's 61) were both found this way, and the same pass refused 810.3 with its scope (all three [Ganking] hits GRANT the keyword, none READS it — live the moment a card prints "units here with [Ganking]") and surfaced 718.5.e / 718.5.f, which are real and uncited: *"Attached cards may have different Controllers from their Top-Most card"* and *"Changes in Control of the Top-Most card do not impact Control of Attached cards and vice versa"*, with 718.5.g still appending the Effect Text and the Might Bonus — so stealing an equipped body hands you the text and the Might while the opponent keeps the card, and your own Equipment survives the theft of its carrier. **Grep `test/` for an existing probe before writing an ad-hoc one**: the rule-reference tokenizer that drops `899.md` already lives in `test/rule-refs.test.ts`, and a lane rebuilt it badly because the fix lived in a test nobody reads while writing a probe.
- **A conquer effect bridged onto a Hold loses any condition that depends on the Main Phase, the Skyfall bridge is dead in the conquer → hold direction, and a mass ready with [Ganking] replaces a whole loop (#161 batch 2, 2026-09-06).** 315.2 (Scoring Step) precedes 316 (Main Phase), so at the instant of a Hold you have played NOTHING "this turn" — which is what kills `VEN-065 Swain, Visionary` (*"When I conquer, if you've played a non-token unit, a non-token gear, and a spell this turn, you score 1 point"*) under `SFD-030 Skyfall of Areion` + Blue Sentinel even though the domains are legal: the same phase-order fact as 316.2's task order, seen from the other end. Skyfall reads *"My hold effects are also conquer effects, and vice versa"*, and the catalogue only ever uses hold → conquer; the reverse — letting Blue Sentinel multiply a conquer payoff every Beginning Phase with no attack — was swept exhaustively and is dead: the pool has exactly five conquer-scoring cards (swept: Tryndamere, Nasus Ascended, Swain, Ivern Friend to All, Kai'Sa Evolutionary; Green Father and Otterpus match "conquer" but score nothing) and each fails differently — `OGN-034 Tryndamere` on 383.2.a.1 (a Hold has no attack and no excess damage), `VEN-046 Nasus` on 103.1.b (Calm + Fury + Mind; `nasus-ascended-sentinel-arena-hold` gets there through the Colorless Reckoner's Arena instead), Swain on the phase order, `UNL-177 Ivern` because he already reads "conquer or hold", `OGN-112 Kai'Sa` because she scores no point. And Trinity Force + Skyfall + Reckoner's Arena is SEVEN (1 + 3 × 2, 103.2.b caps Trinity at three), one short, with the only fix (Ahri) a third domain — refused on arithmetic, not on a reading. The constructive half: `SFD-192 Shurelya's Requiem` ([Unique], Calm/Mind, E4 P2) prints *"When you play this, ready your units"* AND *"[Effect] Your units here have [Ganking]"*, so it replaces the six readies `swain-double-conquer` buys with a 40-Energy loop — 144.4.c.1 collapses the A → base → B walk to one leg — and `swain-shurelya-double-conquer` reaches 8 across two Conquers in one turn for 8 Energy + 3 Power with no loop. **Standing verdict on the finisher families:** Grand Plaza, the Blue Sentinel BURSTs and the Red Brambleback conquer BURSTs are saturated from the feeder side (the Sentinel family provably, since exactly three "trigger an additional time" cards exist); the CHAINs are not — two of four were improved outright, so that class is where the room is.
- **A Deathknell body that is the sole garrison loses the battlefield when it dies, 356.6 makes Hextech Gauntlets free on any 3+ Might body, and 441.1 reaches units (#159 batch 2, 2026-09-06).** 323.6 — *"Players lose control of any controlled Battlefields without their Units occupying them if the turn is in an Open State and there is no Showdown or Combat ongoing there"* — with 190.4.a / 190.4.c: killing your own Ekko, Recurrent for his Deathknell when he is the ONLY unit at a battlefield you hold hands that battlefield back at the next Cleanup, so the next Hold scores one fewer. A three-turn Time Warp ledger that counted "Hold both" every turn was rejected on this (its own notable had priced losing a battlefield to the opponent, never to its own Deathknell); check every self-kill line for who else stands there. 356.6 — *"Energy and Power costs can't be reduced below 0"* — floors `UNL-188 Hextech Gauntlets`' Equip (*"[Equip] 3 Energy + rainbow. This ability's Energy cost is reduced by the Might of the unit you choose"*) at 0 Energy on any body of 3+ Might, so it attaches for one rainbow, and the +3 Might it returns is +3 EXCESS DAMAGE under R28 = A — the currency `OGN-034 Tryndamere`'s "5 or more excess damage" is priced in; Gauntlets and `UNL-187 Piltover Enforcer` are Fury/Order and Tryndamere is Fury, one legend. And `VEN-089 Wild Claw` (*"Look at the top 5 … play it, reducing its Energy cost by 5 … Then you may do this: Empower it"*) is the #153 Empower fact reaching a UNIT: 441.1 makes Empowering the act, so Nasus, Ascended's own 8-Energy [Empower] is never determined; found with `grep -i empower`, no brackets, in zero entries before.
- **A Svellsongur copy carries the unit's STATICS too, and doubling an uncapped converter is a no-op (#160 batch 3, 2026-09-06).** `SFD-089 Rumble, Scrapper` prints two sentences — *"Your Mechs have +1 Might (including me)"* and *"When I hold, play a 3 Might Mech unit token to your base"* — and Svellsongur copies "that unit's text", both of them; so three Svellsongur are eight copies of the STATIC as well as eight of the trigger, and every Mech (187.4 gives the token the tag by rule) stands at +8 as a continuous modifier that 702.3 never caps. Every Svellsongur entry before this one counted only the trigger. 816.1.c verbatim: *"The Trigger Condition is the controller of the permanent's Beginning Phase starting"* — so a `[Temporary]` body created BY a trigger that fires at that same instant (`UNL-084 Sprite Queen`, *"at the start of your Beginning Phase, play a ready 3 Might Sprite … with [Temporary]"*) enters AFTER the condition was checked and does not die that turn, with no 383.3.d ordering argument needed; printed ready, it walks to LeBlanc's battlefield the same Main Phase and never dies again. The obvious first reading ("born at the start, dies at the start") is wrong. And `SFD-117 Ancient Henge` / `SFD-083 Hextech Anomaly` print *"Pay any amount"* with no cap per activation, so a second activation from any readier or from Heimerdinger's copy is worth ZERO — dead pairings on every "readies gear" / "copies exhaust abilities" rule.
- **Granted [Shield] SUMS, "pay X to Y" is a base cost while "pay X. If you do, Y" is not, and the engine × payoff walk's five other facts (#159 / #160, 2026-09-06).** 814.2: *"If a Unit has Shield, or has been granted Shield, and is granted Shield by an additional source, the Shield Value of all granted Shield keywords is summed"* (worked example Stalwart Poro + Block = Shield 4), and 814.1.b.3 makes a bare `[Shield]` worth 1 — Svellsongur copies the unit's WHOLE text, keywords included, so the 2^v composition (#45) multiplies [Shield] exactly as it multiplies the trigger: three Svellsongur on `VEN-138 Shen` (M7, [Shield]) are eight instances = Shield 8 = 15 Might defending beside eight score triggers. No entry had cited 814.2 and it reprices every Svellsongur and Blue Sentinel ([Shield 2]) line for SURVIVAL, which a BURST needs to reach its own Beginning Phase. Svellsongur is GEAR, so it is the one Hold multiplier a `if there is exactly one other UNIT you control here` condition cannot see — `shen-kinkou-sentinel-hold` is pinned at one Sentinel by 383.2.a.1 and stops at 3, `shen-kinkou-svellsongur-hold` is unconstrained and reaches 9. Next: 383.3.b makes *"pay X **to** Y"* a Triggered Ability's BASE COST paid at finalization (383.3.b.1; worked example Ekko, Recurrent *"Recycle me to ready your runes"*), whereas 205 makes *"pay X. **If you do,** Y"* a game action at resolution (its worked example: *"When I attack, you may pay [4][C]. If you do, kill a unit here"*) — Riot gives one example of each. So `SFD-214 Power Nexus` (*"you may pay [4 rainbow] to score 1 point"*) is paid at FINALIZATION, and 383.3.d places every simultaneous Hold trigger before any resolves, so Gold made by the same Hold can never pay it — not even under R25 = A; the Gold has to be banked from the previous turn. 135.2.e.5.b: *"[A] can be spent to pay a Power cost of any Domain"*, so Blue Sentinel's surviving rainbow pays domain costs. 471.1.a.1: *"points Gained from sources that are not Conquer are not beholden to these restrictions"* — 471.1.b's Final Point clause ("Scored every Battlefield this turn") applies to a CONQUER only, never to a Hold point or a card-text "score 1 point", so the eighth point of an ability BURST lands unconditionally. **All 15 two-domain pairs are printed as legends** (C(6,2) = 15, complete over `cards.json`, and no legend has other than two domains), so a Domain Identity check on a line that names no legend reduces to *"is the union of the cards' domains at most two?"*. Compute that identity from `uses`, never from the prose of a name or a note — #155 called two mono-Body entries "Body/Order-family" and refused Time Warp on them for the wrong reason. `VEN-067 Bottled Constellation` (*"At the start of your Main Phase, you may kill 3 other friendly units and/or gear to score 1 point"*) is the pool's only bodies → points converter that needs no battlefield (swept: every other scoring card without here/hold/conquer/battlefield in its text is Find Your Center, Draven, Sumpworks Map, Poppy, Illaoi — none counts bodies), and `OGN-293 The Grand Plaza` is the only other body payoff, so a `token-body-engine` has exactly two outlets. And #155's candidate 1 as specified — Power Nexus + two Blue Sentinel + Time Warp, no Gold — reaches FIVE, not eight: 161.2.a caps the runes at twelve, 161.2.b takes a recycled rune off the board, 315.3.b returns two a turn, so the two-turn Power budget is 14 and floor(14/4) = 3 Nexus firings + 2 base Holds. The Gold (World Atlas, Eminent Benefactor) is what makes it 8.
- **Blue Sentinel Power survives 167 by the Main Phase task ORDER, and a trigger doubler is worth zero on the "you may exhaust me" Hold/Conquer legends (#160 walk, 2026-09-06).** 316.2 *"The following Tasks become Outstanding in the specified order"*, 316.3 *"1. Each player's Rune Pool empties"*, 316.4 *"2. At the start of Main Phase game effects take place"* — the pool empties FIRST, then the Hold-and-Sentinel Power lands, which every Blue Sentinel entry since `world-atlas-sentinel-gold` had asserted without a citation. And two windows that are easy to conflate: 383.3.b makes a cost written right after "you may" the trigger's BASE COST and 383.3.b.1 pays it to FINALIZE, while 312.2.c withholds priority in a Closed State until *"all pending chain items finish being finalized"* — so a doubled `you may exhaust me to …` Hold/Conquer trigger (Chem-Baroness, Gloomist, Deceiver, Green Father, Void Burrower, Piltover Enforcer) needs the exhaust TWICE with no window to ready between the two finalizations, and Blue Sentinel / Reckoner's Arena / Red Brambleback add nothing on that whole family; but 340.4 (*"If the Chain is not empty and there are no Pending Items, the controller of the newest item on the chain gains Priority"*) DOES open a window between the two RESOLUTIONS, so a doubled `ready X` effect is a no-op only when nothing can spend the ready at [Reaction] speed there. `shen-kinkou-sentinel-hold` is the one Blue Sentinel line a second Sentinel breaks. Two `excludes` this walk found for the synergy layer (702.3 / 702.3.a, "a second trigger that is a no-op"): `UNL-043 Enthusiastic Promoter` on `blue-sentinel-hold` and `OGN-164 Sett, Brawler` on `red-brambleback-conquer`, both buffing a FIXED target; `OGN-283 Navori Fighting Pit` escapes because it buffs "a unit".
- **Synergy pairings yield ENGINEs, not finishers; five mechanics from the last two batches of #153 (2026-09-06).** The #153 walk gave all 55 orphan synergy rules a disposition (29 entries, back-links, refusals, one deferral) and **every one of the 29 is ENGINE**: a synergy rule is an anchor+partner VALUE EXCHANGE, while every INFINITE / BURST / CHAIN / ALT_WIN in the catalogue stands on a three-to-five-card LINE — a different object. Mining `synergies.json` buys breadth, never finishers; finishers come from lines. The mechanics: (1) 709 is an EVENT, so a "becomes Mighty" payoff caps at the number of DIFFERENT sub-5 bodies that cross 5, never at the number of pumps. (2) `UNL-166 Stalking Wolf` overrides 822.1.b — Ambush is *"I may be played to a battlefield where you control Units"* — with *"You may [Ambush] me to its battlefield"* for the Bird/Cat/Dog/Poro he kills as a cost, so an M6 lands where you control NOTHING with no move and no exhaust: the first non-token answer to #48's walk-to-the-battlefield bottleneck (and the same clause costs him the [Reaction] half of 822.1.b, so on that line he is a Main Phase play). (3) 818.1 against a play: `SFD-008 Sentinel Adept`'s [Weaponmaster] ATTACHES (Activated Ability, no play trigger fires) while `SFD-024 Rell, Magnetic` PLAYS and then attaches (play triggers DO fire) — complementary, not variants; and every [Equip] cost printed as ONE rune is discounted to nothing by "one rainbow less". (4) Carrying a buff and SPENDING a buff (702.2.b: *"Spending a Buff removes a single Buff counter from a Unit"*) are opposite uses of one counter: `OGN-065 Wizened Elder` and `OGN-125 Bilgewater Bully` are the pool's only two `While I'm buffed` statics (swept: 7 hits for "buffed", the rest are buffers or The Boss's death replacement), and Call to Glory / Albus Ferros / Fae Dragon all pay by spending, which switches them off — a deck picks one side. (5) A bounce to HAND is NOT a Recall (455: to the BASE), which is why `OGN-104 Retreat` rebuys a "play a token HERE" body without losing the "here" while Portal Rescue, which replays to the base, does lose it. Two refusals with the phase order explicit: `OGN-288 Startipped Peak`'s Hold fires at 315.2.b.2 after Awaken (315.1) has finished, so the channelled rune is not readied that turn and its 164.2.b Power would land in the Beginning Phase only for 167 to empty it at the Main Phase — one extra rune, as Energy, from the FOLLOWING turn (confirms #102); `OGN-297 Windswept Hillock` under 810.1.c.3 ([Ganking] adds destinations, never moves) in a two-battlefield Duel is worth exactly one alternative destination per unit standing there. Still open: `green-father-brush-four-tags` (438 Replace vs a battlefield TOKEN under 190.x).
- **104 of the pool's 935 names carry two or more base codes, and the matcher ALREADY handles it — do not re-file that bug (#153 walk batch 4, 2026-09-06).** The measurement is real and worth keeping: Green Father (UNL-195 / UNL-233), Daring Poro (OGN-210 / UNL-225), Karma Channeler (OGN-235 / SFD-237), Bloodharbor Ripper (UNL-185 / UNL-228), every Rune and every Seal, 104 names in all. **The consequence the walk drew from it is FALSE and was checked before anything was filed**: `CardIndex.equivalents(base)` (`src/cards.ts:124`) already resolves a base code to every printing sharing its normalised name and type — **and Tournament Rules 601.2.a is the paragraph that makes name+type the LEGALLY correct unit, not merely the convenient one (added 2026-09-12, #191 batch 29; Tournament Rules, labelled): *"A card may only be included in a deck if it is from a set that is legal in that format OR IT HAS THE SAME NAME AS A CARD FROM A SET THAT IS LEGAL IN THAT FORMAT."*** Legality travels with the NAME, so two printings of one name are interchangeable by rule and every census here that folds by name+type is doing the legally right thing rather than a matching convenience. The same paragraph settles a claim this file makes without a source: 601.2.c says an out-of-range collector number confers no legality (Riot`s example is 300 of 250), which could have made *"VEN-SP1 to SP6 are real playable cards"* wrong — measured, **all six share a name with an in-range printing** (Kai`Sa/OGN-039, Sona/OGN-073, Ahri/OGN-119, Sett/OGN-164, Ezreal/SFD-149, Lux/OGS-014), so they are legal BY NAME and the claim is CONFIRMED with a citation. The condition to re-check on a new set is an SP printing of a name that exists nowhere in range; there are none today, and `matchDeck` builds `owned` through it — `equivalents("UNL-225")` returns `["OGN-210","UNL-225"]`. Measured against the whole catalogue: **0 of the `uses[]` rows have printings `equivalents()` fails to cover, and 0 names split by type**, so there is no matching blind spot and #89's 53.8% is not a floor for this reason. 103.2.b caps by NAME and the matcher matches by base code — those two facts coexist because `equivalents` is the bridge. Reproduce with `cards.equivalents(...)` before believing any future report of this.
- **Three costing facts from the same batch.** 356.4.e's worked example (Eager Apprentice `OGN-084` + Sky Splitter `OGN-014`) is about ORDER and is worth exactly ONE Energy, at Might exactly 7: Sky Splitter's own reduction is "by the highest Might among units you control" and the Apprentice's is "reduced by 1, **to a minimum of 1**", so Apprentice-first takes E8 to 7 and then to 0, Sky-Splitter-first takes it to 1 and the floor holds it there — and at Might 8+ the Apprentice adds nothing at all. A `control X or fewer runes` clause is a TIMING CHOICE, not a deckbuilding constraint: 164.2.b has no exhaust, 161.2.b takes the recycled rune OFF the board to the Rune Deck and 167 empties the pool only at the START of your Main Phase, so you tap ten, spend, dump six for Power and stand at four with the Power still live — but the sibling clause `fewer runes than an opponent at the start of your Beginning Phase` (`VEN-005`, `VEN-006`) is read at 315.2.a, BEFORE 315.3.b channels two, and cannot be gamed. And a static `Your token units have +1 Might` (`UNL-077 Soul Shepherd`) is a CONTINUOUS MODIFIER, not a 702 buff counter, so 702.3's one-per-unit cap never touches it and it stacks with a real buff — the same distinction #102 drew for Lee Sin, Centered. Citation precision for tokens: **185.1 + 185.2.b** ("Token units have a Might") is the pair for *a token unit is a unit* when something COUNTS bodies; **350.2 + 185.2.a** ("Tokens are not cards, but can still be Played") is the pair for *a token is played* when something TRIGGERS. They are different paragraphs and are easy to conflate.
- **A Signature card can force a legend by TAG, an Empower can be handed out without paying the [Empower] cost, and 167 empties the pool and not the runes (#153 walk, 2026-09-06).** `UNL-196 Daisy!` is a Signature card tagged Ivern/Ionia, so 103.2.d.2 makes every list that runs her a **Green Father** list, and 103.2.d.1 spends the whole 3-card Signature budget on her. **But the uniqueness claim that walk shipped is wrong and is being corrected: there are TWO Ivern legends, `UNL-195 Green Father` and `UNL-233 Green Father` (three printings, the `UNL-233*` alt-art included), identically named, both Calm/Order, with the same text.** The conclusion survives — either way it is a Green Father deck — but this is the second time a note claimed "the ONLY card that…" and a full grep refuted it, so the standing rule is now load-bearing: **count base codes, not grep rows, and never ship a uniqueness claim you did not sweep the whole pool for.** Next: 167 reads *"Every player's Rune Pool empties at the start of each player's Main Phase and the end of each player's turn"* — the POOL, not the runes, so a rune readied at the end of your turn is still ready on the opponent's, which is what `OGS-017 Dark Child` sells; and those readies buy ENERGY only, because 164.2.b (`Recycle this: [Reaction] — Add [C]`) carries no exhaust and an already-exhausted rune pays Power on any turn anyway (#44's free floor). Then Empower: 441.1 is *"the act of rendering one or more Game Objects Empowered"* and 441.2 makes it a permanent board state, so an ability that Empowers something **performs that act directly and never pays the target's printed [Empower] cost** — `VEN-062 Hextech Formula` (`exhaust: Empower another gear`) hands `VEN-018 Rage Amplifier` its Empowered state for free, skipping the E6 + 1 Fury Power the Amplifier's own [Empower] asks; 827.1.c.3 governs *"determining a card's Empower cost"* and so has nothing to say about a route that never determines one. Companion: `VEN-054 Questionable Tome`'s [Empower] cost IS the exhaust its own draw needs, so unaided it Empowers or draws in a turn, never both. And the four-tag (Bird/Cat/Dog/Poro) package cannot rebuild itself out of a token: `UNL-217 Trapping Grounds` makes a Bird, but 185 + 186.1 keep it out of the trash, so `UNL-167 Starhound` and `UNL-168 Undying Loyalty` can never choose it. **Open, deliberately not leaned on:** `UNL-195 Green Father`'s *"replace that battlefield with a Brush battlefield token"* — 438 Replace against what a battlefield TOKEN is for 190.x (Control, and 190.6.d's "you" referent) is unruled; `green-father-brush-four-tags` was deferred rather than guessed, and opening it may need an R-number.
- **A body takes the Attacker designation with no move at all, and a gear fetched by a "When I attack" trigger has already missed that attack (#153 walk, 2026-09-06).** 190.3.a.1 — *"units moving to or being played to a battlefield apply Contested status"* — read with 464.2.c.1, which makes the Attacker *"the player whose unit(s) applied the Contested status"*: a unit PLAYED to an occupied enemy battlefield is the Attacker without ever moving, so 144.2's exhaust never comes up (it is the Standard Move's price alone) and 143.4's entering exhausted costs nothing. `SFD-093 Dauntless Vanguard` — *"You may play me to an occupied enemy battlefield"* — is the pool's card for it. And the timing trap on the other side: 464.2.c.3 places a `When I attack` trigger at the Attacker designation and 383.2.a.1 makes its extra condition part of the Trigger Condition, checked when the trigger is PLACED, so an Equipment fetched by that trigger pays from the NEXT combat while a flat Might bonus pays at once (465.2.c reads Might at assignment). Same shape as #150's 477.1.b.1.b correction — check WHEN the trigger was placed, not when the effect resolves. Companion: `Portal Rescue` plays the body *to their base*, so 359.3.f.2 reads "here" as the base, which makes every "play a token here" ETB strictly worse under Portal Rescue than under a bounce-to-hand.
- **The Awaken Phase is NOT inside the Beginning Phase, and a "channel N runes" payoff is a refill (#153 walk, 2026-09-06).** 315 is **Start of Turn**, and it holds four phases in order: **315.1 Awaken Phase** (315.1.b "The Turn Player readies all Game Objects they control that are able to be readied"), **315.2 Beginning Phase** (315.2.a Beginning Step, 315.2.b the Hold), 315.3 Channel Phase, 315.4 Draw Phase. So Awaken is the phase BEFORE the Beginning Phase, not a step of it — every readying has already happened when 816.1.b kills a `[Temporary]` permanent "at the start of the controller's Beginning Phase, before scoring". Consequence: a gear or unit whose payoff costs an exhaust is READY when the Temporary body dies, so **every `[Temporary]`-death payoff with an exhaust in its cost is free once every turn**, no readying effect needed. And 430.1 ("taking one or more Runes from the top of a player's Rune Deck") with 430.3 ("If there aren't sufficient runes in the Rune Deck, channel as many as possible"), 161.2.b (a recycled rune returns to the Rune Deck) and 315.3.b (2 channelled free every turn) make a `channel N runes` payoff a **REFILL, not a ramp**: by the turn you can afford it the Rune Deck is nearly empty, so it returns what recycling put back rather than adding new mana. 430.2.a is the default the card text overrides — "runes are channeled readied" unless the effect says exhausted.
- **A wall is priced in DAMAGE, an Empowered mover draws on its own, and 477.1.b.1.b's example is not about Temporary (#150 walk, 2026-09-06).** 143.2.a needs NONZERO damage to kill, so a 0-Might Reflection costs an attacker 1 damage each and 465.2.c.3 / c.4 make the bill the sum of the minimums — Keeper of Masks + a Sprite is 12 to clear, not 10; a body entering mid-combat gets the Defender designation at the next Cleanup, before 465.2 resolves (319.6 + 323.2.a), which is how a hidden Keeper lands inside the opponent's attack. 144 + 144.2 + 420.3.a: an Empowered "when I move, draw" unit already draws once a turn by standard-moving alone; an effect mover such as Forgotten Signpost is worth exactly +1 card a turn, and 420.1's "between two Locations" means the exhausted fuel must stand somewhere the mover is not. `SFD-048 Stellacorn Herder` prints "When I move, draw 1" with no Empower gate. 356.4.e's worked example IS Eager Apprentice + Sky Splitter (356.4.c.1: the player orders the discounts). Do NOT source "a copy inherits [Temporary]" to 477.1.b.1.b's example — the card copied there is Honest Broker, which has no [Temporary]; the keyword comes from Deceiver's own "Give it [Temporary]". The reading stands on 477.1.b.1.a + 816.3.
- **R32 was looked at and NOT filed (2026-09-06, #62 §6):** whether a Svellsongur copy of `SFD-050 Azir, Ascendant`'s ability carries its own *"Use only once per turn"* count. It blocks nothing (SFD-050 is in no entry and no synergy rule), the clause has exactly ONE hit in the whole pool, `data/errata.json` does not touch the card, and there is a legal ordering that avoids it — 103.2.b allows three copies, so two Azir are two swaps with no question asked. Both readings and the evidence are in `docs/phase0/walks/2026-09-06-issue62-r32-once-per-turn.md`; the walk's own vote would be A (377.2 makes *"use"* the verb of activating and 377.2.b evaluates the condition on *"the ability in question"*, singular; 383.3.e.1 and 371.1 count per ability; and where the rules wanted to declare a duplicate redundant they said so, at 810.2). **476.1 does not decide it** — it governs the application of effects in layers, not the activation of abilities, and reaching for it here is the citation error the 2026-09-04 audit found three times.
- Before filing a new numbered rules reading, try a different LEGAL ORDERING of the same cards first — reorder the chain, resolve before responding, pick a different target, split one window into two. `grand-plaza-loop-time-warp` was blocked on a mistarget reading (filed as R29, 2026-09-04) that vanished once Shadow's Call was simply resolved before Sacrifice instead of in response to it; **813.2** is the direct source for this and 813.1.b is not (upgraded 2026-09-12, #191 batch 20): 813.1.b is about what Reaction GRANTS, while 813.2 is about what it does not RESTRICT - *"The corresponding card or effect with this keyword is not restricted to Closed States or Showdowns. This permission is inclusive of all other timings and options available to the ability as written, Action`s permissions, or by default."* So a Reaction spell never has to be a response. An entry that stands on sequencing beats one that stands on an unruled interpretation, and every reading filed is a question the user has to answer.
- Sources carry their evidence: a `Source` may hold `quote` (the verbatim passage the entry stands on) and `accessed` (the date somebody on this project opened the url and read it there). Fill both whenever you open a page. The 2026-09-04 audit read the two TCGplayer articles end to end and confirmed 9 of 9 citations, but it also found three entries pointing at `infinite.tcgplayer.com/article/...`, which answers 200 to a HEAD and serves a CAPTCHA wall; the readable form is `www.tcgplayer.com/content/article/...`. Ask a reader proxy for the WHOLE page, never a keyword extract — a narrow prompt once made a session call a real quote fabricated.
- **Reddit is closed to this machine as of 2026-09-06.** The 2026-09-04 route (`old.reddit.com` + in-page `fetch` of `.json`) died first ("accounts are required to access old Reddit"); the morning-of-2026-09-06 route (`www.reddit.com` `.json` through the browser's request context) died within hours; by the afternoon curl, r.jina.ai, headless Playwright, HEADED Chrome with the MCP profile's cookies, and the redlib/safereddit mirrors all answer 403 or a "Prove your humanity" CAPTCHA — the one case where the browser rule stops. So a Reddit thread is citable only if the user opens it and pastes the text; otherwise verify the CLAIM against the corpus and Core Rules and cite that instead (stronger evidence anyway). The sub is `r/riftboundtcg`. #46 reports a community list of infinites in thread `1ryj4fw` — unread, treat as a lead. YouTube IS readable without login: `yt-dlp --skip-download --write-auto-sub` on `ytsearchN:` queries gives titles and transcripts; #46 read 37 of them, and a video is cited with URL + timestamp + the subtitle line. **Tournament lists (2026-09-06, rc-mine3):** `piltoverarchive.com/tournaments` (not `/events`) exposes 147 tournaments and 7,605 registered lists through its external tRPC/API, which answers ONLY with the header `Origin: https://piltoverarchive.com`; Riot's six "<City>'s Top Decks" articles on playriftbound.com carry 257 full lists in the dialect `parseDeckText` already reads (253 parse clean, 4 have transcription errata: missing battlefields, a Rune Pool duplicating battlefields, `Trapping Ground`); and riftbound.gg has NO anti-bot at all except its `/decks/<slug>` pages (Cloudflare challenge; the same list is readable through the API) — plain `curl` gets 200 on `/`, `/tournaments/` and `/tournaments/<slug>/`, and `api.dotgg.gg/cgfw/gettournaments`, `/gettournament`, `/getdeck` serve the lists including Vendetta, which Piltover Archive lacks. The full pass (#89) read 8,307 lists from 107 events; 53.8% carry at least one catalogued line complete, 72 of 180 entries appear, 108 never do, and `lux-infinite-energy` shows up in 7 registered lists (once by two independent routes). The tournament link in Piltover Archive lives only in the deck NAME. **Mining pass of the evening of 2026-09-06 (#121):** `api.dotgg.gg/cgfw/gettournaments` tops out at events of 2026-08-29 whatever the query parameters, and Piltover Archive exposes no tournament LISTING endpoint (404/403; only known-UUID deck lookups), so lists published after that date are not discoverable from here until one of them moves; the `yt-dlp` on this machine (2026.08.19) has no `ytsearchdate:` scheme, so search with `ytsearchN:` and filter upload dates by hand; TCGplayer through `r.jina.ai` still works, and its Sep 2 Ornn article's "six might" is CORRECT — #121 called it an arithmetic error by forgetting Brutalizer's printed Might Bonus (`mightBonus: 1` in cards.json; 137.3 / 477.3.d) on top of its conditional +2: 3 + 1 + 2 = 6. A `mightBonus` is a printed trait every ledger must add; the syn8 batch caught it by opening the source itself. Positions and win rates are never recorded (legal posture); what a list proves is that a catalogued line was registered at event X.
- A community deck NAME is not a claim you can walk. Issue #16's candidate #9 was "Viktor infinite without the banned Stealthy Pursuer"; the actual Rift Mana list is `garen-fiora-malzahar-facebreaker-recruits` card for card, predates the ban by five months, and never held Pursuer. Read the decklist before believing a description of it — and when a community report contradicts itself on domain identity (the RQ Vancouver Gutter Palace report names both a Jhin legend and a Chaos card), record the discrepancy instead of picking a side.
- **A re-attach to the same host is a no-op, so every "attach an Equipment to me" payoff needs a SECOND HOST (#168, 2026-09-07).** 434.1.f: *"Attaching a card to a new Top-Most Card will cause it to Detach from the card to which it is currently Attached"*; 434.1.g: *"Attaching a card to its current Top-Most Card will not have any effect"*; 434.1.h: *"If a Game Effect instructs a player to Attach a card to its current Top-Most Card, nothing additional happens."* The pool has exactly two such payoffs, swept on the corpus - `SFD-049 Aphelios, Exalted` (*"When you attach an Equipment to me, choose one that hasn't been chosen this turn"*) and `SFD-119 Jax, Unrelenting` (*"When you attach an Equipment to me, you may pay 1 Energy to draw 1"*) - and neither is pumped by bouncing one Equipment onto the same carrier: the cycle has to be host -> other host -> host, so one free attach ([Weaponmaster], whose reminder text *"even if it's already attached"* only permits taking the Equipment off ANOTHER unit) plus one paid [Equip] is ONE trigger a turn unless a second Equipment or `SFD-208 Forge of the Fluft` supplies the other leg. 818.1 keeps every attach an Activated Ability, so no play trigger rides along.
- **A hidden card is a [Reaction] card, Nami is the swept exception to "a Hold-triggered ready is a no-op", and a stun MUTES without SOFTENING (#169 batch 2, 2026-09-07).** The tail of 811.1.b was quoted by no entry before: the keyword is functionally short for *"... Beginning on the next turn, this gains [Reaction] and you may play this, ignoring its base cost"* - so EVERY face-down card, unit or gear or spell, is a [Reaction] card from the following turn (813.1.c.1: any Closed State on any player's turn), and a hidden UNIT therefore enters mid-combat on the OPPONENT's turn for 0 Energy; entries that treat a hidden play as a your-turn action are under-reading it. `UNL-052 Nami, Headstrong` (*"When I hold, the next time you play a unit this turn, ready it and [Buff] it"*) is the one card that escapes the no-op rule, because that rule is only 315.1.b readying everything before 315.2 fires the Hold and so only reaches a ready that RESOLVES inside 315.2 - Nami's Hold arms a DELAYED effect that resolves in the Main Phase (316), i.e. free [Accelerate] on one unit a turn plus a real 702 buff counter; swept on the corpus, the other Hold-and-ready rows produce no ready at all (SFD-027 and UNL-016 say "I enter ready", a play-time property and not a ready per 415.1; UNL-048 and UNL-199 play tokens printed ready). State the no-op with this exception attached, as with Lee Sin and Spiderling. And 423.1.b (*"A Stunned Unit does not contribute its might to damage in the combat damage step"*) against 423.1.c (*"A Stunned Unit must still have damage applied to it equal to, or greater than, its full might value to be killed"*): a stun never lowers the price of removal, and 423.1.a.2 clears it at cleanup step 3d, so nothing it does crosses the turn boundary. Companion, reusable: a body arriving by EFFECT MOVE mid-combat takes its designation at the very next Cleanup (319.8 *"After a Move is completed"* + 323.2.a + 464.2.c.3.a) and IS in that combat's damage step, while 814.1.c makes [Shield] *"While I am a defender, I have +X [M]"* - worth zero on offence, so a Tank is one size attacking and another defending.
- **Shrinking the defender is the excess-damage lever nobody had pulled, a renewable trigger source is still one activation a turn when the payoff costs an exhaust, and "+N Might this turn" has no cap (#170 batch 2, 2026-09-07).** 465.2.c.4 caps assignment at the minimum lethal and 465.2.c reads CURRENT Might on both sides, so lowering the garrison raises excess damage exactly as raising the attacker does and costs far less - a buff is fixed at +1 (703 *"Each Buff individually contributes +1 Might to a Unit"*) while `OGN-116 Thousand-Tailed Watcher` is -3 on EVERY enemy unit: M8 Tryndamere into M4 + M3 has excess 1 and misses his threshold of 5, and after the Watcher both defenders sit at 1 (477.3.b snapshots the floor) for excess 6, with the swing back down from 7 to 2. Floored against unfloored is the whole choice of reducer, and `SFD-066 Frigid Touch` (no floor, [Reaction]) is bought AFTER seeing the garrison (813.1.c.1 + 312.2.c) while a play-trigger reducer must be committed in the Main Phase. Second: `VEN-143 Master of Shadows` loots with *"Disempower me, [exhaust]: Discard 1, then draw 1"*, so 441.1.b (no re-empowering) and 315.1.b (readied only in YOUR Awakening) make a second banish the same turn worth nothing - **the value of a renewable trigger source is a payoff EVERY TURN, never more payoffs per turn**, the mirror of the #160 finding on doubled "you may exhaust me to..." Hold triggers. Third: *"give me +N Might this turn"* is a continuous modifier applied at 477.3.e.1 and NOT a 702 Buff counter, so 702.3's one-per-unit cap and 703's fixed +1 are both silent and instances stack uncapped; the pool prints exactly 8 `when you play a spell` cards (swept 2026-09-07) - five gate on a cost or spend threshold (OGS-006, OGS-021, UNL-005, UNL-181, UNL-211), one is opponent-turn only (SFD-063), and exactly two are unconditional: `OGN-103 Ravenbloom Student` (+1, Mind) and `UNL-149 Diana, No Longer Human` (+2, Chaos), both in zero entries before. And the measurement lesson that came with them: **`Card.type` is a string ARRAY**, so a probe written as `type === "legend"` returns zero for every card in the pool - a "nothing in the catalogue does X" claim is a MEASUREMENT, and one written against the wrong field fails silently and reads as a discovery. The true count is 73 `uses[]` rows with role `legend` over the catalogue, not 0.
