- **An exhaust in the payoff caps a "becomes Mighty" legend at one rune a turn, and 415.3.a is the only lever on that rate; [Equip] has no exhaust and shuttles all turn (#168 batch 3, 2026-09-07).** Both become-Mighty legends pay their OWN exhaust, so N crossings or N Mighty plays in one turn are worth ONE rune - of the 296 pairs in the matrix's rows 45-47 almost all are notables, not entries. What DOES change the rate is 415.3.a (*"A player Readies all non-spell Game Objects they Control during the Awakening Phase on their turn"*): an exhaust spent on the OPPONENT's turn is refunded at your own Awaken, so the honest doubling is an off-turn crossing (a [Reaction] pump) or an off-turn PLAY ([Ambush], 822.1.b, which grants [Reaction] while being played to a battlefield where you control units). Companion distinction: a *"+N Might this turn"* is a REPEATABLE Mighty crossing because 317.2.c expires it at cleanup step 3d and the body crosses again next turn, while a BUFF is a one-time crossing - 703 fixes it at +1 and 702.3 blocks a second, so a buffed Might-4 body crosses 5 once per PLACEMENT - refined 2026-09-07 by #187: 702.2.b removes the counter and 702.3.a blocks only a buff on a body that already HAS one, and 476.3's second worked example names Fiora, Victorious and has her fall *"directly from 6 Might with three keywords to 4 Might with no keywords"*, so a spent buff can be re-placed and the line re-crossed. **CORRECTED 2026-09-07 (#170 batch 5): an already-attached Equipment can NEVER pay its own [Equip] again.** 818.1.a puts Equip on the card, and an Equipment prints *"[Equip] <cost> (reminder) [Effect] <effect text>"* - the [Effect] marker delimits the Effect Text, so [Equip] sits in the PRINTED RULES TEXT, which 718.2 makes Inactive while attached (*"While in this state, the card's printed Rules Text is Inactive"*) while 718.3 appends only the Effect Text to the carrier; 721.2 then says *"Inactive Abilities do not trigger, do not apply, and cannot be activated."* The printed carve-out proves it: [Weaponmaster] has to say *"even if it's already attached"*. So [Equip] is payable once PER ATTACHMENT and the only legal relocations while attached are ATTACH effects. **Refined 2026-09-07 by #187: it is a one-way door out of the ATTACHMENT, not out of the game.** 718.1 - *"A card remains in this state until Detached"* - binds 718.2 and 721.2 to an ATTACHED card only; 719.5 detaches everything when the Top-Most Card leaves the board; and 457.1 then recalls the un-attached gear to base at the next Cleanup, with a DYING CARRIER as its own worked example. So losing the carrier costs one [Equip] cost and not the card, for every Equipment in the pool. The removal of `prize-of-progress-hexplate-equip-shuttle` still stands, because that line wanted repeated activations WHILE ATTACHED. 719.5.a gives the detach ORDER to the controller of the departing card. The relocations proper are still ATTACH effects (`SFD-193 Grandmaster at Arms`, `SFD-208 Forge of the Fluft`, `SFD-024 Rell, Magnetic`, Aphelios' trigger), with 434.1.f detaching from the old carrier in the same action. The earlier text here said 818.1.b's missing exhaust let one Equipment SHUTTLE between two hosts for as many activations as you had Power; that is wrong and it had one entry standing on it (`prize-of-progress-hexplate-equip-shuttle`, removed and replaced by a Gold-token line, since a repeatable "activated ability of a gear" must be a gear that is NEVER attached). The 434.1.g half - a re-attach to the same host is a no-op - still stands. Last: 809.1.c makes [Deflect] *"an additional cost ... to play"*, so granting Deflect in response to a spell already on the Chain taxes nothing; it is the one keyword that has to be in place before the opponent acts.
- **Run the "you" check on a battlefield before assuming it needs Control, and nothing in the pool accumulates damage across a round (#171 batch 2, 2026-09-07).** 190.6.d blanks a battlefield's text through that WORD alone, so **TWENTY-SIX of the 64 non-token battlefields print no `you` or `your` at all, and 22 of those are playable** (re-measured over `cards.json` on 2026-09-09 by #191 batch 4 and independently by the manager; this line said SIX until then, which was the subset that actively PAYS somebody rather than the class 190.6.d cannot reach). The 26, with the four banned in both formats marked: `OGN-276 Aspirant's Climb` (BANNED), `OGN-277 Back-Alley Bar`, `OGN-284 Obelisk of Power` (BANNED), `OGN-290 The Arena's Greatest` (BANNED), `OGN-292 The Dreaming Tree` (BANNED), `OGN-294 Trifarian War Camp`, `OGN-295 Vilemaw's Lair`, `OGN-296 Void Gate`, `OGN-297 Windswept Hillock`, `SFD-209 Forgotten Monument`, `SFD-216 Rockfall Path`, `UNL-205 Abandoned Hall`, `UNL-206 Altar of Blood`, `UNL-208 Black Flame Altar`, `UNL-210 Forbidding Waste`, `UNL-212 Frozen Fortress`, `UNL-213 Gardens of Becoming`, `UNL-214 Ripper's Bay`, `UNL-215 Star Spring`, `UNL-218 Valley of Idols`, `VEN-157 Dragon Roost`, `VEN-158 Heisho, Shell of the World`, `VEN-159 Kinkou Temple`, `VEN-160 Mystic Vortex`, `VEN-164 Sandswept Tomb`, `VEN-166 Threshold of the Gray`. A literal-word sweep is not the whole test: 190.6.d also blanks an IMPLIED `you`, so `OGN-277 Back-Alley Bar` (*"give it +1 Might"*, no named actor) still needs reading, while a pure static needs nobody. 143.3.b, never cited here before: *"Damage is Healed from Units at two specific times"* - 143.3.b.1 *"At the end of each player's turn"*, 143.3.b.2 *"During a Combat Cleanup"* - so **every repeating 1-damage source is a Might FILTER and never a clock** (walked and refused: Frozen Fortress cannot feed `UNL-174 Shard of Undoing`, because a 1-Might body sent there dies at the OPPONENT's Beginning Phase, which comes first, and a 2-Might body never dies on a second tick). `UNL-216 The Academy` GRANTS [Repeat] to a spell that prints none, strictly wider than `SFD-211 Marai Spire`'s discount on the fourteen that do, and 485.4.a + 103.4.c make the two battlefields mutually exclusive. 355.2.b (*"Some Game Effects may grant players permission to play Units to locations that are not normally Valid"*) is the paragraph behind every "play me somewhere unusual" card, and with 190.3.a.1 + 464.2.c.1 a unit PLAYED to an occupied enemy battlefield is the Attacker with no move and no 144.2 exhaust - `VEN-157 Dragon Roost` extends the `SFD-093 Dauntless Vanguard` trick to every Dragon in the deck for 2 rainbow Power. And R27 makes a Reflection 0 Might while 143.2.a + 465.2.c.4 already charge an attacker one damage for a 0-Might body, so granting it [Shield 1] adds nothing: **price a wall in damage, not in Might.**
- **[Hidden] is capped by the BOARD, 811.1.d never reaches the watcher, and "ignoring its base cost" leaves an optional additional cost payable (#169 batch 3, 2026-09-07).** 811.1.b hides *"at a battlefield you control that doesn't already have a facedown card hidden there"* - one facedown per battlefield, two battlefields in a Duel (485.4) - so every "when you play a card from face down" trigger (`UNL-023 Katarina`, `OGN-167 Ember Monk`, `SFD-121 Black Market Broker`) fires AT MOST TWICE A TURN however many [Hidden] cards the list runs, and only once against a single controlled battlefield; all three read like engines that scale with Hidden density and none does, and 103.2.b has nothing to do with it. 811.1.d binds the HIDDEN CARD and never the watcher: 811.1.d.1 puts the permanent at that battlefield and 811.1.d.2 confines the targets of its PLAY EFFECTS there, but Katarina's "deal 2 to an enemy unit" and the Ember Monk's "+2 Might" are their own Triggered Abilities watching an event, not play effects of the hidden card, so they reach anywhere on the board - half the value of the family is that asymmetry. 356.1.b.3 prints the worked example for the cost half: *"They ignore Legion Rearguard's Base Cost of 2 Energy, but the optional additional cost of 1 Energy and 1 Fury Power is added to its Total Cost and must be paid"*, so a card played free from face down can still buy its own rider (`UNL-028 Pyke, Dockside Butcher` pays 1 Fury for "ready me and +2 Might" off a 0-Energy play), and that generalises to every "ignoring its cost" effect in the pool. Citation upgrade: **204.3.a is the cleaner source for "a cost at the start of a Triggered Ability's effect is its base cost, paid to finalize"** - it says it first and carries its own worked example (Overzealous Fan: *"In order to finalize the ability to the chain, its controller must kill Overzealous Fan"*), with 204.3.b for the costs that are paid at resolution instead; 383.3.b says the same thing later.
- **The same death event collapses under one wording and multiplies under another, and Riot's own example paraphrases a card it names (#168 batch 4, 2026-09-07).** 383.1.b's worked example IS `OGN-118 Wraith of Echoes`: *"If an ability triggers 'the [Nth] time' something happens and that trigger condition is met multiple times simultaneously, the ability's controller picks one of those instances ... The ability triggers only once"* - so his best partner is whatever extracts most from a SINGLE death, never a mass killer, while `UNL-068 Spectral Centaur` (*"When another friendly unit dies, give me +2 Might this turn"*, no Nth-time wording) takes 383.3.d and pays +6 for three simultaneous deaths. Same event, opposite collapse behaviour - read the wording, not the event. **And the rules example misquotes the card it names**: 383.1.b writes *"The first time ANOTHER friendly unit dies each turn"* while OGN-118 prints *"The first time a friendly unit dies each turn"*, so under rule 002 the printed text governs and HIS OWN death pays. That is a discrepancy in Riot's rules text, registered here and NEVER put into `data/errata.json` (the find-string failure is the only mechanism keeping our card text honest). Two swept facts from the same batch: `OGN-228 Vanguard Helm` is the ONLY card in the pool that recovers a buff from a corpse (`grep -inE "dies.*buff|buff.*dies"` returns exactly one row), which is what makes it worth a slot when 702.3 caps a body at one buff - and `OGN-269 The Boss` switches it off, the 808.1.d.1 shape applied to a buff. Every XP sink in the pool is bounded (`grep -nE "Spend [0-9]+ XP"` = 7 cards, 8 costs: five capped by their own exhaust, two by 702.3 because they only buff themselves, `UNL-126 Megatusk` by 810.1.c.3 since [Ganking] is a permission and a second grant does nothing), and Chaos prints no [Level] static at all - **but an identity is a PAIR (103.1.b), so "no outlet" was the wrong conclusion and is corrected here (2026-09-07)**: swept for `[Level`, `UNL-016 Scorchclaw` is the ONLY [Level] card in Fury, so under any Fury/Chaos legend `UNL-129 Vicious Snapjaws` - the pool's only death-to-XP faucet - has exactly ONE outlet, and `UNL-126 Megatusk`, the only Spend-XP card in those two domains, is the anti-synergy that switches that rung off (824.1.d + 730.2). The original sentence was scoped to MONO-Chaos, which is #98's "a legal-in-domain-X count is not well formed" biting a line in this file.
- **A score-gated resource is bankable, a [Unique] one-shot is worth more when a partner takes the loan over, and the Brush is a second sweeper answer that still cannot save a Plaza (#170 batch 3, 2026-09-07).** `SFD-201 Chem-Baroness` reads *"While your score is within 3 points of the Victory Score, your Gold [Add] an additional 1 Energy"*, and 187.5 prints the Gold as *"[Reaction] Kill this, [exhaust]: [Add] rainbow"* with nothing forcing you to cash it - the clause reads its object at the moment of USE, not of creation, so a Gold minted on turn 3 and killed at 5 points pays 1 Power AND 1 Energy and the correct line is to HOARD, the opposite of every other ramp engine; the threshold is format-sensitive (5 of 8 under 194.3, 8 of 11 under 489.3). 825.3.a allows one `SFD-192 Shurelya's Requiem` and only its PLAY readies, so the mass ready is once per game - but `UNL-075 Gustwalker` banks 2 XP per Conquer ([Hunt 2], 823.1.c.1 *"When I Conquer or Hold, my controller gains X XP"*) and his own [Level 3] grants +1 Might and [Ganking], so the double-Conquer turn the Requiem buys pays 4 XP and hands him the borrowed keyword for good (824.1.d + 730.2, as long as no XP is spent). A spell that chooses TWO friendly units chooses each of them ONCE - they are distinct Game Objects - so a plural never doubles a "when you choose me" payoff; only [Repeat] does (820.1.b, 820.2.a), and over `SFD-142 Jae Medarda`'s match list exactly two cards do it, `SFD-151 Bonds of Strength` (Order) and `SFD-034 Feral Strength` (Calm). **Refinement to the #159 Azir line, which said Soul Shepherd is "the only fix in the pool" for 1-Might tokens:** that stands FOR THE PLAZA, and only there. 187.7 makes a Bird token 1 Might with [Deflect], 143.2.a kills on damage equalling its Might and `OGN-133 Flurry of Blades` is E1 for 1 to every unit at battlefields, so `UNL-T03 Brush` (*"Bird, Cat, Dog, Poro, and Ivern units here have +1 Might"*, 187.8) is a SECOND answer in Calm/Order - but scoped to those five tags, at that battlefield only, and never for `OGN-293 The Grand Plaza`, whose text 438.5 removes when the Brush replaces it. `UNL-077 Soul Shepherd` remains the only PERMANENT fix, and it is Mind - narrowed twice now: `OGN-266 Siphon Power` (Mind/Order, E2 + 1 Power, [Reaction], *"+1 Might to friendly units there this turn"*) is a ONE-TURN, ONE-BATTLEFIELD answer in the same identity, bought in RESPONSE to the sweeper already on the Chain (813.1.c.1), and the Brush is a tag-scoped answer in Calm/Order. **State that beside its complement or the next session will refuse a legal line**: Soul Shepherd is a REPAIR for 1-Might bodies, but 187.3 makes a Sand Soldier a 2 MIGHT token, so `SFD-197 / SFD-247 Emperor of the Sands` is a faucet that never needed repairing - and he is Calm/Order, so a Calm/Order token line does not need the Mind card at all (added 2026-09-07).
- **"Choose" is four different game actions under one word, and 811.1.d.2's worked example is Tideturner by name (#169 batch 4, 2026-09-07).** It is the highest false-positive rate in the whole cause/trigger matrix: eleven of the 27 partners across the three choose-a-friendly trigger cards do not fire them, in three shapes a predicate must all exclude - (a) cards that choose in a HAND or a TRASH rather than a friendly unit on the board (`UNL-139 Bone Skewer`, `VEN-104`, `VEN-114`); (b) cards that REMOVE what they chose before the payoff can land (`VEN-106 Wind and Ghosts` banishes or bounces it, so a Blade Dancer ready has nothing left to ready); (c) `SFD-142 Jae Medarda` prints *"When you choose me WITH A SPELL"*, which makes all eight of his unit / gear / battlefield / legend choosers inert. And the escape clause of 811.1.d.2 that #62 found has a named example after all: *"Example: Tideturner is a unit with Hidden and 'When you play me, you may choose a unit you control at another location...' Because its play effect has a targeting restriction that can never be fulfilled by a unit at its battlefield, its target may be chosen freely from among the available options"* - so `OGN-199 Tideturner` is the one hidden play effect in the pool that provably reaches the whole board without an argument.
- **Check 383.3.b BEFORE writing the steps of any "you may <cost> to <effect>" trigger, and measure a "one rainbow less" discount against the POWER component (#171 batch 3, 2026-09-07).** The cost in that wording is the trigger's BASE COST and 383.3.b.1 pays it to FINALIZE - before anything else in the simultaneous batch resolves - so **a buff, token or resource produced by ANOTHER trigger of the SAME event can never pay it, whatever order 383.3.d puts them in**; the honest shape is an engine offset by one event, which is exactly `monastery-hirana-warmogs-conquer-draw` (the obvious walk, "order Warmog's first and spend the buff it just placed", is wrong; from the SECOND Conquer on it is a card per Conquer forever, self-clearing under 702.3 + 702.2.b). Next: **no card in the pool prints a rainbow Power COST**, so a "one rainbow less" discount always comes off a DOMAIN rune and must be priced against the heaviest Power component and never against the Energy - `VEN-136 Ambessa, Respected and Feared` carries one of three unit [Empower] costs with two domain runes (E1 + 2 Order; the others are `VEN-086 Gangplank, Naval` at two Body runes and no Energy, and `VEN-114 Kharox` at E6 + two Chaos - the "only" here was corrected by the #181 cross-audit, 2026-09-07), and 441.1.b + 441.2 make that rebate once per body and permanent. Two sweeps: `VEN-159 Kinkou Temple` (*"Units here with [Tank] have +1 Might"*) is the only card in the pool that PAYS for [Tank], and since a wall is priced in damage (815.1.c.1 + 815.1.c.2 + 465.2.c.4) a flat +1 Might per body DOUBLES it - four 1-Might Birds absorb four elsewhere and eight there - while `VEN-004 Dune Surfer` (*"You ignore [Tank] while assigning combat damage here"*) is the pool's only answer to such a wall. And the pool prints exactly THREE cost-INCREASE rows: `VEN-045 Helm of Suppression` (the only asymmetric one), `VEN-160 Mystic Vortex` (*"During showdowns here, cards with [Reaction] cost rainbow more"* - the only card that makes a [Hidden] card cost anything, since 811.1.b plays it ignoring its base cost while the tail of 811.1.b hands it [Reaction]) and `UNL-219 Vaults of Helia`, which taxes its own controller.
- **416.1.c makes a symmetric recycle ONE event, and the constructive half of the spent-vs-costs split is cheap spells with [Repeat] (#169 batch 5, 2026-09-07).** 416.1.c: *"Each player Recycles cards to their own Main Deck and Rune Deck, regardless of which player is instructed to perform the Recycle action."* So `OGN-244 Divine Judgment` is ONE `OGN-235 Karma, Channeler` trigger and not two, and `UNL-103 Disposal Order` - which the matrix lists as a Karma partner - recycles only opponents' cards to THEIR decks and fires her never (`OGN-212 Forge of the Future` fires her only if one of its four is your own card). Karma counts recycle EVENTS and not cards (*"when you recycle one or more cards"*), so two cheap recycles beat one big one, and her printed *"(Runes aren't cards.)"* with 161.2.b keeps every 164.2.b rune recycle in the game invisible to her - the predicate trap the synergy layer has to exclude. And the half of the #98 split nobody had used constructively: *"if you spent N or more"* reads the RUNE POOL (166.2 + 203.1 + 356.4.b) while a [Repeat] cost counts in full (820.1.c.1), so **a cheap spell with an additional cost crosses a threshold that a discounted expensive spell fails** - a `UNL-211 Forgotten Library` deck wants cheap spells with [Repeat], not big ones.
- **A REFUSAL IS SCOPED TO THE REASON THAT WAS GIVEN, and a rune-denominated discount is a COUNT and not a payment (#170 batch 4, 2026-09-07).** `OGN-288 Startipped Peak` (*"When you hold here, you may channel 1 rune exhausted"*) was refused twice - by #102 and again by the #153 orphan-rules walk - on the ground that the channelled rune is neither readied that turn (315.2.b.2 runs after 315.1 has finished) nor spendable before 167 empties the pool, so it is "one extra rune, as Energy, from the following turn". Every word of that is true and neither clause bites, because the rule's own two partners do not want mana: `VEN-032 Frostcoat Mother` and `VEN-050 Grumpy Rockbear` print *"[Empower] 12 Energy. This ability costs 1 Energy less for each rune you CONTROL"* - an exhausted rune on the board is a rune you control, so the discount lands in full the same turn. **Before treating a recorded refusal as general, check whether the partner list was ever read**; this is the #59 Garbage Grabber lesson in its general form. Companion arithmetic: 161.2.a fixes the Rune Deck at *"Exactly 12 Rune cards"* so "runes you control" tops out at 12 and 356.6 floors the [Empower] at 0, but 164.2.b recycles a rune for Power and 161.2.b returns it *"to the Rune Deck, not the Main Deck"* - so **every Power you pay slides you back up a rune-denominated cost**, and #44's "free floor of 2 Power a turn" is free in mana and NOT free for anything priced in runes controlled; the Peak's Hold is the only free repeating refill in the pool. Two more from the batch: the pool's only two *"While I'm buffed"* statics (`OGN-065 Wizened Elder`, `OGN-125 Bilgewater Bully`) resolve in OPPOSITE directions - `OGN-223 Peak Guardian` places a counter on the whole garrison and never touches it (702.2.a), while `OGN-207 Call to Glory`'s free mode spends one (702.2.b) and so switches off the very statics it was paired with. And `UNL-037 Shadow Watcher` (*"If a friendly unit died during your Beginning Phase this turn, I enter ready"*) states a condition ORDINARY PLAY CANNOT MEET - 315.1 and 315.2 both precede 316 and combat is a Main Phase event - so its only repeatable cause in the pool is a [Temporary] body dying at 816.1.b, i.e. a scheduled death (`UNL-048 Trevor Snoozebottom` mints a fresh one on every Hold).
- **808.1.c is the paragraph that separates a Deathknell from a "when another unit dies" trigger, and a departure rider never pays the body that walked in (#168 batch 5, 2026-09-07).** 808.1.c: *"It is functionally short for 'When I die, [Effect].'"*, with 808.1.d making the Trigger *"the Permanent being Killed and sent to the Trash"* - so a trigger watching OTHER deaths is not a Deathknell and `OGN-236 Karthus, Eternal` (*"Your [Deathknell] effects trigger an additional time"*) does not touch it: Karthus doubles `OGN-239 Machine Evangel`'s three Recruits to six and leaves `OGN-246 Viktor, Leader`'s one at one, so that board is SEVEN, not eight. Next, 144.4 with 810.1.c.3: a battlefield carrying a *"when a unit moves FROM here"* rider pays only bodies an EFFECT drags off it, never the body that walked in from the base, so **a mover without [Ganking] can never collect its own departure rider** - and `SFD-079 Bard, Mercurial` moves the board to an OPEN battlefield where 344.2 assigns no damage, which makes a +1 Might per departure worth exactly zero on that line. Third, the general form of the [Temporary]-death note: the Awaken is a phase EARLIER than the Beginning Phase (315.1 before 315.2), so **any payoff whose cost is its own exhaust is free against ANY scheduled Beginning-Phase death**, not only a [Temporary] one - `UNL-209 Dusk Rose Lab` included. Two citation corrections from the same batch, caught before shipping: **416.1** (not 416.1.a) is where *"puts it on the bottom of the corresponding deck"* lives - 416.1.a only says Main Deck cards go to the Main Deck - and 808.1.d.2 is not the sentence an earlier draft attributed to it.
- **A readier or a copier pays only where the effect has a HARD CAP per use, and the Svellsongur partner that survives eightfold copying is the one whose every clause is optional or re-chosen (#170 batch 5, 2026-09-07).** The negative half is already recorded (a multiplier is worth nothing on `SFD-117 Ancient Henge`, `SFD-083 Hextech Anomaly` or `SFD-177 Azir`, which print "any amount" / "any number"); the positive half is how you PICK a partner out of a 76-row list - `VEN-060 Sky Cruiser`'s *"Deal 4"* and `SFD-078 Temporal Portal`'s *"the NEXT spell you play this turn"* are both hard-capped per use, so a second activation is a second full effect. For Svellsongur: `UNL-051 Ivern, Nurturer` says *"You may reveal a unit from among them and draw it"* - 431.1.c keeps looking and revealing free of Burn Out and the *"may"* makes eight instances eight DECLINABLE draws, where eight of a bare *"draw 1"* (`UNL-060 Vilemaw`, `VEN-042 Shen, Scourge of Shadows`) is a forced Burn Out - and his buff names *"a friendly unit"*, re-chosen each instance, so 702.3.a never flattens it: eight buffs on eight bodies. Every "multiplier is a no-op on a fixed target" case in the ledger turns on the buffer naming ONE body. **The one shape Svellsongur cannot multiply is a per-EVENT delayed trigger**: eight copies of `UNL-052 Nami, Headstrong` all watch the same "next unit you play", and 415.1.c + 702.3.a make seven of them do nothing. And `SFD-221 Veiled Temple` pays PER CHANGE OF HANDS, never per turn - 466.5 establishes Control *"if they didn't already control this Battlefield"* and 348.2.a carries the identical clause for a Showdown - so holding it is worth zero.
- **Void Gate is a SEVENTH Bonus Damage source, granted [Assault] sums like granted [Shield], and giving one body [Tank] protects every OTHER body (#171 batch 4, 2026-09-07).** The project's Bonus Damage sweep was written as "all six cards say *your* spells and abilities", and that phrasing is exactly why it could not see `OGN-296 Void Gate` (*"Spells and abilities deal 1 Bonus Damage to units here"*) - worded for the LOCATION, which is also why it never says "you", why 190.6.d cannot blank it, and why it arms the OPPONENT too. 715.2 pays it per target (*"If the Deal action has multiple targets, the amount of Damage dealt to each target is increased by Bonus Damage individually and separately"*), 714 sums instances, 715.4 keeps it augmenting a Deal rather than creating one, and 713 + 417.6.c keep the whole family disjoint from excess damage. Next: 807.2 sums granted [Assault] exactly as 814.2 sums granted [Shield] (Riot's example: Petty Officer + Cleave = Assault 4), and `SFD-183 Purifier`'s *"Your Equipment EACH give [Assault]"* is a per-EQUIPMENT grant, so stacking Equipment on one body stacks the keyword as well as the Might Bonuses (434.1.d). 187.4 gives a Mech token its tag BY RULE (*"A 3 [M] Mech token is a domainless unit token with 3 Might and the Mech tag"*), so every "your Mechs have X" static reaches tokens with no extra text - and `SFD-071 Breakneck Mech` grants them [Ganking], the one keyword that answers the base-born-token bottleneck, since 144.4.a/b confine a base-born token to one battlefield forever and 144.4.c.1 does not. Finally, **granting [Tank] to one body is a protection for every OTHER body** (815.1.c.2: units without Tank are invalid assignments until every Tank unit has lethal), a toll booth and not a shield; `VEN-147 Eye of Twilight` grants it at [Action] speed and 806.1.c.2 says outright *"This can be activated during showdowns on any player's turn"*, so the grant lands AFTER 464.2.c.3 designates the attackers and their summed Might is countable.
- **Bonus Damage is paid per DEAL ACTION and a second source is merely additive, Challenge-family damage is UNIT damage, and only Rengar can reinforce the battlefield you are attacking (#173 batch 1, 2026-09-07).** 715.2's own worked example names `OGN-020 Annie, Fiery`, and it settles the family: *"the amount of Damage dealt to each target is increased by Bonus Damage individually and separately"*, so the payoff scales with how many Deal ACTIONS a spell performs, never with how big they are - while 714 (*"If more than one instance of Bonus Damage is applied or granted to a Deal action, all instances are summed and applied once"*) makes a second Bonus Damage card worth +1 more per action and never a doubling. 417.6.b.3 is the clean citation for the #119 disjointness, and its worked example is Challenge: *"The damage that Challenge causes to be dealt is dealt by the chosen units, not by Challenge"* - so the whole "they deal damage equal to their Mights to each other" family is UNIT damage, which a blanket "prevent all spell and ability damage" fog (`OGN-145 Unyielding Spirit`) leaves untouched. Next, 355.2.a (a play goes to *"the controller's Base or a battlefield the controller controls"*) is the general form of why nothing can reinforce the battlefield you are ATTACKING - refuse-bucket C - and **the count depends entirely on the predicate (re-swept 2026-09-12): FIVE cards own text reaches a battlefield holding ENEMY units** - `OGN-161 Deadbloom Predator`, `SFD-093 Dauntless Vanguard`, `SFD-025 Rengar, Pouncing`, Arachnoid Horror and `UNL-120 Rengar, Trophy Hunter`, all five already catalogued - while exactly ONE survives the narrower question of arriving inside an ALREADY-OPENED combat, **and the reason is worth more than either number — but it is one paragraph short of the truth, corrected 2026-09-12: 806.1.c.1 makes [Action] read *"This can be played during showdowns on any player`s turn"*, and `SFD-111 Here to Help` is an Action spell that plays a unit into an OPEN Showdown on the opponent`s turn. Rengar is alone on the ATTACKING side because of 355.2.a, not because of the keyword** — Here to Help says *"to a battlefield you control"* and 464.2.c.2 makes the attacked battlefield one you do not. The finer split this catalogue already owns is 358.4: in a Showdown OPEN state the card being played is the one that CLOSES it, so [Action] suffices; once the state is CLOSED only [Reaction] gets in. So Here to Help deploys a blocker after attackers are declared and can never be held as an answer to a removal spell already on the Chain, and Rengar can.** Arachnoid Horror second sentence is also a 355.2.b grant to the whole board, a third route beside Ava Achiever. The Reaction card is: `SFD-025 Rengar, Pouncing`, *"I can be played to a battlefield you're attacking"*, who arrives inside the open combat and is in that damage step by 464.2.c.3.a + 319.6 + 323.2.a. **But that is a claim about card text and not about the game**: 355.2.b grants permission from ANOTHER card's effect, and `OGN-107 Ava Achiever` is a second route - *"When I attack, you may pay 1 Mind to play a card with [Hidden] from your hand, ignoring its cost. If it's a unit, play it HERE"* - with 359.3.f.2 reading "here" at execution, where she is standing. Rengar walks himself in; Ava throws someone ELSE in, free (narrowed 2026-09-07 by the #170 walk). 464.2.c.3.a is the paragraph for both: *"If a Unit ... becomes present at this Battlefield after this moment, it will gain the Attacker or Defender designation during the Cleanup phase following the action that caused it to become present."* And 811.1.c.1 cuts both ways on Ava - she plays from HAND, so 811.1.d.2's confinement never engages and her payload's targets are unrestricted, but the three *"when you play a card from face down"* watchers never see her. A *"Units can't move to base"* prohibition (`SFD-014 Minotaur Reckoner`) beats EFFECT moves as well as Standard ones - 420.1 defines Moving generally, 420.2.b makes the Standard Move merely one thing that *"may also cause Movement"*, 449 has spells and abilities cause Moves, and 054.1 makes forbid beat permit - so it kills the whole evacuation family (323.6 needs the garrison to leave), and the only escape is 455, a Recall being a relocation *"without it being a Move"*. And the constructive half of #95's `VEN-022 Endless Riches` note: it exempts the Main Deck by name (*"If a card would go to your trash from anywhere other than your Main Deck, banish it instead"*), which is why a self-mill is its only renewable input, and 431.2.b makes a Burn Out recycle the trash - i.e. your hand - back into the deck.
- **Two drawbacks can cancel exactly, 206 at its widest survives where #47's Empower family died, and the pool has ONE Power-to-Energy conversion (#170 batch 6 and #171 batch 5, 2026-09-07).** `OGN-109 Dr. Mundo, Expert` reads his Might off your trash AND recycles 3 from it every Beginning Phase with no "may", so alone he eats his own stat line; `VEN-165 Shadow Temple` burns 3 in on every Hold and alone is a fuse, since 431.1.b burns you out past the deck and 431.2.b then recycles the whole trash back, resetting every trash payoff in the deck. Together they are net ZERO on both zones forever - and the phase order is the right way round, 315.2.a.1 (*"At the start of Beginning Phase game effects take place"*) preceding 315.2.b's Hold, so the recycle empties before the Burn refills. Next, the discount family that SURVIVES on the other side of the line #47 drew: `SFD-055 Needlessly Large Yordle` prints E10 + 3 Calm Power and floors at E6 + 1 Calm, and `VEN-145 Curator of the Sands` still reads the PRINTED 10 and still fires, because 206.1's *"unless otherwise specified"* is specified only by Empower (827.1.c.3). The rune rebate that pays for it is ENERGY and not Power: 164.2 gives a Basic Rune exactly two abilities, and only 164.2.a costs an exhaust - 164.2.b's cost is the RECYCLE - so an already-tapped rune still pays Power the same turn and readying runes never widens the free Power floor of #44. `VEN-141 Butcher of the Sands` is the pool's ONLY Power-to-Energy conversion (11 corpus rows carry `[Add] :rb_energy`; its cost is the only one containing a rune), the mirror of `SFD-117 Ancient Henge` - and honestly, as an exchange it is the WRONG direction against the ~9-Energy-per-Power figure; what it sells is that the Power came out of runes with no second use. And `VEN-076 Repair Specialist` is one of TWO [Assault] carriers whose X is a quantity you BUILD rather than a printed constant (the other is `SFD-131 Ancient Warmonger`, *"[Assault] equal to the number of enemy units here"* - a defender-side count; corrected by the #181 cross-audit, 2026-09-07) (807.1.b formats it *"Assault [X]"* with X *"referenced in the functional text"*), counting *"gear you control"* - which never has to be ATTACHED, since 818.1 makes [Equip] a separate Activated Ability nothing in the line pays and 323.7's Cleanup recall changes where an unattached gear is, not who controls it. Milestone from the same pair of batches: **all 49 legend NAMES in the pool now carry at least one entry.**
- **Coverage is keyed on NAME+TYPE, not on base code, and when the Core Rules name a card that card is a walk waiting to happen (#173/#174, 2026-09-07).** `CardIndex.equivalents` (`src/cards.ts:124`) resolves a base code to every printing sharing its normalised name and type, and `matchDeck` builds `owned` through it, so a REPRINT of a catalogued card is NOT uncatalogued: walking it produces an entry the matcher already covers. **How big the correction is depends entirely on the lane, so MEASURE it, never assume it** (all three measured 2026-09-07): Calm/Mind/Order 182 by base code against 154 by name, a ~15% over-count; the whole deckable pool 443 against 358, 19.2%; mono-Body 38 against 36, 5.3%; and 0 of the 64 non-token battlefields share a name at all. **One reported figure in this family was wrong and is corrected here**: a walk reported Fury/Body/Chaos as 158 against 158, a ZERO gap; re-measured by the manager on 2026-09-07 at catalogue 560 the block is 121 against 113, a **6.6%** gap, with eight multi-base names in it (fury rune, body rune, chaos rune, Pouty Poro, Draven Showboat, Seal of Rage, Mystic Poro, Gangplank Naval). The mono-Body half of that same block measured 5.3% independently and matched the manager exactly, which is what exposed the block figure. A lane that reports a zero gap should be re-run before it is quoted. The 104 multi-base names concentrate in runes, Seals, promos and legends, which is also why three basic runes in one lane were already covered THROUGH `equivalents()`. Do not carry the 15% as a general discount (SFD-224 Aphelios Exalted = SFD-049, SFD-226 Seal of Focus = OGN-081, SFD-225 Irelia Fervent = SFD-057, VEN-170 Shen Scourge of Shadows = VEN-042, which `shen-duo-mutual-hold` already walks). **Any "cards in no entry" census must fold printings by name+type first** (`.scratch/uncat-cmo.mjs` does it and carries the reason in a comment). And a cheap vein nobody had mined: the Core Rules' own worked examples name real cards, and three of one batch's five entries stood on cards named in the rules that sat in NO entry - 477.3.c is Last Stand answered by Eclipse, 808.1.d.1 is a Zhonya's Hourglass save, 814.2 is Stalwart Poro under Block. Grep the rules file for card names before hunting anywhere else.
- **Challenge-family damage is UNIT damage so the pool's one blanket fog leaves it working, 740.2.a is the half of "alone" that decides how to switch Fiora on, and a token swarm has two problems that one domain cannot both fix (#173 batch 2, #170 batch 7, 2026-09-07).** 417.6.b.3: *"When a spell or ability specifies a Unit as the source of the Damage for the Deal action, it is not in addition to the spell or ability that instructed it"*, with Challenge as its worked example (*"The damage that Challenge causes to be dealt is dealt by the chosen units, not by Challenge"*) - so the seven "they deal damage equal to their Mights to each other" cards are UNIT damage, and `OGN-145 Unyielding Spirit` (the pool's only blanket *"prevent all spell and ability damage this turn"*) blanks every burn spell in the format while leaving that whole removal suite intact; 437.4 makes the prevention total, so no kill event is generated at all, and 437.5 keeps combat assignment legal. 740.2.a is the missing half of #119's Fiora, Peerless question: *"A unit is alone when there are no other friendly units at the same location"*, and 740.2.b needs BOTH sides down to one - so a board RESET is the enabler and a bigger garrison is anti-synergic. 434.1.c (*"The Top-Most card has all Effect Text of all cards Attached to it appended to its Rules Text"*) decides who "me" is inside an attachment's text: `UNL-019 Blighted Battleaxe`'s "deal 4 to me" hits the CARRIER, and `SFD-107 Strike Down`'s mandatory detach removes the text before the trigger can exist. And the token fork, which generalises to every token line: the bodies cannot MOVE the turn they are minted (143.4, whose widest alteration in the pool is `OGN-011 Magma Wurm`, *"Other friendly units enter ready"* - every friendly unit, not only tokens) and the bodies DIE to one damage (143.2.a against `OGN-133 Flurry of Blades`, whose sweep kills any number of 1-Might tokens simultaneously, answered only by `UNL-077 Soul Shepherd`'s +1) - **CORRECTED 2026-09-09: that is not a hard fork.** `UNL-077 Soul Shepherd` is the only board-wide, TOKEN-scoped +1, but it is not the only permanent one - swept for a static, non-"this turn" +N Might on friendly units, the pool prints SEVEN: `OGN-151 Lee Sin, Centered` (Body, +2, buffed units at his battlefield), `OGN-243 Darius, Executioner` and `OGS-013 Garen, Commander` (both Order, +1, *"other friendly units have +1 Might HERE"* - the identical sentence), `UNL-077 Soul Shepherd` (Mind, +1, token units, board-wide), `UNL-147 Baron Nashor` (Chaos, +2, other friendly units), `UNL-191 Wuju Master` (Calm/Body legend, +1 at [Level 6]) and `VEN-018 Rage Amplifier` (Fury GEAR, *"Your units have +1 Might"*, rising to +2 while Empowered). So an Order swarm has two fixes of its own, and a FURY token engine can take Magma Wurm AND the Rage Amplifier - `hextech-formula-rage-amplifier-free-empower` is already in the catalogue. The original sentence generalised from one card to the whole pool without a sweep.
- **340.1 vs 383.3.b decides whether ORDERING can save a line, exactly two cards ignore [Deflect], and the base-code census over-counts by 19.2% (#170 batch 8, #171 batch 6, 2026-09-07).** 340.1 (*"The newest Finalized Chain Item resolves"*) means a trigger you want to resolve FIRST must be placed LAST under 383.3.d - **but only when its cost is paid on resolution**; if the cost sits right after a leading "you may", 383.3.b makes it the trigger's base cost paid at FINALIZATION and no ordering can help (`minefield-last-rites-mill-reanimate` is the first case, `monastery-hirana-warmogs-conquer-draw` the second, and the two entries state the difference in each other's terms). Exactly TWO cards in the pool ignore [Deflect], swept: `VEN-061 Decree of Insight` (*"Ignore [Deflect] while paying this spell's cost"*, its own cost only) and `VEN-158 Heisho, Shell of the World` (*"Players ignore [Deflect] while paying for spells and abilities choosing something here"* - both players) - and since 809.1.c charges *"for each time they choose"*, the tax scales with CHOICES, so 355.10.d's programmatically-selected half of the removal pool never pays it and Heisho is only worth it for the choosing half. `OGN-294 Trifarian War Camp` is the only COLOURLESS answer to the 1-Might-per-body problem (Soul Shepherd is Mind, Kinkou Temple needs [Tank]), and 485.4.a + 103.4.c mean it can never share a board with the Grand Plaza - a swarm's obvious payoff. Census numbers, measured: 443 uncatalogued deckable cards by base code against 358 by name+type (19.2% over-count), and ZERO of the 64 non-token battlefields share a name, so the correction moves a battlefield census by nothing. And a standing rule for parallel walks, learned when three cards went from free to catalogued while one batch was being written: **re-validate immediately before REPORTING, not immediately after writing.**
- **[Repeat] gives one extra execution PER INSTANCE, not per spell, and 740.4 is the single home for "costs within instructions" (#173 batch 3, 2026-09-07).** 820.1.c.2: *"If a spell or ability has more than one instance of Repeat, each Cost may be paid or not paid individually"*, with 820.1.c.3 *"Each Repeat Cost can be paid only a single time"* - so the standing line from #97 ("[Repeat] gives EXACTLY one extra execution, 820.1.b") is true per instance, and TWO instances are THREE executions. `UNL-146 Syndra, Transcendent` is the only card in the pool that GRANTS a [Repeat] instance, so she is the only route to three - and she does it for every spell played in her window, which is a synergy-rule lead and not a pile of entries. 740.4 gathers the "costs within instructions" family in one place and splits it by where the cost sits: 740.4.a.1 *"For spells, costs within instructions are paid on resolution of the spell"* - which is 204.3.b's worked example, `OGN-268 Bullet Time`, *"Pay any amount of [A] to deal that much"*, the one spell in the pool SIZED AFTER the opponent has already responded (it is also a Signature card, tagged Miss Fortune, so its line is one legend) - against 740.4.a.2, *"For triggered abilities, costs within instructions that appear in the first part of the trigger effect are paid on finalization"*, which is 204.3.a / 383.3.b reached from the other end. Two more: a printed *"I can't be readied"* costs a unit only what its EXHAUST would have bought, so `UNL-144 Maduli`, whose only ability costs 1 Chaos Power and no exhaust, loses nothing (144.2 and 420.3.a put the exhaust on the Standard Move alone, and combat does not read exhaustion) - and the constructive use of #159's Azir refusal is that **an empty board is a free Conquer through 344.2, never 323.9**: twelve runes give 12 Energy and 164.2.b recycles three ALREADY EXHAUSTED ones for the 3 Power, so `SFD-147 Downwell`'s turn is exactly affordable.
- **An Equipment has TWO costs and no 1-Energy rebate can ever free the attach, a `needs` tag cannot carry an extra condition, and the battlefield vein closed with seven refusals (#171 batch 7, 2026-09-07).** 818.1 makes [Equip] an Activated Ability separate from the card's play cost, so a battlefield that zeroes a PLAY cost (`SFD-213 Ornn's Forge`, one E1 gear a turn) never touches the attach - and measured over the corpus, **every [Equip] cost in the pool that contains Energy also contains a rune** (six at E1 + one domain rune, one per domain, plus `UNL-188 Hextech Gauntlets` at E3 + rainbow), which is why a 1-Energy rebate can never free one and is what refuses `VEN-161 Piltovan Forge`. [Quick-Draw] is the exception that deletes the attach cost outright: 819.1.d makes it short for *"[Reaction]"* plus *"When you play this, attach it to a Unit you control"*, so no Equip cost is ever determined. **A `needs` feature tag cannot carry an extra condition** - `SFD-218 Sunken Temple` wants a 5+ Might conqueror while `sprite-queen-targon-peak-conquer-cycle` satisfies `conquer-engine` with a 3-Might Sprite, so read the card's condition and never the tag. `OGN-291 The Candlelit Sanctum` REFINES the standing "no deck-manipulation card turns a draw probability into a certainty": it cannot tutor a named card, but recycling one of the top two leaves the other on top by your own ordering and 315.4.b draws it - and 431.1.c keeps it safe with the deck empty where drawing (431.1.a) and milling (431.1.b) are not. And the "four or fewer runes" family is GAMEABLE while its sibling is not: 164.2.b's cost is the RECYCLE, so already-tapped runes can be dumped for Power in your own Main Phase (`VEN-001 Baccai Sandspinner`, `VEN-016 Eclipse Dragon`, `VEN-162 Protective Sands`, all Fury), whereas *"fewer runes than an opponent at the start of your Beginning Phase"* (`VEN-005`, `VEN-006`) is read at 315.2.a before 315.3.b channels two - and the cost the reading glosses over is that 315.3.b rebuilds at two a turn, so every rune dumped is two Channel Phases. The seven battlefield refusals with their paragraphs are in section 22 of `docs/phase0/walks/2026-09-07-uncatalogued-cards.md`; **all 64 non-token battlefields and all 49 legend names are now catalogued, staged or refused by rule.**
- **"Kill all DAMAGED units" reads a MARKER and not a threshold, [Deflect] is a TAX while "can't be chosen" is a PROHIBITION, and a Conquer point is banked at the Score (#173 batch 4, 2026-09-07).** 143.2.a is death BY damage (marked damage at or above Might), but a kill BY EFFECT keyed on the word *"damaged"* ignores Might entirely - so two points off a 3-Energy gear kill a 10-Might body, and every pinger in the pool is a universal removal enabler beside `OGN-159 Warwick, Hunter` (*"When I attack, kill all damaged enemy units here"*). The clock is 466.1.a.1, *"Insert '3c. Heal all Units.'"* - the combat cleanup wipes marked damage, so the ping and the attack are the same Main Phase, and 383.2.a.1 + 464.2.c.3 put the mark before the Attacker DESIGNATION, never in response. Next, two things the catalogue's notables have treated as interchangeable and which are different in kind: 809.1.c prices [Deflect] as extra Power **per choice** (809.1.c.1: any domain), so a player with the Power simply pays, while `SFD-105 Ruin Runner`'s *"I can't be chosen by enemy spells and abilities"* is absolute under 054.1 - and 355.10.d consults NEITHER, so every programmatically-selected sweeper in the pool goes through both. Third, the split inside the word "exhausted": an exhausted rune is POWER NOW and ENERGY NEXT TURN, because 164.2.a costs the rune's exhaust (nothing until 415.3.a) while 164.2.b costs none (one Power of its domain immediately, at Reaction speed) - that is the real payoff of every *"channel N runes exhausted"* card, and 430.3's *"channel as many as possible"* is the rule behind `OGN-138 Catalyst of Aeons`' printed cantrip mode. Fourth, the load-bearing half of 471.1.a.1 (*"points Gained from sources that are not Conquer are not beholden to these restrictions"*) is its converse: **a CONQUER engine is exactly the kind that can stall at seven** under 471.1.b, and readers assume it the other way round. And fifth: a Conquer point is banked at the Score and surviving on the battlefield is not required - 469.1 + 471.1 Gain it at the Conquer and 323.6 strips the battlefield when the body leaves without unbanking it, which is what `OGN-035 Vayne, Hunter` is built on (*"When I conquer, you may pay 1 Energy to return me to my owner's hand"*), reaching the two-Conquer ceiling on one unit card beside `OGN-021 Sun Disc`.
- **Order has TWO untargetable-removal paragraphs and they are different, 185.2.d puts a token INSIDE the rules for its type, and a body can arrive after the attackers are declared (#180 batches 2-3, 2026-09-07).** 355.10.e is a set *"chosen in whole or in part by other players"* and its printed example PARAPHRASES `OGN-209 Cull the Weak` rather than quoting it (**corrected 2026-09-12**: the rule writes *"Each player kills a unit they control"* and the card prints *"Each player kills one of their units"* — same effect, different words, and the old wording here invited a walker to quote the RULE and attribute it to the CARD). The example does spell out *"does not target"*, and **411.1 is where the rules quote that card exactly**: *"Cull the Weak is a spell that reads `Each player kills one of their units.`"*; 355.10.f is an object *"identified in an instruction that a player 'must' complete"*, whose example is *"You must recycle one of your runes"* - that is `UNL-170 Atakhan`'s *"the defender must kill one of their units here"*, firing on EVERY attack, and it is also why `OGN-287 Sigil of the Storm`'s forced recycle chooses nothing. Both dodge 809.1.c's [Deflect] tax and both leave the CHOICE with the opponent, which is a limit to state and not to hide. 185.2.d is the opposite side of every token trap this file records (416.1 is defined over CARDS, 186.1 keeps a token out of the trash): *"Tokens have a type. They follow all rules for their type unless otherwise specified"*, so a token unit IS a unit for a cost that says *"kill a friendly unit"* and 203.3 never bites - which makes `VEN-124 Escaped Grayback`'s [Empower] free. Do not conflate the two sides when reading a cost. And 319.6 + 323.2.a give a body that enters MID-COMBAT the Defender designation before 465.2 resolves, while 143.4's entering exhausted costs nothing on defence (neither 464.2.c.3 nor 465.2.b filters by state) - so `OGN-241 Shen, Kinkou` ([Reaction], and his own parenthetical grants *"including to a battlefield you control"*) lands a 5-Might [Tank] AFTER the attackers are declared, and 815.1.c.2 then makes every other body of yours an invalid assignment until he has taken exactly five. That damage redirect now has THREE distinct routes in the catalogue - a legend's exhaust, a free Quick-Draw attach, and a whole extra body - and each entry names the other two. Two more terms found DEFINED in the 740 glossary that this project had been deriving from card text: 740.3.a for *"tie"* (it names the combat-cleanup step) and 740.2.a for *"alone"* (*"no other friendly units at the same location"*), which is strictly better than the reminder text on Lonely Poro that the catalogue was citing.
- **205 is the POSITIVE MIRROR of the 383.3.b trap, a mass buffer and a buff-recovery card are opposites, and NO card carries two of Bird/Cat/Dog/Poro (#170 batch 2 of the successor, 2026-09-07).** This file records three times that a resource produced by another trigger of the SAME event can never fund a 383.3.b base cost, because 383.3.b.1 demands payment at finalization. 205 names the family where it DOES work: *"An instruction that requires a player to pay resources or spend counters or XP that does not also have a linked Effect, is not a Cost"*, and its worked example is *"When I attack, you may pay [4][C]. If you do, kill a unit here"* - **"you may X. If you do, Y" is a game action checked at RESOLUTION**, so 383.3.d (the controller orders simultaneous triggers) with 340.1 lets one trigger of an event feed another; `OGN-056 Adaptatron` eating the Gold that `SFD-220 Treasure Hoard` minted on the same Conquer is the worked case. General form: a resource from the same event cannot pay a BASE COST but can satisfy a 205 GAME ACTION, and a resource from an EARLIER PHASE pays either (a Hold buff at 315.2.b.2 funds a Main-Phase Conquer from the first one). Next, 702.3.a makes a mass buffer and a buff-RECOVERY card opposites: `OGN-228 Vanguard Helm` (*"When a buffed friendly unit dies, buff ANOTHER friendly unit"*) is a no-op behind `OGN-223 Peak Guardian`'s mass buff, since no counter is placed on an already-buffed body - which is why its entry pairs with `OGN-141 Kinkou Monk` (*"buff up to TWO other"*) instead; the same rule means doubling a buff SOURCE readies `SFD-047 Simian Ancestor` zero extra times, and only a buff SPENDER repeats him. For an XP sink that buffs ITSELF (`UNL-162 Enthralling Protector`, `UNL-102 Crowd Favorite`, both *"Spend 2 XP: [Buff] me"*), the bound is 702.3 and not the XP: worth 2 XP once, forever, until a spender removes the counter - which names the bound that "every XP sink in the pool is bounded" only asserted, and the rate is derivable (315.2.b.2 Holds every battlefield you control, 485.4 puts two on a Duel table, so [Hunt] is 2 XP a turn = exactly one buff a turn). And measured over `data/cards.json`: **67 printings carry at least one of Bird/Cat/Dog/Poro and ZERO carry two**, so `UNL-046 Friendship`'s +4 and `UNL-177 Ivern, Friend to All`'s four-tag Hold each cost four separate bodies - there is no two-for-one, which is the first thing anyone tries in that vein.
- **Mass [Vision] is ONE look with N re-rolls, a REFUND is not a discount so no floor reaches it, and the [Level] census tops at one card (#170 batch 3 of the successor, 2026-09-07).** 817.2.b, cited by no entry before: *"If the player does not recycle the top card and nothing else happens in between the triggers resolving, each instance of Vision will see the same card"* - so four Birds under a mass-Vision anchor buy a four-deep DIG WITH A STOP BUTTON (817.2.a lets you choose per instance), never four free cards; and 431.1.c with 431.1.c.1 makes it the one card-selection effect that is safe in an empty-deck shell, where every draw is 431.1.a. Next, 356.4.e is normally cited to stop two discounts stacking below a floor; read the other way, **a refund is not a discount and no floor reaches it** - `OGN-140 Herald of Scales`' floored -2 Energy on Dragons plus `UNL-104 Gentle Gemdragon`'s *"ready up to 2 runes"* (2 Energy back by 164.2.a) is a flat 4 off every Dragon printed at E4+, and makes `SFD-006 Eager Drakehound` (E3, the cheapest Dragon in the pool) ENERGY-POSITIVE. Level census, measured on the corpus: six [Level 3], nine [Level 6], four [Level 11] and exactly ONE [Level 16] - `UNL-059 Master Yi, Unstoppable`, in zero entries until now - and his rungs say *"less INSTEAD"*, so they REPLACE rather than stack (E12+3 Calm / E10+2 / E8+1 / E6+0 at 11 XP; stacking them reads 0 Energy and is wrong by six). And the highest uncovered-partner rule in `synergies.json` is mostly dead on inspection: `pit-crew-gear-ready` (104 partners on a bare "any gear" predicate) reduces to gear played in YOUR Main Phase after the anchor has exhausted, which refuses a Hold-triggered gear play (315.1.b + 415.1.c) and an opponent-turn one (144.1.b + 415.3.a) by name; the surviving fuel is the Gold gear TOKEN, which 350.2 + 187.5 make a gear play and 103.2.b does not cap. Third confirmation that **a high uncovered-partner count is a warning flag, not a prize.**
- **"I" in an appended Effect Text is the CARRIER, "this" is the attached gear, and the summing-keyword family is exactly three (#180 batch 5, 2026-09-07).** 053.1 (*"Units and legends say 'I,' 'me,' etc."*) with 053.2 (*"Gear and spells say 'this.'"*) and 136.2.d - *"Effect Text may refer to 'this' or to the name of the Attached game object that appended the Effect Text. Doing so refers to the Attached game object and not the Top-Most Card"*, whose worked examples are Guardian Angel and Brutalizer - settle every attachment's pronouns: read with 136.2.b and 434.1.e, **no gear-kill line in the pool can ever cash an [Effect] Deathknell**, because `SFD-172 Sacred Shears` draws for its CARRIER's death and never for its own. That refuses a whole family by rule rather than by arithmetic. 809.2 sums granted [Deflect] beside 807.2 ([Assault]) and 814.2 ([Shield]) — and the family is FOUR paragraphs, not three: **823.2 sums granted [Hunt] the same way** — and the 2026-09-09 sweep that reported ZERO cards granting Hunt was wrong, **corrected 2026-09-12: the zero is an artifact of a TEXT-level predicate.** 434.1.c and 718.5.g append an Equipment`s Effect Text to its carrier`s Rules Text, so **an Equipment whose Effect half is a bare keyword IS a granter**, and a sweep for *"give X"* or *"have X"* cannot see it. Measured over `cards.json`, EIGHT Equipment do it — `SFD-009 Serrated Dirk` (Assault 2), `SFD-033 Doran`s Shield` (Tank), `SFD-064 Cloth Armor` (Shield 2), `SFD-090 The Zero Drive` and `SFD-172 Sacred Shears` (Deathknell), `SFD-102 Hexdrinker` (Deflect), `SFD-133 Boots of Swiftness` (Ganking) and **`UNL-096 Hunter`s Machete`, which grants [Hunt]** — so 823.2 has a live case and all four summing rules are usable. The precedent for reading appended text as a grant is this file`s own and is load-bearing: three Svellsongur on Shen are priced here as *"eight instances = Shield 8"*, which is 814.2 summing keywords that arrived through exactly this channel. **Reusable form: a "does the pool grant X" sweep must read the EFFECT half of every Equipment, not only card text**. All of them sum when granted from several sources, and [Deflect] is the only one that taxes the OPPONENT instead of adding Might. Three more first citations: 812.2 (*"All instances of Legion on cards a player controls are satisfied by that player playing a single card"*, with 812.1.c needing that card merely FINALIZED); 818.1.c.1, which puts a trigger fired by paying an [Equip] cost on the chain ABOVE the Equip itself; and 808.1.d.3, which is what lets a Deathknell read a state the body had before it left the board - the paragraph behind the pool's exactly two Empowered-gated Deathknells (`VEN-078`, `VEN-128`). Also swept: exactly three cards print a *"[Level N] I enter ready"* clause (`UNL-016`, `UNL-151`, `UNL-191`), and 824.1.d with 730.2 make that whole family ANTI-synergic with every XP sink - spending the XP switches the rung off. And `OGN-236 Karthus, Eternal` is mono-ORDER, checked against `cards.json`, which is what keeps the pool's Deathknell multiplier out of every Fury/Chaos death outlet.
- **The rules-name vein has 18 cards left in it, a token is played but is never a CARD, and Iascylla schedules a defend trigger (#170 batch 6, 2026-09-07).** The cheapest vein in this project is still "grep the rules file for card names", and it is now measured with the name+type fold: **129 pool card names appear in the Core Rules text, 32 sit in NO entry, and after removing six Runes, three tokens and one banned card, 22 are real playable cards the rules point at by name** - four were used that batch, so **18 remain**, among them six WORKED EXAMPLES (`OGN-010 Legion Rearguard` at 356.1.b.3, `OGN-097 Blastcone Fae` at 811.1.d.2, `OGN-105 Singularity` at 715.2, `OGS-006 Lux, Illuminated` and `SFD-077 Rocket Barrage` at 206, `OGS-018 Tibbers` at 103.2.d.3) plus `OGN-058 Discipline`, `OGN-229 Vengeance`, `OGS-011 Flash`, `SFD-034 Feral Strength`, `SFD-186 Spinning Axe` and `SFD-194 Counter Strike`. Next, the NEGATIVE half of the token rule, which belongs beside the positive one this file already carries: 350.2 makes a token PLAYED, but 185.2.a keeps it from being a CARD - so *"each card you've played this turn"* (`SFD-012 Battering Ram`) and every [Legion] clause count ZERO tokens, and a token engine feeds neither. State the pair together. And 464.2.c.1 (*"The Attacker is the player whose unit(s) applied the Contested status to the Battlefield"*) makes `UNL-050 Iascylla` the pool's only free repeating way to SCHEDULE a defend trigger - dragging an enemy body onto your battlefield makes THEM the attacker on YOUR turn, so a *"when I defend"* payoff fires every turn instead of at the opponent's discretion, and 464.2.e.1 puts the Defender's triggers on the Chain last, which by 340.1 resolves them first. She is also the THIRD card that escapes "a Hold-triggered X is a no-op", for the same reason as `UNL-052 Nami`: the Hold arms a DELAYED effect that resolves at 316.4, in the Main Phase, rather than inside 315.2.
- **Prevent is the only save that MOVES nothing, [Repeat] on a modal spell buys both modes, and a "quote" field must be pasted (#170 batch 7, 2026-09-07).** 437.4 - *"Damage dealt to a Unit that has that all of that damage Prevented is not considered to have been dealt to it at all"* - with 437.5 and 437.5.a (damage is still ASSIGNED up to lethal counting the Prevent Value, then eaten, so it works inside the damage step at [Reaction] speed): no relocation, no exhaust, no kill event. That makes it the correct shield for any *"While I'm at a battlefield"* unit, where the heal/exhaust/RECALL family relocates to the base under 455 and switches the text off for a turn - `SFD-194 Counter Strike` is the card that does not, and it cantrips (it is also a Signature card tagged Jax, so its line is one legend). 820.2.a is what makes [Repeat] worth running on a MODAL spell: *"Choices made for the additional execution do not have to be the same as the choices made for the initial execution"*, and its worked example is Rocket Barrage - one card, both modes - while 820.1.b still caps it at exactly one extra execution, so no [Repeat] card is ever an engine. Removal census, members named: `OGN-229 Vengeance` (*"Kill a unit."*, Order E4 P2) is the only SINGLE-TARGET kill in the pool with neither a location clause nor any other gate - `UNL-180 The Ruination` also omits the clause but is a sweeper, `VEN-131 Decree of Unity` omits it but gates on an enemy Chaos unit or gear, and Hidden Blade, Blast of Power, Drag Under and Death from Below all say *"at a battlefield"* - and exactly three cards NAME a unit *"in a base"* as a target (`SFD-077 Rocket Barrage`, `SFD-116 Yone, Blademaster`, `UNL-182 Curtain Call`) - **but naming is not the only route, and the count of cards that REACH a base depends entirely on the predicate you sweep with - CORRECTED 2026-09-09, twice in one day.** A walk first raised it from nine to eleven; re-measured by the manager with `kill|deal N to (an) (enemy) unit` and NO `at a battlefield` / `here`, it is **SIXTEEN by that predicate and the predicate is still short by one (2026-09-12): `VEN-017 Morgana`s amount is not a NUMERAL** — she deals damage equal to damage already marked — and her instruction carries neither a location clause nor a *here*, so she reaches an enemy unit in a BASE and a `deal N` sweep cannot see her. Re-swept for a Deal instruction naming *"a unit"* with no location clause: 13 rows, ten of them genuine enemy-facing Deals (`OGN-029`, `OGN-248`, `OGN-252`, `OGS-022`, `SFD-077`, `UNL-020`, `UNL-023`, `UNL-067`, `UNL-073`, `VEN-017`). **This line has now been wrong at nine, at eleven and at sixteen, every time because of the predicate** — which is the whole argument for stating the predicate beside the number. Counting the original sweep, it is: `OGN-029 Falling Star`, `OGN-037 Immortal Phoenix`, `OGN-229 Vengeance`, `OGN-234 Harnessed Dragon`, `OGN-248 Icathian Rain`, `OGN-252 Super Mega Death Rocket!`, `OGS-022 Final Spark`, `SFD-077 Rocket Barrage`, `SFD-158 Sandshifter`, `UNL-020 Dancing Grenade`, `UNL-023 Katarina, Reckless`, `UNL-067 Ruined Rex`, `UNL-073 Deadly Flourish`, `UNL-118 Elder Dragon`, `UNL-164 Safety Inspector`, `VEN-154 Public Execution` - and a seventeenth, `VEN-131 Decree of Unity`, that a unit-only predicate cannot see because its sentence reads *"unit or gear"*. **The GATE is a separate axis from the location clause and that is where the earlier counts went wrong**: Vengeance is still the only one with neither a location clause NOR any other gate, while the Dragon is gated by costing E8 + 2 Power, Sandshifter by a Might 3 ceiling, Public Execution by needing a bigger friendly body, Safety Inspector by 3 XP and by 355.10.e handing the choice to the opponent, and Ruined Rex by having to die first. **State the predicate with the number or the number is meaningless** - this line has now been wrong at nine and at eleven, and the earlier text's own count of "six more" was measured on `deal` alone and never re-run after `kill` was added to the question** (corrected 2026-09-07 by #173): six more print damage to *"a unit"* with NEITHER *"at a battlefield"* nor *"here"* - `OGN-029 Falling Star`, `OGN-248 Icathian Rain`, `OGN-252 Super Mega Death Rocket!`, `OGS-022 Final Spark`, `UNL-020 Dancing Grenade`, `UNL-026 Xerath, Freed` - and an unrestricted *"a unit"* reaches a base as surely as a clause that says so. `OGN-070 Mageseeker Warden` still manufactures the zone most removal cannot see; it is just less airtight than three cards would make it. Swept alongside: every single-target Deal instruction in the pool prints 2, 3, 4, 5, 6 or 8, and the 8 is `OGS-022 Final Spark` alone. Standing note earned twice this walk: **a `quote` field must be PASTED from the rules file, never typed from memory** - three were paraphrases and were replaced before staging.
- **A [Tank]'s Might is a THRESHOLD, not an ordering, and [Assault] is printed Might on a bloodless conquer (#180 close-out, 2026-09-07).** 815.1.b is functionally *"I must be assigned LETHAL damage before any other unit with the same controller as me that does not have [Tank] during the Combat Damage step"*, and 815.1.c.2 makes every non-Tank body an INVALID assignment until it has - so **an attack whose summed Might is less than the Tank's kills nothing at all**, not merely the wrong thing; and 815.2 makes a second Tank grant redundant, which leaves the Tank's own Might as the only lever on a printed one. The [Assault] face of the empty-battlefield trap is 807.1.d, *"Being an attacker means the Unit has gained the Attacker designation during Combat"*: 323.9 stages no Combat without opposing units, so every Assault body and every *"while I'm attacking"* clause contributes only its printed Might on a bloodless conquer. And 155 (*"A spell can be played during an Open State outside of Showdowns on its controller's turn"*, first cited here) is what prices the [Action] keyword on a REMOVAL spell: `OGN-229 Vengeance` is cheaper and wider than `OGS-012 Blast of Power` and yet cannot take a battlefield, because the #118 removal-conquer chain needs the spell INSIDE an opened Combat. Three sweeps from the same close-out, members named: `UNL-161 Divining Shells` is the only Might pump in Order whose activation costs no Energy and no Power; `OGN-219 Vanguard Sergeant` is the only mono-Order card in the pool with NO rules text; and the Decree cycle is six cards in three mirrored pairs of opposed domains (Fury-Calm, Mind-Body, Chaos-Order) of which `VEN-131` alone kills outright and alone reaches a gear. **All 139 mono-Order base codes, all 64 non-token battlefields and all 49 legend names are now catalogued, staged or refused by rule.**
- **718.5.b is the exact complement of 718.2: an attached Equipment is text you cannot use and a card the opponent can still kill (#170 batch 8, 2026-09-07).** *"Attached cards still can be chosen or targeted by game effects while Attached"* - so the two facts belong in one sentence and do not conflict: its printed Rules Text is Inactive (718.2) and its [Equip] is unusable (721.2), but the CARD remains a legal target. That makes an [Action] gear kill the answer to the Equipment BURSTs, and it lands INSIDE the opened combat before 465.2.c reads the Might Bonus - 137.3.a stops the Bonus *"as soon as the card with the Might Bonus is no longer Attached"*, and 718.3 / 719.1 take the appended Effect Text away with it. `OGN-224 Salvage` (E2 + 1 Power, *"You may kill up to one gear. Draw 1."*) is the clean version and `SFD-160 Zaun Punk` the two-for-one; both were in ZERO entries. Companion shape from the same batch: `UNL-161 Divining Shells`' activation is *"Kill this, exhaust"*, so like [Equip] it is a ONE-WAY DOOR - one use, ever - which is worth checking on any "cheapest activation in its domain" claim.
- **The mutual-damage family walks past a [Tank] wall, and half of every exchange in it pays the OPPONENT (#186 batch 1, 2026-09-07).** 815.1.b and 815.1.c.2 are scoped by their own words to *"during the Combat Damage step"*, and 417.6.b.3's worked example is Challenge itself - so *"they deal damage equal to their Mights to each other"* is UNIT damage outside that step and a Tank wall does not constrain it at all; 465.2.c.4's minimum-lethal cap does not apply either, and 417.6.c keeps every point of it out of the excess-damage family. Six cards print the exact wording, measured on the corpus: `OGN-128`, `OGS-008`, `SFD-114`, `UNL-110`, `VEN-083` (mono-Body) and `OGN-258` (Calm/Body); `UNL-110 Clash of Giants` is the only one with no friendly-or-enemy restriction, so it can point two of the OPPONENT's bodies at each other. The catch, and it is printed on none of them: **417.6.b.4** (first cited here) makes *"the controller of the source of a Deal action responsible for that Deal action"*, with the example spelling out that the controller of the ENEMY unit is responsible for the damage their unit deals **and their "when you deal damage" triggers fire** - so half of every exchange in this family is the opponent dealing damage, with everything that implies. Two more from the same batch: `UNL-108 Wily Newtfish` is the ONLY card in the pool whose XP clause reads a TURN rather than a threshold (swept: one hit), which makes it the one XP payoff a SPENDING list can run - 824.1.d and 730.2 switch every [Level N] off when the XP is spent, and `UNL-094 Gemhand Hunter` carries one clause of each kind on the same card, so in one deck its Hunt half keeps paying while its Level half goes dark. And [Shield] and [Assault] Might is INVISIBLE outside a combat: 814.1.c and 807.1.c are conditional on a designation 464.2.c.3 hands out inside one, so a [Shield 2] body printed at Might 3 counts as a 3 for any Mighty-counting discount.
- **The uncited half of the rules file is the vein, not the card lists (#170 close-out, 2026-09-07).** Measured over `combos.json` + `synergies.json`: **207 of the Core Rules' 385 top-level rule numbers are cited by NOTHING in this project.** Most are structural, but every large finding of 2026-09-07 came from exactly there - 718.2 broke a shipped entry, 817.2.b inverted a whole family, 137.3.a made a Might Bonus permanent, 356.1.b.3 made a free play still buy its rider, 417.6.b.4 handed half a damage exchange to the opponent. The uncited blocks with real play content, in reading order: **443 SKIP** (its own worked example is *"They skip their next point from conquering this turn"* - a replacement effect on POINTS, cited by no entry), **432 Double**, **418 Heal** (418.1.a: *"If Damage is cleared for any reason it is considered Healing"*, so every heal is a watchable event), **451/452** (Non-Combat Showdown against a Combat opened from a Move - the #159 split at its source), **454/457 Recalls** (457: *"Gear can be Recalled"*, while every Recall finding here is about units), **473-478 Layers**, and the head of **701 Buffs**. Companion, now closed: the rules-NAMED-card vein is spent - 129 pool names appear in the rules text, 16 are uncatalogued, and after six Runes, three tokens and one banned card only THREE usable cards remain (`OGN-283 Navori Fighting Pit`, `OGS-011 Flash`, `SFD-034 Feral Strength`), so do not plan a slice around it. And the fifth and sixth proofs that **the uncovered-partner column ranks PREDICATE WIDTH and not opportunity**: `pit-crew-gear-ready` (106 = any gear) and `yordle-explorer-power-two` (82 = any printed Power >= 2), beside the four buff-supply rules and #161's Frigid Jewel and Prize of Progress. `fiora-buff-ready` shares the buff predicate at 98 and is CORRECT, because 709's Mighty crossing is exactly what a Might modifier does.
- **466.3.a is stricter than every "when I win a combat" reminder in the pool, and a trigger is PLACED at one moment and READ at another (#186 batch 2, 2026-09-07).** Winning a combat requires being the ONLY player with units remaining, and 466.3.d makes it *"No Result"* if units were recalled during step 3d of the Combat Cleanup, if both players have units present, or if neither does - and 466.1.a.2 recalls surviving ATTACKERS whenever Defenders remain. So a printed reminder of the shape *"I win if I remain after combat"* is a simplification, and **a defence that merely survives pays nothing**. Second, the distinction that a merged entry had blurred and a later walk caught: a trigger is PLACED at the Attacker designation (464.2.c.3) but READS its instruction on EXECUTION (359.3.f.2, worked examples: Yasuo, Remorseful mistargeting after his target goes home, and reading his current Might) - so a ping trigger placed SECOND under 383.3.d resolves FIRST under 340.1 and can mark damage that a kill trigger then sees, which the loose phrasing would have refused. Third, [Shield] Might is DECISIVE inside a combat and INVISIBLE outside one (814.1.c is conditional on the Defender designation), so a printed-3 body with [Shield 2] reads 5 for 465.2.c and 3 for any Main-Phase Mighty count - two entries now point in opposite directions on that keyword and name each other. And 053.2 decides which object an *"if Empowered"* clause asks about: a gear or spell says *"this"* meaning ITSELF, so one card doubles on its own Empowered state while another reads the target's. `OGN-161 Deadbloom Predator` is the SECOND card in the pool that attacks without moving (355.2.b + 190.3.a.1 + 464.2.c.1), beside `SFD-093 Dauntless Vanguard`.
- **A census must count over `cards.json` deduped by BASE, never over `poolOf` — that is what produced the bogus zero (#173, 2026-09-07).** `poolOf` (`src/builder.ts:136`) is `basesByName(...).values()` mapped to `bases[0]`, so **it already collapses by name**: a census built on it reports the name+type number in BOTH columns and its "naive base-code count" was never a base-code count at all. That is the whole explanation of the walk that reported Fury/Body/Chaos as 158 against 158, a zero gap, which this file repeated until it was re-measured. Counted properly at catalogue 564, the Fury/Chaos lane is 76 by base code against 72 by name+type, a 5.3% gap, with the four multi-base names NAMED (Pouty Poro, Draven Showboat, Seal of Rage, Mystic Poro). **And a regex on the singular verb misses cards**: this project's gear-removal inventory was built with `kill .* gear` and never saw `OGN-179 Acceptable Losses`, which prints *"Each player KILLS one of their gear"*; swept as `kills? .* gear`, the pool has exactly two "each player kills" rows (OGN-179 and `OGN-209 Cull the Weak`). 055.1 is the whole of that card - *"If all of a card's instructions are impossible, it is still played and resolved, but nothing happens"* - so in a list running ZERO gear the pool's only symmetric gear wipe is ONE-SIDED for 1 Energy, and 185.2.d making a Gold gear TOKEN legal fodder is why a Gold producer is anti-synergic with it.
- **807.3 turns granted [Assault] from a Might package into a REMOVAL package, and the Bonus Damage maximum in the pool is six Deal actions (#173 batch 1, 2026-09-07).** 807.3, cited by nothing before: *"Assault, and whether or not a unit has Assault, is a characteristic of the Unit and may be checked or referenced by other Game Effects"* - so a card can read the SUMMED keyword (807.2) as a NUMBER, with 807.1.b.3 making a bare [Assault] worth 1. Next, the family maximum, swept: exactly six cards deal damage to *"a unit"* with no *"at a battlefield"* and no *"here"* - `OGN-029 Falling Star`, `OGN-248 Icathian Rain`, `OGN-252 Super Mega Death Rocket!`, `OGS-022 Final Spark`, `UNL-020 Dancing Grenade`, `UNL-026 Xerath, Freed` - and `OGN-248` is the only one with more than two Deal actions, printing *"Deal 2 to a unit."* SIX TIMES; since 715.2 pays Bonus Damage per action and 714 keeps a second source additive, two sources are +2 per action and the ceiling is 24. None of the six has a location clause, so all of them reach a BASE. Third: 190.3.a.1 reads *"moving to OR BEING PLAYED TO"*, so a card that makes the OPPONENT play a unit at YOUR battlefield makes THEM the attacker on YOUR turn (464.2.c.1) - `UNL-139 Bone Skewer` is the only card that does it out of their HAND, for free, and it is a DEFENSIVE payoff only, since 466.5 gives Control just to a player who did not already have it. And 136.2.d cuts the other way from the Sacred Shears case when the attachment says *"I"*: `VEN-011 Pendulum Blade`'s *"+2 Might when I move to a battlefield"* is the CARRIER's, and with 144.4.c.1 one [Ganking] move is BOTH a departure and an arrival, so a departure rider and an arrival rider are paid by the same action.
- **[Weaponmaster] does NOT activate the Equip and does NOT choose the unit, so it fires none of the payoffs a paid [Equip] does (#186 close-out, 2026-09-07).** The keyword is nine clauses and two of them reprice it. 821.1.c makes it short for *"When you play me, you may choose a Card you control with the Equipment tag. **Necessary portions of its Rules Text are no longer Inactive if they are currently Inactive.** Pay the cost of its Equip ability, reduced by [A], to attach it to this unit"* - that middle sentence is the mechanism behind *"even if it's already attached"* against 718.2/434.1.e, and it is why the keyword is the ONE exception to the [Equip] one-way door. And 821.1.c.6 says outright: *"The Equip ability is not activated this way, and the unit with the Weaponmaster ability is not chosen."* So a [Weaponmaster] attach triggers **no** "when you use an activated ability of a gear" payoff and **no** "when you choose me" payoff, where a paid [Equip] triggers both (818.1.b.1 makes its choice a Target). Four 821.1.c paragraphs are first citations here; 821.1.c.7 also makes multiple instances trigger separately and choose different targets. Second, a clause of 811.1.b the reminder text omits: a card is hidden *"for as long as you control that battlefield"*, so 323.6 throws the facedown away the moment your garrison walks off to attack elsewhere - which makes a body that CANNOT leave (no move to base, no [Ganking], so 144.4 gives it nowhere to go) the cheapest anchor for a hidden card. And the full value of a rune channelled EXHAUSTED, stated once: 1 Power immediately (164.2.b, whose cost is the recycle and not an exhaust), 1 Energy from your next Awakening (415.3.a), and a rune you CONTROL the whole time - which is why a *"for each rune you control"* payoff is paid in full the same turn.
- **Two fleet rules earned by a near-miss, and the alarm that caught it (#186, 2026-09-07).** A batch script OVERWROTE a staging file instead of appending, which would have silently dropped five finished entries the manager had not merged yet. Nothing reached the repo, and what caught it was the census run immediately before reporting: **four spells REAPPEARED as uncovered, and that number can only go down.** So: **a staging script APPENDS, never overwrites**, and **re-validate immediately before REPORTING, not after writing** - the shared tree moves under you either way. A census that moves the wrong way is an alarm, not a rounding difference.
- **432 Doubling and 433 Swapping were cited by NOTHING, and both are printed traps (#187 batch 1, 2026-09-07).** 432.1: *"Doubling is the act of increasing a numeric attribute by an amount equal to that attribute's CURRENT value"*, and 432.1.a fixes that amount for the stated duration - its own worked example is a 3-Might body with [Shield 2] doubled as a Defender, ending *"After combat, Shield no longer applies, but the +5 Might from Last Stand does, so the unit's Might is 8."* So **a doubler played AFTER the Attacker/Defender designation is worth the whole [Assault] or [Shield] value more than the same card played before it**, and the increase survives the designation lapsing. Six cards in the pool double a Might and four already carried entries; none cited the block. 433 Swapping likewise: 433.1.b makes a swap a 2D swing (+D to the lower, -D to the higher), 433.1.a makes both FIXED modifiers for the duration - a snapshot, never a live mirror - and 433.1.c is the trap: *"If both attributes are the same numeric value, Swapping has no effect"*, so swapping a Might-8 attacker with a Might-8 defender is a dead card. That also corrects the #119 line "Swap has one real user, Switcheroo, already in eight entries": the eight were SYNERGY-RULE instances, and `SFD-145 Switcheroo` was in zero entries until now.
