Three blocks read with no card behind them, recorded so nobody reads them twice: **443 Skip** (exactly one card prints "Skip", zero cards trigger on the Draw Phase, and 443.1.a's *"They skip their next point from conquering this turn"* is a replacement effect on POINTS the pool prints no card for), **418 Heal** (418.1.a makes every damage clear a Heal and ZERO cards watch one - what it does explain is why the word "heal" on a save card is load-bearing, since 458.1 means a bare Recall sends the body home still carrying its marked damage), and **451/452/453**, one-line headings that add no mechanic. Two measurement notes worth keeping: an uncited-blocks probe must require a SUB-RULE (`\d{3}\.[0-9a-z.]*`), because a bare `\d{3}` matches three-digit quantities in entry prose and falsely reports 432, 443, 451-455 and 701 as cited; and **`/tmp/rc-walks/` is shared and this shell has `noclobber` ON**, so a `cat >` against an existing staging file fails SILENTLY and a later merge can fold another session's entries into yours - use `set +o noclobber`, a session-prefixed temp name, and re-read the staging id list after every write.
- **381 read against 310.2 is a general bar on buying a payoff inside the event that needs it (#173 batch 2, 2026-09-07).** While ANY trigger of an event sits on the Chain the state is Neutral Closed (310.2: *"There is no Showdown or Combat in progress and a Chain exists"*), and 381 confines every Activated Ability to an Open State on your own turn - so **an ACTIVATED payoff can never be bought inside the event it wants to modify**; 340.4's priority window does not help and 383.3.d's ordering does not either. That is the same "engine offset by one event" shape this file records for 383.3.b base costs, reached by a completely different rule, and it is the general form of both. Worked case: a *"Disempower me, exhaust: give a unit -2 Might"* legend cannot shrink a body for its OWN "when I become Empowered" banish - the reduction has to come from an earlier empower event, which raises the ceiling it can remove from 3 Might to 5. Companion facts: a [Level] rung is a THRESHOLD YOU HOLD and not a price you pay (824.1.d + 730.2), so a [Level] deck wants a faucet and NO sink — **scoped 2026-09-12 by 727.1.c.3.a, which was uncited:** *"If the condition for the Dependent Keyword causes the Activated Ability to become Inactive after it has been added to the chain as a Pending Item, that chain item will not be affected and will proceed with being played as normal."* Spending the XP switches off a PASSIVE and an ability not yet activated, NOT one already on the chain. The population is exactly one card (`UNL-049 Honeyfruit`, the pool`s only Level-gated ACTIVATED ability) and 444.2.c closes even that, since an Add ability with [Reaction] finalizes and resolves immediately and never sits on the chain long enough — dead letter in this pool, but **the refinement is the rule and not the count** - and the sink is usually printed in the same domain. Three cards read your own point total as a QUANTITY rather than a threshold: `OGN-028 Draven, Showboat` (his MIGHT is your score), `OGN-112 Kai'Sa, Evolutionary` (points as a cost ceiling) and `SFD-055 Needlessly Large Yordle` (points scored from HOLDING this turn) - Draven counts all three kinds of point in 194.1 including R2's card-text gains, and he is smallest when you are behind, so he widens a lead and never closes a gap. And 449 with 420.2.b: **a move BY EFFECT is not restricted by 144.4 at all**, so it goes battlefield to battlefield with no [Ganking] and moves an EXHAUSTED body (420.3.a puts the exhaust on the Standard Move alone).
- **450 is the clean citation for the enemy-mover family, and there are FOUR routes to "the opponent attacks on your turn" (#173 batch 3, 2026-09-07).** 450: *"The Destination becomes Contested if it is an Uncontested Battlefield not controlled by the controller of the Unit or Units that moved."* This file reaches that through 190.3.a.1, which covers PLAYING as well as moving; 450 says it in one sentence for the move case and had never been cited. The four routes, each with what it alone adds: `UNL-050 Iascylla` (free and repeating), `OGN-043 Charm` (mono-CALM - this line said Calm/Mind in two places until 2026-09-09, when #187 ran the family against `cards.json`), `UNL-139 Bone Skewer` (the only one that pulls the body out of their HAND, mono-CHAOS, not Fury/Chaos) and `VEN-148 Shadow Dash` (picks a body already on their board AND pumps your garrison with the same card, Calm/Order) - a fifth entry in this family must say what it adds to all four. Companion: 829.1.b makes every [Flow] replay *"a card played from anywhere other than your hand"*, which is the printed trigger on `VEN-155 Heart of the Tempest`, its own forced legend - the Order/Chaos [Flow] pool is seven cards (`VEN-098`, `VEN-100`, `VEN-105`, `VEN-113`, `VEN-116`, `VEN-127`, `VEN-156`), and the cap is the LEGEND's exhaust, so six Flow plays a game still buy one payoff a turn (415.3.a). And 477.3.b's worked example IS the *"-N to a minimum of 1"* shape, so a floored reduction is worth ZERO against a body already at 1 and never stacks into a kill - 143.2.a needs marked damage and a Might reduction marks none - while 477.3.e.2.a applies decreases last, so the two halves of a symmetric +1/-1 card never race.
- **[Backline] is the self-protecting MIRROR of [Tank], granting Tank to a Backline body is a printed trap, and every "ignore" card is one-way (#187 batch 2, 2026-09-07).** 465.2.c.6 through .c.10 were cited by NOTHING while .c.2-.c.5 are cited in 210 places, and .c.6's worked example prints the entire assignment order: *"That player must assign combat damage first to the unit with Tank, then to the unit with no abilities, then to the unit with Backline."* So a Tank pays a toll for the rest of the garrison (815.1.c.2) and a Backline body is the LAST thing that can legally be assigned damage. 465.2.c.8 is the trap: *"If a Unit has one or more Abilities or effects applying to it that demand it be assigned damage in a specific way that is exclusionary, then the ASSIGNING PLAYER chooses only one of those abilities to apply"* - and its worked example is Caitlyn, Patrolling given Tank, BY NAME - so granting [Tank] to a [Backline] body hands the choice to the ATTACKER, and `OGN-057 Block` and `SFD-033 Doran's Shield` are anti-synergic with all five Backline cards. 465.2.c.10 closes it from the other side: *"If a unit cannot be dealt damage, then no amount of damage can be considered lethal. Such a unit is exempt from any considerations of mandatory assignment"* - a full Prevent switches a Tank wall off. **767 makes every "ignore" card one-way and scoped**: *"Ignored abilities are only treated as inactive for the specific game action or procedure described, and only by the players directed by the ability"*, with the worked example spelling out that another player assigning damage at the same location will NOT be able to ignore Backline - so `VEN-004 Dune Surfer` is a KEY and not a dispel; the same board stops walling you and keeps walling the opponent, and 764-767 had never been cited. And the pool has **SIX cards with the Backline EFFECT and FOUR with the keyword**, and the difference is what a card that READS keywords would see (722.1): `UNL-043`, `UNL-090`, `UNL-141` and `UNL-145` print [Backline], while `OGN-068 Caitlyn, Patrolling` and `SFD-173 Soraka, Wanderer` print the sentence in prose - *"I must be assigned combat damage last"* - with no keyword at all (measured 2026-09-07: `grep -ci backline` over the corpus returns exactly 4). 465.2.c.6 orders assignment by *"requirements and restrictions on damage assignment"* and its worked example quotes the EFFECT, so all six are assigned damage last. An earlier version of this line said FIVE and called Caitlyn a bracket-less keyword print; both halves were wrong, which is the standing lesson about counted sets reaching a claim that was never swept. Two blocks read with no card behind them: **478/479 Dependency** (every worked example needs a passive *"Might increased to N"*, which no card prints - a Double and a Swap bake their amounts in at the action, so no dependency arises; re-read it if a future set prints that shape, since 479.2 would make a floor ANTI-synergic with a pump) and **741-749 Counters**, where 745.2 is the general form of 702.2.b.2 and **748** (*"Game Objects that change zones to a non-board zone lose all of their Counters"*) is the real rule behind buff recovery - both better citations than the 702.x this file currently uses.
- **A floored reduction is SNAPSHOTTED when applied, "alone" costs you your own board, and an Energy-only discount belongs on an Energy-only cost (#188 batch 4, 2026-09-07).** 477.3.b snapshots the reduction at the moment it applies, and Riot's worked example is `OGN-093 Smoke Screen`'s exact wording: a *"-4, to a minimum of 1"* aimed at a 2-Might body is worth -1 FOR THE WHOLE TURN even if that body is later pumped to 10 - so a floored reducer must be aimed AFTER the opponent pumps, which is precisely what [Reaction] timing buys, and aiming it early throws most of it away. 740.2.a is worth re-reading against intuition: a unit is alone when there are no other FRIENDLY units at the same location, so **the enemy garrison is irrelevant to "alone" and the real cost of the whole family is YOUR OWN board** - 465.2.c assigns each side its SUMMED Might, so a lone raider brings one body and receives a garrison (740.2.b's stricter "one on one" is the clause that needs both sides down to one). And 356.6 floors the ENERGY half of a cost at zero and never touches Power, so **the best target for an Energy-only discount is the most expensive unit with NO Power cost**, not simply the most expensive unit. `OGN-067 Blitzcrank, Impassive` is a THIRD enemy-mover that fires a Defend trigger on your own turn - and it is NOT "the only one in mono-Calm", which is what this line said until 2026-09-09: measured against `cards.json`, FOUR of the seven movers are mono-Calm (`OGN-043 Charm`, `OGN-067 Blitzcrank`, `UNL-050 Iascylla`, `UNL-054 Tricksy Tentacles`), against `UNL-139 Bone Skewer` and `UNL-141 Evelynn` in mono-Chaos and `VEN-148 Shadow Dash` in Calm/Order, so ONE Calm legend covers four of them and the family is a CALM family - which is the OPPOSITE shape to the one this file used to state, where a new entry had to distinguish itself against four identities; the seven are Charm, Blitzcrank, Iascylla and Tricksy Tentacles in mono-Calm, Bone Skewer and Evelynn in mono-Chaos, Shadow Dash in Calm/Order and any new entry must say what it adds to all of them.
- **A [Tank] wall does not make you OVERKILL, and 417.6.b.3 cuts both ways (#173 batch 4, 2026-09-07).** The natural model - "the wall eats my damage" - is wrong: 465.2.c.4 caps every assignment at minimum lethal either way, so what 815.1.c.2 costs you is **ORDER**, not damage. Your Might goes into the wall first and the real threats live on what is left; ignoring [Tank] therefore changes WHICH bodies die, never how much damage exists, and an ignore-Tank card is **not** an excess-damage enabler (R28 = A defines excess as attacking Might never assigned, and the garrison's total lethal requirement is unchanged). 764-766 is the general paragraph behind every "you ignore X" card and this file had only the cards' own wording: 766 - *"Any abilities ignored in this way are treated as INACTIVE for the purposes of the game action or procedure"* - and its second worked example is verbatim the Dune Surfer sentence with Backline in it. Inactive is stronger than a bypass: 465.2.c.6's requirement has nothing left to obey. Second, the half of 417.6.b.3 this file did not record: by the same sentence that makes Challenge-family damage UNIT damage (so the pool's only blanket fog leaves it working), **no Bonus Damage source raises it either**, because all seven read *"your SPELLS AND ABILITIES deal N Bonus Damage"* - so a card like `OGN-260 Last Breath` is immune to the fog and unimprovable by the pump, from one paragraph. And a printed *"N Energy OR N rainbow less"* choice is settled by this project's own rate: **always take the Power** - it is priced near nine Energy inside a loop and is scarcer in a normal game too (315.3.b returns two runes a turn, 164.2.b spends one permanently to the Rune Deck) - and since no card prints a rainbow Power COST, the rainbow half always comes off a printed DOMAIN Power and is never dead letter.
- **"Then do this:" is a SEPARATE chain item with a full priority window before it, and 755 ignores every cost a re-aim incurs (#187 batch 3, 2026-09-07).** 387.1: *"Reflexive Triggers can be recognized by the phrase 'Do this:' or 'Do one of the following:'"*, and 388.1 adds the second half to the chain as its own Pending Item - which then goes through 336.1's process, locks its targets and any 204.3.a base cost at 337.1, and hands out priority at 337.4 with 338.1.a admitting a [Reaction]. **Eight cards print it**: `OGN-258 Dragon's Rage`, `SFD-024 Rell, Magnetic`, `SFD-154 Guards!`, `SFD-198 Arise!`, `UNL-081 Keeper of Masks`, `UNL-139 Bone Skewer`, `UNL-199 Deceiver`, `UNL-200 Mirror Image`. Two consequences for entries already in the catalogue: the Reflection tokens are on the board and **the COPY SOURCE is still killable** in the window before "becomes a copy" resolves; and a *"you may pay 1 Order rune to ready it"* second half is a base cost paid at FINALIZATION, so the rune must be banked before the window opens. 387.1.a's *"'Do this' can be followed by 'N times'"* is DEAD LETTER - no card prints it, so there is no reflexive multiplier to hunt. Next, 750-755 was cited by nothing and 755 inverts the obvious reading of a re-aim: *"Any costs 'to play' the spell or ability that are incurred as a result of new choices made in this way are ignored"*, with 755.1 *"The spell or ability is already played and its costs paid"* - so **re-aiming a spell onto a [Deflect] body costs NOTHING**, since 809.1.c prices Deflect as an additional cost TO PLAY. But 753.1 (*"A player may not make new choices this way that would be illegal"*) leaves a *"can't be chosen"* prohibition standing - the THIRD independent appearance of the tax-versus-prohibition split, and the cleanest. 752.1 is a CLOSED list of what may be remade (*"locations to be played to, modes, destinations, and targets"*) and 752.2 freezes "as you play this" choices and Optional Additional Costs, so a [Repeat] cost's choices and an [Empower] payment can never be remade; the pool prints the clause on exactly two cards. And one result: 413.4 and 440.4 make a Burn Out a STEP INSIDE the draw or burn action rather than a failure of it, so the draw STILL COMPLETES out of the deck 431.2.b just refilled - the right citations for a claim this file sourced to 431 alone.
- **A GOLD TOKEN IS A GEAR, so every Gold-maker advances a gear count, and the pool's cheap gear is its mana base (#188 batch 5, 2026-09-07).** 187.5 verbatim: *"A Gold gear token is a domainless gear token with '[Reaction][>] Kill this, [E]: [Add] [A].'"* - with 185.2.d (*"Tokens have a type. They follow all rules for their type unless otherwise specified"*) that makes a Gold a gear for every *"control N or more gear"* threshold in Mind (`VEN-058 Patched Porobot`, Dropboarder, Plaza Guardian, `VEN-076 Repair Specialist`), and the token's own [Add] costs its own exhaust, so a Gold is never mana the turn it is born unless `SFD-171 Renata Glasc, Industrialist` is out (R25 = A, and she is Order, so that wants a Mind/Order legend). The companion is that a gear COUNT is cheap to build: the Seal cycle is printed at Energy cost ZERO, so a *"no more than 1 Energy"* window (`SFD-074 Pickpocket`) catches every Seal plus Orb of Regret, Eye of the Herald and Symbol of the Solari at E1, and 718.5.b keeps an ATTACHED one a legal choice. And the tax-versus-prohibition split gets its fourth independent appearance from the defensive side: 054.1 makes *"can't be chosen"* absolute where 809.1.c prices [Deflect], both blind to 355.10.d and 355.10.e, and **740.2.c defines "in combat"** (*"A unit is in combat if it is occupying a battlefield where combat is ongoing"*), which is what makes a protection scoped to combat the exact inverse of a [Tank]. Last: `VEN-036 Sandstone Chimera`'s symmetric halving turns ONE-SIDED on a turn you can name - 430.3 channels *"as many as possible"* against 161.2.a's twelve and 315.3.b's two a turn, so from the moment your own Rune Deck is empty it costs you nothing - and its clause is scoped to the FREE channel of the Channel Phase, so a card-based channel is untouched.
- **734-738 Additional Turns is Riot's own Time Warp arithmetic, and 358.3.a says a prohibition does not brick the card that walks into it (#187 batch 4, 2026-09-07).** The block was cited by nothing and its worked examples name the two cards this project's CHAIN theory rests on. 738's first example confirms the four-consecutive-turns ceiling in Riot's own notation (*"If the turn queue is represented as [> A > B > C > D >], then these Additional Turns will appear as [> A > A* > A* > B > C > D >]"*), and its second names `OGN-115 Promising Future` and Time Warp together to give the ordering rule: each Additional Turn is inserted IMMEDIATELY AFTER THE CURRENT TURN, so the LAST one created is taken FIRST. In a Duel that inverts the obvious fear - Promising Future says *"Starting with the next player"*, so the opponent plays their banished Time Warp first and yours is queued AHEAD of theirs - and 737 adds that *"it does not change the Turn Order of the game"*, so nobody loses a turn. Next, **358.3.a is the general rule for playing into a prohibition** and its worked example is `OGN-070 Mageseeker Warden` by name: *"If a Game Effect prevents the performance of a game action, that effect doesn't prevent cards and abilities that instruct a player to perform that game action from being played or finalized. On resolution, that game action will be skipped as it is an impossible instruction."* The split that cashes it is 419.4.b - *"Non-triggered abilities that check cards being played do so by means of referencing whether said cards have been Finalized"* (its example is Battering Ram counting a COUNTERED spell) - against 419.4.a.1, which keeps a *"when you play"* TRIGGER off a card that never resolves: **a non-triggered check needs only finalization, a trigger needs resolution**, so [Legion], "cards played this turn" and cost reductions all count a fizzled card. Three more first citations: **390.3.a** is the general rule for every *"then banish it"* / *"then recycle it"* rider (the project sourced it to 829.1.b.1 alone) and 390.2 / 390.4 are the right paragraphs for a delayed trigger such as Nami's; **395 + 397** scope a Linked set to ONE Game Object and forbid feeding it from outside (*"Any units banished by effects other than component Linked Abilities in the same set as the activated ability cannot be played"*), which is why `SFD-090 The Zero Drive`, `UNL-148 Cursed Sarcophagus` **and `UNL-181 Virtuoso`** can never be fed by another banisher and three copies do not share a pool. **This line said "the pool`s only TWO `banished with this` cards" until 2026-09-12, and that is true at the precision of the PHRASE and false at the precision of the CONCEPT** — 053.1 makes a LEGEND say *"me"* where a gear says *"this"*, so a grep for the phrase cannot see Virtuoso`s *"if there are four spells banished WITH ME"*. The paragraph for all three is **411.6**, uncited until now while its neighbours 411.4 and 411.5 carry 11 and 10 citations: a check that an action was performed *"with"* a Game Object requires the action to be ATTRIBUTED to that object`s spell or ability, controlled by that player, and the player to be responsible for it. **411.6.a is what makes The Zero Drive work at all** — *"Abilities granted via the effect text of a Game Object to another Game Object count for BOTH Game Objects for this purpose"* — because the Drive`s Deathknell is Effect Text that 718.3 appends to the CARRIER, so the banish is performed by an ability sitting on a unit and, read without 411.6.a, the cash-out would have nothing to look at. **And the contrast is two words wide**: *"banished with me"* is 411.6 and as narrow as a check gets, while `VEN-143 Master of Shadows`` *"When YOU banish a card you own"* is 411.4 — responsibility, not attribution — and is therefore fed by all 41 banish sources in the pool including every [Flow] replay; and **324.2** (*"If events during a Special Cleanup require another Cleanup, a normal Cleanup is invoked"*) is the paragraph the #62 recall family assumed and never had: 466.1.a.1's heal and 466.1.a.2's attacker recall happen ONCE per combat however many deaths that cleanup causes.
- **An opponent's [Deflect] pushes you PAST your own "spent N" threshold, and 816.1.b is the only effect that lands inside the opponent's Beginning Phase before their Scoring Step (#173 batch 5, 2026-09-07).** This file records the destructive half of the costs-versus-spent split (a discount switches a *"if you spent N or more"* payoff off and never touches a *"costs N or more"* one); the constructive half is that 809.1.c prices [Deflect] as *"an amount of Power equal to [Deflect Value] more to play as an additional cost for each time they choose"*, and 166.2 / 203.1 / 356.4.b read "spent" off what actually left the Rune Pool - so an opponent taxing your spell HELPS `UNL-004 Prepared Neophyte`, exactly as 820.1.c.1's [Repeat] cost does: a 1-Energy spell with a 3-Energy Repeat crosses a "spent 4 or more" line that a 6-Energy spell discounted to 3 fails. Next, the mirror of the denial lens's "nothing protects your own Hold" (every lock says *this turn* and is Main-Phase only, i.e. 316 after 315.2): 816.1.b is *"At the start of this permanent's controller's Beginning Phase, before scoring, kill this"*, so a [Temporary] granted to an ENEMY body on your turn kills their garrison at 315.2.a and 319.6 + 323.6 then strip their Control before 315.2.b.2 asks what they hold - it denies the Hold AND the battlefield. **The six granters, named**: `OGN-069 Last Stand` (Calm), `OGN-180 Fading Memories` (Chaos, the only one that also reaches a GEAR), `UNL-070 Turn to Dust` (Mind), `UNL-165 Shadow's Call` (Order), `UNL-199 Deceiver` and `UNL-200 Mirror Image` (Mind/Order). Third, 808.1.d.1's own example covers EVERY would-die replacement, banish included (*"for example because its 'killed' event was replaced with a recall, the triggered ability will be removed from the chain"*), so `UNL-007 Smite`'s *"if it would die this turn, banish it instead"* erases the Deathknell and the trash value for the whole turn and not only for its own 3 damage - and since 431.2.b recycles a burnt-out trash back into the deck, **banishing is the only permanent answer to a trash engine**; damage-based trash removal is a delay. Fourth, the finalization trap in full: 204.3.a makes a cost at the start of a trigger's effect payable *"to finalize"*, and **312.2.c is why you cannot go and find it with PRIORITY** - *"When the turn is in a Closed State, all pending chain items finish being finalized"*, so nobody receives priority in between. **But priority is not payment, and this file said the Power must already be banked, which 444.2.c refutes (2026-09-12):** *"Players may activate abilities that Add resources with the Reaction keyword at any time that they are instructed to Pay resources. Those abilities finalize and resolve immediately, ignoring normal restrictions"* — and 164.2 gives every Basic Rune two abilities, BOTH carrying Reaction, so a ready rune pays into a finalization with no priority window at all. The whole of rule 444 was uncited. The 0-Energy Power source is still good because it is FREE; it is no longer NECESSARY; the answer is a 0-Energy Power source (`OGN-040 Seal of Rage` is Fury's counterpart to the Mind deck's `OGN-120 Seal of Insight`, one Power once and one back every turn under 415.3.a, never competing with the turn's Energy). Two sweeps from the same batch: exactly TWO cards print *"can't be countered"* (`VEN-015 Decree of Rage`, `VEN-069 Mel, Newly Awakened`), and a [Weaponmaster] attach is an Activated Ability (818.1), so a *"when you play a gear"* trigger is blank against it.
- **182 lets an effect hand a token to the OPPONENT, 142.4.b makes lethal a re-checked measurement, and every ordinary token's cost is 0 for all purposes (#187 batches 5-7, 2026-09-07).** 182, cited by nothing: *"A token's controller is the controller of the spell or ability that created it, unless the token's type innately determines control or that spell or ability specifies that a different player is the token's controller"* - and exactly TWO cards in the pool use that exception, swept: `SFD-081 Card Sharp` (a Gold each, symmetric on paper) and `UNL-130 Walking Roost` (a 1-Might Bird to an opponent, a pure drawback). What breaks Card Sharp's symmetry is the word YOUR on `SFD-171 Renata Glasc, Industrialist` and `SFD-201 Chem-Baroness`, neither of which reaches the Gold the opponent took; 184.1 is the paragraph R25 = A was ruled over and is worth citing by number. The Bird carries [Deflect] by rule (187.7), so 355.10.d against 809.1.c prices that drawback off YOUR OWN removal suite: a choosing spell pays a rainbow to answer it and a programmatic sweeper deletes it for nothing. Next, **142.1-142.4 was cited by nothing and 142.4.b is the one that reprices removal**: *"Lethal Damage for a Unit is a non-zero amount greater than or equal to that Unit's Might"*, with the worked example of a Might 5 body carrying 3 damage that Frigid Touch kills by shrinking it - so a Might 6 body needs six damage OR two damage and -4 Might, and a floored reducer cannot substitute (477.3.b caps the reduction, not the result). 142.4.b's second example is the 0-Might wall at its source, and 142.3.a scopes *"your damage"* to damage YOU marked, which is why 417.6.b.4's mutual-damage exchange never feeds `UNL-118 Elder Dragon`. And the token half: 185.3.a.1 makes an ordinary token's cost *"0 for all purposes"* while 185.3.a.2's own example appends a REAL cost to a Deceiver Reflection through the copy effect - so a Reflection is the only token in the pool worth anything to a cost-counting effect (Atakhan's discount, Heedless Resurrection's ceiling), and 185.3.b does the same for domains, which is the rule under the synergy layer's `domains.length === 0` filter. Two more first citations: 758.1 checks untargetability AT RESOLUTION (*"the spell or ability will mistarget on resolution"*) and 758.2.a works a five-card pool example by name (Alpha Wildclaw, Vilemaw, Rebuke, Discipline, Star-Crossed), so a cantrip that moves a Might number pulls a body under `UNL-057 Alpha Wildclaw`'s umbrella while the removal is already on the Chain - he is the only one of the pool's six untargetability cards whose protected SET moves at [Reaction] speed; and **345** (*"As a Showdown begins, the player who applied Contested status to the Battlefield gains Focus"*) settles who acts first in a combat showdown - with 464.2.c.1, the ATTACKER, and no entry had ever said so.
- **A bounce to hand re-arms an [Equip], an exhaust-a-friendly cost is free on the opponent's turn, and 355.2.a is why a spell reinforces what a play cannot (#173 batches 6-7 and #188 batches 6-9, 2026-09-07).** 718.2 is scoped to *"while in this state"*, which is the door the #170 correction leaves open: an ATTACHED Equipment can never pay its own [Equip] again, but `SFD-135 Factory Recall` returns it to hand where the printed text is Active, so a replay buys a NEW carrier for 1 Energy at [Action] speed - and on a [Quick-Draw] Equipment it is free, since 819.1.d determines no Equip cost at all. Next: **an exhaust-a-friendly-unit cost is FREE at [Reaction] speed on the opponent's turn** - 144.1.b forbids the Standard Move outside your Main Phase so readiness buys nothing there, neither 464.2.c.3 nor 465.2.b filters by state so an exhausted defender fights at full Might, and 415.3.a readies it at your own Awakening - which is what makes `OGN-049 Playful Phantom`, a printed blank with no rules text, the perfect fuel: a body with no abilities has no opportunity cost when tapped. Third, 355.2.a confines a PLAYED unit to your base or a battlefield you control, but 449 lets a spell cause a MOVE with only its own restrictions and 420.3.a puts the exhaust on the Standard Move alone - so a spell reinforces a battlefield you are ATTACKING where a play cannot, and 319.6 with 323.2.a hand the arrival its designation before 465.2 resolves; the mirror is that a HIDDEN removal spell is defence-only, because 811.1.b hides only at a battlefield you control and 811.1.d.2 pins its targets there. Fourth, an opponent's [Deflect] is an ADDITIONAL COST (809.1.c), so their tax pushes you PAST your own *"spent N or more"* threshold, exactly as a [Repeat] cost does (820.1.c.1) - the constructive half of the costs-versus-spent split. Swept sets, members named: eight cards add ENERGY at [Reaction] speed (`OGN-098`, `UNL-049`, `SFD-083`, `UNL-093`, `OGS-014`, `OGN-253`, `UNL-197`, `VEN-141`), which matters because 415.3.a readies runes only in your own Awakening and 164.2.b lets an exhausted rune pay only POWER; six cards Empower themselves with no [Empower] cost (`VEN-143`, `VEN-151`, `VEN-153`, `VEN-155`, `VEN-092`, `VEN-028`), of which Mournful Witness is the only UNIT; and the five *"within 3 points of the Victory Score"* cards do NOT read the same player - `OGN-047` and `OGN-079` read the OPPONENT (a comeback clause), `SFD-201` reads YOURS (a closing clause), with `UNL-116` and `VEN-091` making up the five, and the threshold itself moves with the format (194.3's 8 against 489.3's 11).
- **428.5.c is why a burn spell ever "kills with a spell", 370.4 with 373.2 makes a replacement a FORK and not a doubling, and 355.14.h.1 forbids concentrating the remainder of a split (#187 batches 8-9, 2026-09-07).** 428.5.c, cited by nothing: *"When one or more Units is killed due to a Cleanup, that kill action is attributed to the spell or ability that resolved immediately prior to that Cleanup that dealt damage to the Unit or Units"* - without it NO damage spell would ever satisfy a *"kill a unit with a spell"* clause, since 143.2.a kills in a Cleanup and a burn spell performs no kill instruction - and 428.5.c.2 excludes the Combat Cleanup, where the kill belongs to the UNITS, so that payoff stays switched off in combat. 428.5.d names `OGN-037 Immortal Phoenix` and composes with the reflexive-trigger finding: splitting a spell into separate chain items with *"Do this"* does not cost it the attribution, because *"both the spell and its ability receive attribution"*. Next, 370.4 (*"A Game Object can apply its Replacement Effects to any qualifying events that occur simultaneously with it leaving the zone that its Replacement Effect is active in"*, worked example `SFD-173 Soraka, Wanderer`) means a board wipe does NOT beat a would-die replacement, it only costs it - while 370.3 is the negative mirror, since a replacement that lives in the TRASH does not cover a simultaneous death. **373.2 is the limit 370.4 does not state**: *"each Replacement Effect may only be applied in one sequence, to any number of events that are qualified to be replaced"*, and Riot's example is Soraka wearing a Guardian Angel - saving her first means her own effect can then only reach the bodies at the BASE, saving the garrison first means she dies - **so a second replacement on the same body is a fork between two halves of the board, never a doubling**; 373.1.a adds that replaced bodies are recalled BEFORE the unsaved ones finish dying, so they generate no death event at all. And the whole 355.14 SPLITTING block was uncited: targets lock at finalization (355.14.b) and the count is capped by the damage available when PLAYED (355.14.c), but the division is chosen at resolution (355.14.e) with a minimum of 1 each (355.14.f/g) - so shrinking the SOURCE forces the damage to spread, because 355.14.h.1 says the controller *"cannot choose to have fewer Targets than they have damage to split"*. Riot's example is written from the DEFENDING side and names Feral Strength and Frigid Touch as the two answers, and 355.14.i keeps every cost paid and every trigger fired on a target that is later dropped.
- **A false fact travels further than a walked line: check any legality claim against `data/legality.json`, and a `quote` must be pasted AND UNTRUNCATED (#189 / #192 cross-audits and the fc quote sweep, 2026-09-07).** The two audits of the 135 entries written after #181 found 62/68 and 59/67 clean, and every defect in both was in one family: **a claim that was TYPED rather than MEASURED.** The worst was *"OGS-017 is restricted in 2v2"*, which was false and had spread to **twenty-one** places in `combos.json` while the audit's scope only saw six - `data/legality.json` has exactly ONE restricted row, `OGS-019 Wuju Bladesman - Starter`, a mono-Body legend that is not even a member of the Fury/Chaos lists the sentence was attached to. A public tool telling a player a card is restricted when it is not is worse than an arithmetic slip, so **every ban or restriction sentence is checked against `data/legality.json` (whose `entries[].bases` is an ARRAY and whose only non-banned row is that one) before it ships.** The rest were legend censuses typed from memory (Fury/Mind is THREE names, Calm/Order FOUR, Body/Order FIVE, Fury/Chaos FIVE), sweeps off by one (thirteen `[Empower]` disjunction rows, not fourteen; five cards read your point total, not three; nine *"alone"* cards, not eight) and one superlative that was simply false (`SFD-132 Beast Below` at Might 8 is not the largest body mono-Chaos prints - `UNL-147 Baron Nashor` is Might 12 at E10 + 3 Power). Companion standing rule, earned by a script `rc-walk-fc` wrote against its own 39 entries (2,171 rule references checked, all existing; 391 quoted passages checked, 27 flagged): **a `quote` field must be pasted AND UNTRUNCATED - if you elide, mark it with an ellipsis** - because a sentence closed with a period reads as complete, and two of the seventeen repaired had dropped the exact half they were cited for (806.1.b ends *"even when it is not the Controlling player's turn"*, 136.2.d ends *"even if the Top-Most Card shares a name with the Attached game object"*). Both checks are cheap enough to run every batch: assert every `NNN.x.y` token exists in the rules file, and assert every single-quoted passage appears there verbatim with whitespace and curly quotes normalised.
- **"X. Then Y." survives a replacement and "X. If you do, Y." does not, Bonus Damage on a SPLIT buys a whole extra body, and combat damage IS eventually dealt (#187 batch 10 and #191 batch 1, 2026-09-07).** 359.3.e.14.b, cited by nothing: *"If the Game Action performed in an earlier linked instruction is replaced, this will not affect the later linked instruction, unless the later linked instruction directly references the Game Action being performed"* - and Riot contrasts two pool cards by name, `OGN-213 Hidden Blade` (*"Kill a unit at a battlefield. Its controller draws 2"* - the draw references the UNIT, so it executes even when the kill is replaced) against `SFD-163 Deathgrip` (*"Kill a friendly unit. If you do, …"* - which does not). So a would-die shield on your own body turns Hidden Blade into 2 Energy for 2 cards with the unit kept, while 359.3.e.14.a kills BOTH forms if the target is merely bounced, because the instruction is then IGNORED rather than replaced. Next, **715.3 is a third shape of Bonus Damage** the project did not have: on a Deal that SPLITS, the bonus applies to the amount to be split and *"can alter the number of targets eligible to be chosen"* (Riot's example: `OGN-041 Volibear, Furious` under `OGS-001 Annie, Fiery`, six damage across up to six bodies), so 715.1 is single-target, 715.2 is per target of a multi-target Deal, and 715.3 is +1 damage AND +1 BODY - with 715.4 keeping Bonus Damage from ever creating a Deal action. **417.1.a is a refinement to this file's own shorthand**: *"Assigning Damage during the Combat Damage Step is not Dealing Damage, but will cause Damage to be Dealt when assignment is complete"* - so combat damage IS dealt and a *"when I take damage"* payoff (`OGN-221 Imperial Decree`) fires off combat on both sides; what does not attach to it is BONUS DAMAGE, which 713 grants to Deal ACTIONS and 417.3.a keeps off the assignment. Read "combat damage is not a Deal action" as being about Bonus Damage only. And 417.1.e.1 (*"Only Valid Damage is Dealt"*) has a worked example where a FULL Prevent stops a *"when I take damage"* trigger and a partial one does not - the precise reason 437.4 is a different mechanism from the heal/exhaust/recall shields. Last, what INACTIVE text still does, in four paragraphs nobody had cited: 722.1 keeps a card's KEYWORDS visible to anything that reads keywords, 722.2 keeps target eligibility parsing them (Riot's example: a *"destroy a gear with Temporary"* spell can still choose an attached `SFD-186 Spinning Axe`), 723 says Rules Text is never Inactive by default and 724 says Effect Text is Inactive UNLESS attached - so attached and unattached are exact inversions - while **725.3 is the paragraph behind [Weaponmaster]'s "even if it's already attached"**, which this project had been deriving from reminder text. Three blocks returned EMPTY with the reason, so nobody re-reads them: 178.2/178.3 multi-type Game Objects (the type histogram over `cards.json` is unit 629, spell 233, rune 18, gear 114, legend 127, battlefield 66, empty 2 - NO card has two types, and Experimental Hexplate's *"I am a Mech"* is a tag), 383.3.e.2 (the rule is real - a leading *"you may"* is decided at FINALIZATION and declining does not spend a once-per-turn trigger - but none of the pool's four *"once each turn"* cards has that shape), and 416.5.a (two cards recycled to the RUNE Deck go back in the owner's chosen order while the Main Deck is random, and no card recycles two runes in one action).
- **A TIME WARP CHAIN STARVES ANY CONQUER ENGINE WHOSE PAYOFF NEEDS COMBAT, and that is a one-question test for the whole conquer bucket (#193 close-out, 2026-09-07).** 323.9 marks a Combat as Staged only *"at each Battlefield that Contested was applied to that have Units present controlled by opposing players"*, and 807.1.d makes being an attacker mean *"the Unit has gained the Attacker designation during Combat"* - so on the second and third turns of a Time Warp chain, where the opponent never takes a turn and therefore never puts a new garrison anywhere, **every attack-trigger card in the line goes INERT**. Only an engine that takes an UNCONTROLLED battlefield through 344.2 (*"If Control of a Battlefield is Contested, there aren't units controlled by different players there, and the turn is in a Neutral Open State, a Showdown is opened during the next Cleanup"*) can chain at all. Ask of every conquer candidate: **does this payoff need an enemy garrison?** If it does, it is worth one turn and the chain is dead from the second - which bounds the 31-row conquer-engine bucket from above before anyone walks it. Two refusals from the same close-out, both with the arithmetic rather than a verdict: `power-nexus-sentinel-renata-mastermind` reaches 5 and not 8 (Time Warp and Renata want 22 resource-units in a turn that yields 18), and `yasuo-remorseful-svellsongur-nasus-conquer` reaches 4, on the starvation rule above. And the sweep that opens the lane instead: the pool has FOURTEEN cards whose text gains a point (`OGN-034`, `OGN-066`, `OGN-205`, `OGN-290` - banned in both formats - `SFD-088`, `SFD-115`, `SFD-148`, `SFD-214`, `UNL-177`, `VEN-046`, `VEN-053` denial rather than gain, `VEN-065`, `VEN-067`, `VEN-138`), of which EIGHT score on a Hold or a Conquer - this file's BURST-saturation note says six, and the two it misses are `OGN-034 Tryndamere` (catalogued under Red Brambleback) and **`VEN-065 Swain, Visionary`, which is catalogued under NO multiplier at all**: a containment check against Red Brambleback, Reckoner's Arena and Svellsongur returns nothing, and his only two entries are one-turn double-Conquer CHAINs that need an infinite loop to pay for themselves. That is the open cell in a family recorded as closed.
- **Two pause-day corrections, one of them to a correction (2026-09-07, fleet stand-down).** First, a walker reported that `dune-surfer-armed-assailant-ignore-tank` quotes an Example under **766** that does not exist, and that this file's claim about *"766's second worked example"* is therefore wrong. **It is not: 766 carries TWO examples**, one for Deflect and one whose text is *"A unit reads 'You ignore Backline while assigning combat damage here.' … The unit's controller can assign damage to any units with Backline as if they didn't have it."* The entry and this file are both right and neither should be "fixed"; the lesson is the one this project keeps paying for from the other side - **an absence is a measurement too, and "I cannot find it" needs the same grep as "I found it".** Second, a real tension between two standing rules, worth knowing before it bites again: the rule *"check every legality sentence against `data/legality.json`"* pushes a walker to write the restricted row's base code (`OGS-019`) into `prerequisites.easy`, and `test/legend-lines.test.ts` reads ANY legend base code in that field as a legend NAMED FOR THE ENTRY - so a mono-Order or mono-Chaos line that mentions the Calm/Body Bladesman fails the domain check. **Describe the restricted row without writing its base code unless that legend is genuinely legal for the entry**; the field is a legend list, not a place for a legality footnote.
- **An "if" clause immediately after the trigger is a CONDITION and the same clause later is an EFFECT, a replaced COST is still paid, and Bard carries the payoffs rather than a spare body (#187 batch 15 and #193 batch 2, 2026-09-09).** 383.2.a.1 is cited across this catalogue for the "count is part of the Trigger Condition" half; its two worked examples establish a WORD-ORDER TEST nobody had written down. `OGN-073 Sona, Harmonious` (*"At the end of your turn, if I'm at a battlefield, ready up to 4 friendly runes"*) has the clause immediately after the trigger, so it is CONDITION - checked when the trigger is placed, and Riot spells out that **"if she is removed in reaction to the Triggered Ability, it will still resolve"**. `OGN-251 Loose Cannon` (*"At the start of your Beginning Phase, draw 1 if you have one or fewer cards in your hand"*) has it later, and the example says outright that it *"is not immediately after the trigger condition, so it is part of the effect and not the condition"* - checked on resolution, and removal in response DOES stop it. Purely where the clause sits in the sentence, and it decides whether a trigger is removal-proof. Next, **357.2.a: *"Costs that are replaced with other events by replacement effects are still considered paid"*** - Riot's example is `OGN-208 Cruel Patron` paying its kill-a-friendly-unit cost into a `OGN-077 Zhonya's Hourglass`, and it must be carried WITH 370.1.a.1's example (*"A unit's death being replaced by Zhonya's Hourglass is the same as the kill action that caused that death not occurring"*) or the two read as a contradiction. The general form, which this lane has now met three times from three directions: **a replaced COST is still paid (357.2.a); an EFFECT that references the replaced game action is NOT performed (359.3.e.14.b, Deathgrip's "if you do"); and a DEATHKNELL on the saved body is removed (808.1.d.1).** Third, from the conquer lane: **383.4.c.2 pays only what was PRESENT during the Conquer action**, so every double-Conquer line that takes the second battlefield with a SPARE BODY collects the 1-point Score and nothing else - while `SFD-079 Bard, Mercurial` moves *"any number of your units to an open battlefield"* and therefore carries the PAYOFFS, making the same pack pay at both Conquers (7 + 7 instead of 7 + 1). That is a lever on the whole conquer bucket rather than a card in one line. Its price is printed: 170.11.c needs the second battlefield unoccupied AND uncontrolled, and Bard's additional cost exhausts your legend, which 315.1.b readies only at your own Awakening - so three copies buy three TURNS, not three relocations. Companion arithmetic from the same walk: 135.2.e.6.c processes a `[C]` on a MULTI-domain card as any of that card's domains (Riot's example is `SFD-196 Defiant Dance`) while 135.2.e.6.b makes a `[C]` on a domainless card an `[A]` - **so a dual-domain card's Power cost is easier to pay than a mono-domain card's of the same printed cost**, which this project had never stated.
- **`grep "^NNN\."` ON THE RULES FILE LIES: 89 headings sit immediately after a form feed and a bare caret cannot see them (measured 2026-09-09).** The file carries 120 form-feed page breaks, and `grep -c "^487\.5\."` returns **0** for a paragraph that exists - as does `grep -cE "^ *487\.5\."`, because a literal space class does not match `\f`. **The safe form is `grep -E "^[[:space:]]*NNN\."`**, since `[[:space:]]` matches the form feed; the committed probes (`probe-500-899.ts`, `audit-fc.ts`) already anchor that way and are fine. What is not fine is the one-off grep any of us types at the terminal - including the manager's verification greps, which used the space-class form all through 2026-09-07. **This has a false-NEGATIVE mode only**: it can report a real paragraph as nonexistent and can never invent one, so every citation confirmed by a hand grep is still confirmed, and the risk is a walk being told its correct citation does not exist. It is the same shape as the 766 case the same morning, where an absence was reported without being swept: **an absence is a measurement, and a measurement made with the wrong anchor is not one.** 718 is in the affected set, and this project leans on it constantly.
- **RE-RUN A SURPRISING ABSENCE WITH A DIFFERENT INSTRUMENT BEFORE YOU WRITE IT DOWN - it bit five times on 2026-09-09 and every time the wrong answer looked like a discovery.** The failures, all real, all caught: **`gh issue view --json body` OMITS THE COMMENTS** (issue #11 is 2,234 bytes of body against **61,321 of comments**, and R1, R2, R6, R25, R28 and R33 all live in the comments - a body-only pass reported 57 citations as untraceable, and refetching with `body,comments` took it to 38); a **base-code probe misses an issue that writes card NAMES** in prose, which made #89 look as if it had no record of a line it carries in full (name-aware, 26 more resolved, leaving 2); an **unquoted shell variable in a `for` loop** made `gh` report all 21 issue numbers as missing; **`grep "^NNN\."` on the rules file** returns zero for the 89 headings that sit after a form-feed page break, and so does `^ *NNN\.`, because a literal space does not match `\f` (use `^[[:space:]]*`); and a **`Card.type === "legend"` test** returns zero for every card, because `type` is an ARRAY. Every one of these produced a large, confident, WRONG absence that was an artifact of the instrument and never of the repository - and the companion case is the one that went the other way, where a walk reported that rule 766 carries only one worked example and it carries two. **An absence is a measurement. A measurement made with the wrong instrument is not one.** The standing form: if a probe tells you something you did not expect to be MISSING, prove it missing a second way before it reaches a report, an entry, or this file. **A sixth, 2026-09-12, and it is the mirror case - a wrong IDENTITY rather than a wrong absence:** a range-scoped probe handed its range as ONE quoted shell word saw `argv[2] = "100 299"` and `argv[3] = undefined`, so `Number()` gave NaN, every comparison went false, and TWO different ranges printed the same rows - which reads as the finding *"these ranges have identical leftovers"*. **Echo the parsed range in the output of any range-scoped probe**: one line, and it was the only reason the bug was caught.
- Riftbound is not MTG: no life total, win at 8 points, infinite draw is a liability (Burn Out), units enter exhausted, Rune Pool empties each turn, Domain Identity is subset-of-legend and every Legend has exactly two domains.
- Rules readings the catalogue stands on are numbered R1–R27 on issue #11 and ruled there by the user. Ruled 2026-09-03: R1 = A with stacking (Blue Sentinel / Red Brambleback add one trigger instance each on the single Score; N copies → N+1 instances). R2 = A: card-text "Score 1 point" is a point Gain by ability (Core Rules 194.1.c), not a 469 Score, so 470's once-per-battlefield cap does not apply — **and as of 2026-09-12 there IS a Riot document drawing the same line, which this file had recorded as nonexistent (#191 batch 30; Tournament Rules, labelled). 702.3.a: the Forgetting to Score a Point remedy covers *"a point from conquering or holding a battlefield"* and then says *"This does not apply to points that would be scored from card triggers such as Ahri, Alluring"* — naming the exact card, in a second Riot book.** Be exact about its strength: it corroborates R2`s PREMISE, that Riot treats a conquer-or-hold point and a card-trigger point as two different KINDS to the extent of giving one a remedy and the other none. It does NOT adjudicate R2`s CONCLUSION, which is about whether 470`s cap applies; a penalty guideline is not a rules ruling and rule 002 does not reach it. The ruling stays where the user put it, and if Riot ever rewords the cards from *"score"* to *"gain"* — the condition this file already names as confirming R2 — this paragraph will have been the early sign; sources are Core Rules 194.1/468/470, Tournament Rules 702.3.a, the Spiritforged FAQ ("extra points", 2026-01-14) and the Vendetta FAQ Swain ruling ("2 points total", 2026-08-14), all quoted on #11. Riot has said it may reword "score points" on cards; a change to "gain" confirms A, anything else reopens R2. Ruled 2026-09-04: R25 = A — Renata Glasc, Industrialist's "Your tokens enter ready" beats Treasure Hunter's "play a Gold gear token exhausted", so the Gold is Power the turn it appears; rule 375 only makes a replacement inherit modifications it is itself silent about (its own example duplicates the token and says nothing about ready/exhausted) while 369.3/370.1.c let Renata rewrite how a token enters. R26 = B — a "When you play a spell" trigger waits until the spell has resolved and banishes it from the trash; the literal Chain read (playing completes at Finalize 337.1, the trigger goes on at once 383.3.c, 340.1 resolves newest-first) would have Virtuoso counter his own spell, but the Core Rules example at 359.3.e.10 has such an ability fire "as the spell resolves", and a Riot rewording that puts the trigger above the spell reopens R26. Ruled 2026-09-04 (second batch): R6 = A — Svellsongur copies the unit's CURRENT text, not a snapshot ("for as long as this is attached to it" describes a continuous state, not a one-off event); this raises svellsongur-copy-hold from 9 to 11 points, ceiling 26. R10 = A — LeBlanc, Everywhere at Once's "Your [Temporary] effects at my battlefield don't trigger" does switch off the kill, because 816.1 makes [Temporary] a Triggered Ability keyword and 383 makes Triggered Abilities effects; the counter-reading would have to deny that an ability is an effect, against 383 and 145.1/151.1. R28 = A — "excess damage" (printed on six cards, defined nowhere) is attacking Might that never got assigned, because 465.2.c.4 forbids assigning a unit more damage than the minimum needed to kill it; Sivir's "deal that much" confirms it is a computable quantity, and reading B would make the six cards dead letter against rule 002. R7, R9, R20, R27 and R29 were all RETIRED the same day rather than ruled — four as settled by rules text, R7 as arithmetic (the joint loop ledger, issue #21). R20 because 344.2 opens a Showdown at the next Cleanup when a battlefield is Contested with NO units of different players there, 348.2.a establishes Control at its close and 348.2.a.1 says outright "This results in a Conquer", while 345/347.1 make that showdown a real window to play cards in. R27 because Might is not a copyable trait: 477.1.a lists the inherent traits without it and handles Might assignment separately at 477.1.a.1, 477.1.b.1.a lists the copyable traits without it, and 185.3.a.2's worked example is a Deceiver Reflection copy that appends "all copyable traits, including the cost" — cost, not Might. So a Reflection copy carries the copied card's Rules Text at 0 Might. R9 because 420.1/420.2.a make an effect move the primary kind of Move (456.1 carves out recalls precisely because ordinary effect moves DO trigger move abilities) and 359.3.f.1/f.2 read "here" at execution, with the Yasuo, Remorseful example saying so outright; R29 — see the ordering rule above. R8 = A, ruled 2026-09-04 after a web search found NO official Riot ruling (all four set FAQs silent, including the two that printed the cards): a battlefield's own Hold trigger IS one of "your hold effects", because 190.6.a gives its Controller the ability, 190.6.c keeps that control unless the ability names another player, 190.6.d makes "you" in a battlefield's abilities mean its Controller, and 383.4.d.2 counts an ability referencing the Holding player as a Hold Effect — four referents, one player. The ownership objection does not arise, since 127.1 plus 485.4.a mean the battlefield you control in a Duel is normally the one you brought. R30 = B, voted 2026-09-06 (opened by the internet mining pass, #46): Heimerdinger, Inventor's "I have all [exhaust] abilities of all friendly legends, units, and gear" copies Renata Glasc, Mastermind's `exhaust: Score 1 point` WITH its "Use my abilities only while I'm at a battlefield" clause, because 377.2.b makes that sentence a condition of the Activated Ability (its worked example, Ultrasoft Poro, quotes the sentence inside the ability) and 053.1 re-reads "I'm" on whoever now has the ability — so Heimerdinger must stand at a battlefield, Renata may stay at the base, and the community's "scores from the base" line (six sources) is cited as a source, not followed. A Riot ruling the other way reopens R30. R15 = A, voted 2026-09-06: Dancing Grenade's "1 additional Bonus Damage for each time this spell has dealt damage this turn" counts per card per turn and survives the trash between re-plays — the card defines a history window on purpose, 124 tracks objects not events, and reading B would make the clause dead letter (the R28 argument). It decides the class of `gemdragon-henge-vi-blind-fury` (INFINITE under A, a dead-even ENGINE under B); the 2026-09-03 reclassification to ENGINE rested on a reconstruction made without the walkthrough video, which yt-dlp can read since 2026-09-06 — a refutation written from a description is not a refutation. R31 ("open battlefield", printed on five cards) was RETIRED on 2026-09-06 — and the Core Rules DO define it, which the first search missed because the text is quoted: **170.11.c "Battlefields can be “open.” This means they are unoccupied and uncontrolled."** — BOTH conditions, not only "no Controller" as the retirement first said on the strength of Yone's errata (`data/errata.json`, "open battlefield" → "a battlefield that was uncontrolled", which is consistent: an uncontrolled battlefield with units on it is not open). Every entry that leans on the word must check both. Two lessons: before filing a reading over a printed term, grep the rules with `-i` and WITHOUT quotes or brackets around the word (`“open.”` hid it), then `data/errata.json` and the four set FAQs. R33 = A, voted 2026-09-06 (#100): Noxus Saboteur's "Your opponents' [Hidden] cards can't be revealed here" blocks PLAYING the hidden card at that battlefield — 054.1 makes a card that forbids beat a card that permits (811.1.b), and 055's "ignore impossible instructions" covers a card's own instructions, not another card's prohibition; reading B would leave the Saboteur dead letter (the R28 argument). **An entry whose notes say its evidence was UNREACHABLE is a debt, not a closure**: each time a new access route opens (yt-dlp transcripts came back on 2026-09-06; Reddit closed the same day), `grep -i 'out of reach\|reconstructed from\|could not be obtained\|bot wall' data/combos.json` and re-walk every hit against the source itself. There was exactly one such note and it was wrong for three days. Every combo is now verified; R3–R5, R11–R19 and R21–R24 are open but block nothing. A ruling never promotes an entry by itself: `verified` still needs a human walking the loop.

## Synergies (#22)
- `data/synergies.json` is a SEPARATE file and must stay separate. `combos.json`'s whole value is that every row in it was walked by hand; a text-matched row in there destroys that invariant.
- The unit is a PATTERN: one `anchor` base code plus a `partner` predicate over the pool. The rule is hand-verified once and carries `basis.rules` (Core Rules paragraphs), optional `basis.readings` (R-numbers from issue #11) and `basis.combos` (the verified entries it was extracted from). The instances are text matches. **Regla verificada, instancias por texto** — the UI says so in its own words and never presents a pair as a combo.
- **Print the whole match list and read it before shipping a rule.** `npm run synergies` prints every list, `-- --match` prints only the fragment each predicate caught, `-- <id>` narrows. A rule whose list you cannot read end to end is written too loosely; `test/synergies.test.ts` caps a rule at 150 matches for that reason.
- What the predicates are allowed to lean on: 383.4.c.1 and 383.4.d.1 give `"When I conquer…"` / `"When I hold…"` as the standard wording of those trigger categories, and 823.1.b makes [Hunt] both. That is why the hold and conquer predicates are honest rather than guesses.
- False positives found by reading the lists, and the shapes they come in — check any new rule against all four: a **nested quote** (Relentless Pursuit and Zed, From the Shadows carry a trigger written for another permanent inside their own text); a **second trigger that is a no-op** (Blitzcrank returns himself to hand, Hallowed Tomb needs an empty Champion Zone) - **and note the scope, because a 2026-09-07 walk read it as a defect and it is not**: that exclude is right ON A MULTIPLIER RULE, where the second instance finds him already in hand. The same trigger is a REBUY on its own, since 315.2.b.2 Holds and 469.1 / 471.1 gain the point BEFORE it sends him back, so he is replayed each turn to drag another enemy body. An exclude is scoped to its rule; check which before calling it wrong; a **doubled drawback** (Vaults of Helia, Sigil of the Storm); and a **condition that cannot be met on the other event** — 383.4.g.1 says an ability whose non-conquer parts are unfulfilled never reaches the chain, which is what kills Tryndamere, Sivir, Yeti Brawler, Yone and Swain under Reckoner's Arena. Record each as an `excludes` entry with its reason; never drop one silently.
- Tokens and the two helper cards are filtered out of every list (`domains.length === 0 && !type.includes("battlefield")`), since a decklist can never hold one. An anchor banned in every format fails validation — that is how `stealthy-pursuer-move-triggers` was caught.
- **A keyword lens opens with `grep -i <word>`, never with `[Keyword]`.** The bracketed form misses
  every card that writes the mechanic in prose: 8 cards say `empower`/`disempower` in lower case with
  no brackets and 4 of those are legends, so a `\[Empower\]` sweep silently skipped the legends of
  the family it was hunting (#34, 2026-09-05). Open wide with `-i`, then close by reading the list.
- **A battlefield you provide starts UNCONTROLLED, so its text is off until you take it.** 190.1:
  *"Control is established over Battlefields through the course of play."* 190.6.d closes it: *"If the
  battlefield has no Controller, 'you' refers to no one, and all such instructions are ignored."* The
  project had cited 190.6.d for R8 but never its second sentence. So a `While you control this
  battlefield` discount is NOT a deckbuilding freebie — walking a body there and establishing Control
  (466.5) is part of the entry's cost. Six battlefields print that wording; only SFD-211 Marai Spire
  is in the catalogue, and `ezreal-marai-spire-free-repeat` says so in its own notable (2026-09-05).
- **A battlefield is drawn at random, and only in some formats, and you cannot stack copies of it.** 485.4.a: a
  player provides three and only 1 is used; **485.5 and 487.5 say "randomly selects"**, while **486.5 says only
  "selects"**. So two of your battlefields never share a board (#33 fixes the synergy layer on this), and a
  battlefield-dependent combo is not certain to come online. **AND THE STANDING CAVEAT — glossed on 81 entries as "one in three, at random" — IS WRONG IN BOTH DIRECTIONS FOR A MATCH, because nobody read 486.5 to the end (corrected 2026-09-12, #187 batch 32).** Its second half: *"After this game, if a player won, the Battlefields that were used are to be REMOVED AND NOT SELECTED AGAIN FOR THIS MATCH. One of the remaining Battlefields that were set aside must be chosen instead"*, with 486.6 spelling out the wrapper (best of 3; after a game win, reset, remove the battlefields in play, choose new ones from those set aside). **So in a DUEL (485) it is one in three AT RANDOM; in a MATCH (486) it is a FREE CHOICE IN GAME 1 and then REMOVED for the rest of the match.** Both halves matter: game 1 has no randomness at all, so a line needing `OGN-293 The Grand Plaza` or `SFD-214 Power Nexus` has it **whenever it wants** — strictly BETTER than the caveat says — while games 2 and 3 cannot repeat it, so the line executes in **at most one game of three** — strictly WORSE than "one in three at random", because random draws are independent and this is a REMOVAL. 486.5.a is the only relief (a drawn game returns them), 103.4.c is why the obvious dodge fails, and 486.6.a allows a removed battlefield back only in games 4-5 of a best-of-5, after each of yours has been presented once and never more than twice. **Reassuringly, no arithmetic moves**: 486.3 is Victory Score 8 and 486.4 is Battlefield Count 2, so a Match GAME is a Duel in every parameter except the rotation, and 486.7 is word-for-word 485.7, so the turn-clock model needs no adjustment. Bringing 3 copies of the same battlefield is NOT a
  way out: **Core Rules 103.4.c** ("Cannot include more than one of a Battlefield of the same name when there
  are more than one required for the deck") and **Tournament Rules 402.1** ("exactly 3 battlefields each with a
  unique name") both forbid it. Issue #35 (2026-09-05) was opened believing the Core Rules were silent on this;
  they are not — 103.2.b is the Main Deck cap, the battlefield cap is three paragraphs further down. Every entry
  that needs a specific battlefield states this in `prerequisites.notable`, per format.
- **`basis.combos` means "the verified entries this rule was extracted from", which is NOT the same as "entries whose `uses[]` contain the anchor" — and the file holds both kinds.** Measured 2026-09-07 over 386 references: 27 point at an entry that does not run the anchor card, because the entry demonstrates the same MECHANISM with a different payoff (`tryndamere-excess-damage-might-reduction` -> `sivir-ambitious-frigid-touch-excess`, `dauntless-vanguard-occupied-battlefield-assault` -> `stare-down-arachnoid-horror-lone-survivor`). Both readings are legitimate and neither is a defect; what is a defect is a batch claiming it verified the anchor is present when two of its 77 new references are of the other kind. State which kind you are adding, and do not "fix" the existing ones.
- A rule that matches most of the pool is a fact about the pool, not a pairing. "Retreat replays any play effect" measured 132 and was narrowed to play effects that leave another permanent behind (14).
- UI lives in the deck panel **below** "What to add", so #18 keeps the placement the user chose.

## Web gotchas — three from the 2026-09-07 deck-card work (#177, #178)
- **The shared text-field style is scoped to `input[type=url], textarea, select`, so a bare text input renders WHITE.** Extend it with a CLASS (`.field-input`), never with `input[type=text]`: an attribute selector out-specifies `.detail-name` and would silently change the deckbuilder's own name field.
- **`.empty` is taken.** `web/styles.css:178` makes it a page-wide `position: absolute; inset: 0` empty state, so a LOCAL "nothing here" modifier needs its own name (`noart`, after the existing `.pool-noart`). Using `.empty` threw the deck-card thumbnail to the top of the viewport and read as a layout bug rather than a class collision.
- **Riot's card images are FULL-CARD scans (744x1039, illustration down to ~64%, name plate below that), and the CDN honours Sanity's `rect=x,y,w,h` in SOURCE pixels**, with the source size in the filename. That is how to crop to the illustration without coupling the crop to any layout dimension. `object-fit: cover` alone cannot do it: it trims only the axis the box has proportionally less of, so any box narrower than 1.38:1 crops the SIDES and shows the whole card - which is why a 58px full-height strip displayed the card's own printed title, and why the fix is a CDN rect and not a guessed `object-position`.
- **Never put backticks or `[ ]` inside a `maestro send` argument.** The receiving side is an interactive zsh: it evaluates the backticks and globs the brackets, and pieces of the message vanish silently. Write the text to a file and send `"$(cat file)"`.

## Legal posture
- No ads, no donations, no meta stats (play/win rates), no combo *execution*, exact Riot disclaimer text from `docs/plan.md` §1.
- **Do not copy LOOPLINE.** It is the reference for *what a combo route map does*, never for how it looks or what it calls things. Copying its colour tokens, wordmark lockup, nav labels, headings or column labels is out of bounds — the 2026-09-02 build did exactly that and had to be reworked (issues #1–#3).
- Before shipping any UI string, check it is not lifted verbatim from the reference.

## UI — visual identity
- Palette (from playriftbound.com, not from LOOPLINE), named as `web/styles.css` names it, which is the only place these numbers live: bg `#0f1a1e`, text `#f9eedc`, muted `#8fa7b3`, faint `#8199a4`, accent `#EF7D00`. The panels are THREE tiers and the lines TWO — `--panel` `#16242b` (cards and rows on the page), `--panel-2` `#1e3043` (the step above it: a checked segment, a filled chip, a pip), `--panel-3` `#293a4c` (defined, drawn nowhere today), `--line` `#24404c` (the ordinary border), `--line-2` `#336073` (the one that has to be seen). Until 2026-09-06 this line named only two panels and one line and used the old names, so "the panel colour" read as `#1E3043` when the code means `#16242b` (#131). Contrast is measured from the tokens in `test/a11y.test.ts`, never from here.
- One type family: Inter. Hierarchy comes from size and weight, never from a display face. Cinzel (the Trajan analog) was tried on 2026-09-03 and rejected by the user as poster-like and illegible at diagram sizes — do not reintroduce a serif or display face.
- Corners are rounded: containers 10px, controls 6px, diagram nodes 9–10px. Sharp 2–3px corners were tried and rejected as "too square". The diamond motif lives on the brand mark only; status dots and swatches are circles.
- Battlefields are printed landscape (`orientation: "landscape"` in cards.json, 66 printings). Any card rendering must honour that or the art gets cropped and the text stands on end.
- Domain colours are reserved and must not be reused for anything else: Fury `#ce212d`, Calm `#15ac72`, Mind `#22799c`, Body `#e4720c`, Chaos `#6c4993`, Order `#d0ab01`.
- One accent. Outcome classes read through text and weight, not through a categorical rainbow.
- **The grey scale is measured, not chosen: `--muted` `#8fa7b3` and `--faint` `#8199a4` both clear WCAG AA (4.5:1) on `--bg`, `--panel` and `--panel-2`, and a new tint has to as well** (#81, 2026-09-06). `--faint` was `#6a8794` and measured 4.17:1 on `--panel` and 3.53:1 on `--panel-2` — the tray chip's class line, the "What to add" class tag, the drawer's card sub-line, the import note in My decks. The fix was the same hue and saturation with the lightness raised 49.8% → 57.5%, the first step that passes all three; nothing on `--panel-3` is `--faint`, which is why AA is not asked of it. `test/a11y.test.ts` computes the ratios from the tokens in `web/styles.css` rather than repeating numbers, so it fails the moment a token moves. Same file pins the one focus ring — `:focus-visible { outline: 2px solid var(--accent) }`, since Chrome's default is a light blue that appears nowhere else here — and allows `outline: none` only where another element draws the focus (the segmented control's label, the SVG node's frame).
- No emoji, no blue/purple gradients, no decorative shadows, no glassmorphism.
- Hand-rolled SVG graph — no graph library.
- The **What to add** panel (#18, 2026-09-04) lives in the deck panel under the status card, not as a third view segment and not as a separate page — the user chose that placement over both. Its logic is `src/plan.ts` (`planDeck`), kept out of `web/` so it has tests. Three rules the user set and that must not be quietly reversed: it ranks by **copies to add**, with the combo class only breaking a tie; it shows the cheapest route **whatever it costs** — a six-card route is still displayed, because the count itself is the honest signal and hiding it hides a real answer; and it never suggests a card outside the legend's two domains (Domain Identity 103.1.b), which `test/plan.test.ts` pins against the mono-Fury fixture. It also drops any line whose ingredients are banned in the format being matched, so the panel never recommends buying an illegal card even when the matcher correctly reports the hit.
- **The editor at `#/decks/<id>` is the deckbuilder (#101, 2026-09-06), not a textarea.** Two fixed columns (pool with search, domain chips, zone, type/set/cost/sort; deck in text rows per zone with −/+, Runes Auto, a cost curve and Construction as a one-line-per-rule checklist), `Pool | Deck` tabs under 900px. The arithmetic (caps, auto-runes, curve, identity filter) is `src/builder.ts` with tests; `web/builder.ts` is the DOM; paste/deck code/Piltover URL import lives in a dialog and still goes through `loadDeck`. A saved deck is still `text` + `name` + `format`, so nothing in Supabase changed. The spec the user approved is `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-06-deckbuilder-design.md`; Stats, sample hand and playtest are out of scope by decision. The sideboard an imported list carries is drawn read-only.
- The **Banned and restricted** panel (#23, 2026-09-04) is the first thing in the deck panel, above the status card, because a list brought to a tournament wants that before any combo. `deckRestrictions(deck, cards, format)` in `src/deck.ts` takes the format rather than storing a flag on the parse, so the panel re-reads on the format toggle. `banned` and `restricted` are NOT the same thing and must not render the same: restricted is a cap, not an illegal card, and `OGS-019` is restricted in 2v2 only.
- The panel answers only what is computable. The narrative half of a hunt entry ("Svellsongur on Hwei fires twice per move") came from an agent reading card text and is NOT derivable from `combos.json`; "Already in this list" therefore means catalogued lines the deck completes plus catalogued pieces it holds, and nothing is invented to fill that section.

## Web gotchas
- CSP forbids inline `style=`: colour SVG through attributes, and HTML through CSSOM (`el.style.x = …`).
- `wrangler.toml` needs `run_worker_first = true` or the security headers never apply to static files.
- `[hidden]` needs `display:none !important` because `.empty` sets `display:grid`.
- Playwright MCP can only write screenshots into `.playwright-mcp/` (gitignored).
- **Every card field that `src/` reads at match time or in `checkBuild` MUST be listed in `scripts/web-card-fields.mjs`**, or it reaches the browser as `undefined` while every test passes (the tests read `data/cards.json`, the bundle reads the trimmed payload). It happened twice: the Mech tags (#27) and `signature` (#103, caught by rc-builder on the live site: 103.2.d said "No Signature cards" with four in the deck). `test/web-payload.test.ts` pins the list now; add the field there too.
- **Every card field that `src/` reads at match time or in `checkBuild` MUST be listed in `scripts/web-card-fields.mjs`**, or it reaches the browser as `undefined` while every test passes (tests read `data/cards.json`, the bundle reads the trimmed payload). It happened twice: the Mech tags (#27) and `signature` (#103, caught by rc-builder on the live site, where 103.2.d said "No Signature cards" with four in the deck). `test/web-payload.test.ts` pins the list now; add any new field there too.

## Accounts (#31)
- **The site lives at `https://riftcombo.app`** (bought through Vercel on 2026-09-05, #42; renewal US$15/yr,
  Vercel nameservers). `www.riftcombo.app` and `riftcombo.vercel.app` answer 308 to the apex, and that
  redirect is a **project-domain setting** (`PATCH /v9/projects/<id>/domains/<host>` with
  `{"redirect":"riftcombo.app","redirectStatusCode":308}`), not a `vercel.json` rule: a host-matched
  `redirects` entry in `vercel.json` was tried first on 2026-09-05 and never matched on a Git deploy.
  The old host stays in the Supabase redirect allow-list; every link, `site_url` and doc names `.app`.
- The hosted Supabase project is `bpbwsimgiyxzaorunqeo` in `us-east-1`, the region iad deploys to.
  Its **anon key is public by design** and rides in `public/app.js`; what keeps one player's rows
  away from another is RLS, proved by `node scripts/check-rls.mjs` (14 checks, two real users,
  run it **against the hosted project**, not only a local stack). The `service_role` key and the
  database password live in `.env.local` and reach nothing else — `test/headers.test.ts` fails if
  either name appears anywhere under `web/`.
- **`supabase/config.toml` is the source of truth for Auth, and `supabase config push` applies it.**
  Site URL, the redirect allow-list and the Google provider are all in there, with the client id and
  secret read as `env(...)`. Changing them in the dashboard by hand puts the repo and the project out
  of step, and the next `config push` silently reverts it.
- **`vercel.json` is generated, never hand-edited.** `SUPABASE_URL=… node scripts/build-headers.mjs`
  writes it and the result is committed, because Vercel reads it before the build command runs. The
  same variable feeds the bundle through esbuild's `define`, so the origin is set in one place.
- Sign-in is a **full-page redirect**, and that is load-bearing: `Cross-Origin-Opener-Policy:
  same-origin` severs a popup from the window that opened it and the flow never reports back, with
  no error printed anywhere.
- A Google OAuth client **caps at two client secrets and offers no way to delete one** — only to
  disable it. Rotating past that cap means deleting the whole client and creating a new one, which
  costs re-entering the redirect URI and updating the client id. Google keeps a deleted client
  restorable for 30 days.
- **The whole app sits behind sign-in (#39, user decision 2026-09-05, reversing #31's "anonymous stays
  identical").** `<body data-auth>` carries the state — `pending` (HTML default, shows neither side so
  nothing flashes), `out` (the entrance only), `in` (the app), `open` (a build with no `SUPABASE_URL`,
  which has no gate to open and behaves as before). `gate()` in `web/account.ts` sets it from the
  same supabase-js listener that reports the exchanged `?code=` session, and `web/styles.css` does the
  showing. The entrance carries the ONE sign-in button; the header shows only name + Sign out.
  `test/headers.test.ts` pins the single button and the pending default.
- Adding a redirect URI can take Google "5 minutes to a few hours" to apply. Do not debug it blind:
  `curl -sL "$(curl -s -o /dev/null -w '%{redirect_url}' 'https://<ref>.supabase.co/auth/v1/authorize?provider=google&redirect_to=https%3A%2F%2Friftcombo.app')"`
  answers `redirect_uri_mismatch` until it is live and the Google sign-in page after.

## Verify
- Run `npm test` and `npm run typecheck` before claiming anything works. Rebuild data with `npm run build:data`.
- **Deploy quota (2026-09-06): sessions push to `work`, the orchestrator promotes to `master`.** Vercel's Hobby cap is "Deployments Created per Day: 100", scoped to the ACCOUNT (shared with the other projects on it) over a rolling 24 h window, and a build cancelled by the Ignored Build Step still counts as created; on 2026-09-06 this repo's 130 commits spent the whole quota and blocked another project's production deploys. So every session commits as usual and runs `git push origin HEAD:work` — `work` has deployments disabled in `vercel.json` (`git.deploymentEnabled`), so it is a backup, never a build — and only the orchestrator runs `git push origin HEAD:master`, in batches, at most a handful of times a day. Never push `master` from a child session. The `*/*` and `*/**` globs in that block switch off previews only for branches with a slash; an unslashed branch other than `work` would still deploy, and `"*": false` must never be used because it also matches `master` and kills production (recoverable only from the dashboard).
- **Deploy is a push to master.** The Vercel project has been connected to `GermanAbuArab/RiftCombo`
  since 2026-09-05 (`vercel git connect`), so every push to master builds `npm run build:web` on
  Vercel from the COMMITTED tree and goes live; check it with `npx vercel ls riftcombo`. Before that,
  `npx vercel deploy --prod --yes </dev/null` uploaded the working tree, which twice shipped another
  session's uncommitted files. Keep that command only as a fallback when the Git build is broken.
- `npm run dev` serves the app on http://127.0.0.1:8787. Kill the wrangler process when the session ends.
- **Do not loop-poll the live bundle.** On 2026-09-06 repeated `curl https://riftcombo.app/app.js` (1.7 MB, from this IP, by several sessions at once) tripped Vercel's automatic mitigation: every request from this machine — curl, browser UA, headless Chromium — got `403` + `x-vercel-mitigated: challenge` ("Vercel Security Checkpoint") while `r.jina.ai/https://riftcombo.app/` from another network answered 200, and the project has no firewall config at all. So a 403 challenge from here is NOT an outage: confirm from another vantage before touching anything, check a deploy ONCE with `npx vercel ls riftcombo`, and read the live bundle at most once per deploy.
